**Just closed:** M6 Phase 4 — `Tree::absolute_position` transform-awareness (§8), **closing M6 entirely (all 4 phases)**. The post-M5 code review finding: `absolute_position` never composed `PaintProperties.transform`, so `open_overlay`'s anchor placement, `splitter_geometry`'s drag math, and every `engine-py` synthetic-point entry point silently computed the wrong canvas position for a node inside a panned/zoomed `Container`. Every real caller enumerated via grep first (two in `engine-core`, six in `engine-py`) — a small, fully enumerated set where *every* caller wants the transform-aware answer, unlike `add_child`'s ~80 or `hit_test`'s hot per-frame path, so this rewrites `absolute_position` in place rather than adding a parallel checked method the way M6 Phase 1/M5 Phase 2 did. Composes the identical `parent * translate(layout.location) * own_transform` product `paint_node`/`hit_test_at` already compose, walking the chain root-to-node (the order an `Affine` composes correctly in) rather than the old bottom-up accumulation. New `engine-core` test proves a node under a real ancestor transform reports its real position; the full pre-existing suite (every overlay/splitter-drag/docking pixel test) passed unmodified, confirming the no-op-for-identity-transform claim. New pytest test proves `Window.click(node)` still finds a node after `Node.animate("transform", ...)` has moved it — would have failed before this fix. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 51, up from 50)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, up from 77, 1 skipped) and all ten examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M7 Phase 1 — real MD3 motion tokens (§7.5). `MotionCurve` had exactly one real variant, `Linear`, since M3 step 2. Verified the exact curve values against a real, authoritative source rather than recalling them — two secondary sources disagreed on "Emphasized" (one flattened it to `Standard`'s own value, a common CSS approximation), resolved by cross-checking Android's own `MotionTokens.kt` (generated directly from the official Material Design spec): `Emphasized` is a genuine two-segment compound curve joined at `(0.166666, 0.4)`, not a single 4-parameter curve. New `CubicSegment` type evaluates any cubic Bézier and solves "given x, find the eased y" via bisection on `X(t)` — the same problem every browser engine solves for CSS `cubic-bezier()`. Four new tests, including a round-trip consistency check (the same rigor `kurbo::Affine::inverse()`'s own tests use) and a real, externally-verified landmark check on `Emphasized`'s own split point — all passed on the first run. No consumers wired to a non-`Linear` curve yet (this phase is the data table itself); Phase 3 (ripple/hover/focus-ring) and Phase 5 (container transform) are what use one for real. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 55, up from 51)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, 1 skipped) unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M7 Phase 2 — elevation → real shadow rendering (§7.2). Confirmed `engine-render` cannot depend on `engine-md3` (§4's own dependency diagram has no such edge) — the shadow-geometry math lives directly in `engine-render`, matching ripple/hover's own established precedent (M4 Phase 5). Rejected a first web source's elevation values as "alpha-stage, may change" and fetched the real, authoritative source instead: Material Web's own production `_elevation.scss`, which encodes the exact per-level shadow values as a continuous piecewise-linear formula — hand-verified against all 6 documented integer levels, both shadow layers, before trusting it. The CSS blur-radius → Gaussian std-dev conversion (`std_dev = blur/2`) verified against the real W3C spec text. MD3's `shadow` color role confirmed constant black regardless of theme, so it didn't need to wait for Phase 3's dynamic-color wiring. New `elevation_shadow.rs`: elevation 0 paints nothing (full pre-existing suite passed unmodified, confirming the no-op claim); a real elevation paints real shadow pixels below the node, not just its own fill — both passed on the first run. New `examples/elevation.py`. Full `cargo test --workspace`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, 1 skipped) and all eleven examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M7 Phase 3 — dynamic color, wired for real (§7.1, completing §7.3). `InteractionState` gains a real `tint: Color` field (default real black, byte-for-byte the old hardcoded value); `engine-render`'s ripple/hover paint arm reads it instead of `Color::from_rgba8(0, 0, 0, 255)`. New `engine-py::window::ThemeState`/`SharedTheme`, shared with every `Node` a `Window` hands out the same way `handlers`/`context_menus` already are (`View`'s own 3 construction sites get a fresh, private, always-black instance instead — out of this phase's stated "Window/App-level" scope, so `View`'s behavior is unchanged). `Window.set_theme(seed, dark=False)` builds a real `DynamicTheme::from_seed` and immediately pushes the resolved "on-surface" color into every already-opted-in node via new `Tree::set_all_interaction_tints`; `Node.enable_interaction()` applies the same color immediately for a node opting in afterward. Real live OS theme switching wired end to end: new `InputEvent::ThemeChanged { dark }` (a true no-op in `Tree::dispatch`, mirroring `Scroll`'s own precedent), a new `engine-platform::translate_theme` mapping the real, verified `winit::window::Theme::{Light,Dark}` (confirmed unsupported on this dev machine's own X11/Wayland session per `winit`'s own docs — a real platform limitation, not a bug), and a new `ThemeChanged` arm in `engine-py::App::run`'s `on_input` closure that re-resolves and re-pushes the tint. New pixel test proves a real, non-black tint paints a real hue (not just a darker gray); new `examples/theme.py`. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 58 up from 55, `engine-render` +1, `engine-platform` +1)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, 1 skipped, unaffected) and all twelve examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M7 Phase 4 — shape morphing, wired for real (§7.4). Real finding: `engine_md3::ShapeKey` (M3 step 10) had no actual MD3-specific content at all — only `Interpolate`/`peniko::kurbo`, no `ColorScheme`/`DynamicTheme`. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §2's own struct sketch and `engine-core::node.rs`'s own doc comment both already named `PaintProperties.shape: Animated<ShapeKey>` as the intended landing spot — impossible while `ShapeKey` lived in `engine-md3` (§4's dependency rule). Resolved the identical way M7 Phase 1 resolved `MotionCurve`: moved `shape_morph.rs` into `engine-core` verbatim (confirmed via grep — zero outside references, no re-export needed), correcting `engine-md3`'s own now-stale `Cargo.toml` description in the process. New `ShapeKey::empty()`/`is_empty()` is the "no morph set" sentinel; `paint_node`'s `Rect`/`Splitter` fill now draws the morphed silhouette instead of the plain rounded rect when active, a true no-op otherwise. New `engine-py::Node.animate("shape", [(x,y), ...])` arm builds a closed `BezPath` and converts via `ShapeKey::from_path`, mirroring `"transform"`'s own real, narrower-shape pattern. New pixel test proves the default is unmodified and a real triangle morph paints the real silhouette (a bounding-box corner outside the triangle stays background); new `examples/shape_morph.py`. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 65 up from 58, `engine-md3` down to 4 having lost the relocated suite, `engine-render` +2)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, 1 skipped, unaffected) and all thirteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M7 Phase 5 — container transform (§7.6), **closing M7 entirely (all 5 phases)**. Real finding: the "one genuinely new `engine-core` primitive" §7.6's own text names (`Node::computed_layout() -> taffy::Layout`) already existed — `Tree::layout(id)`/`Tree::absolute_position(id)` (M6 Phase 4) already expose exactly the real, computed per-node position/size the Capture step needs, confirmed via direct read before writing any code. New `engine-md3::container_transform` module implements the real 4-step "begin" choreography: captures the trigger's real bounds/appearance, captures the destination's own real targets, initializes the destination to the trigger's captured from-state via a computed non-uniform scale+translate `Affine` (verified safe against `Interpolate for Affine`'s own "any zero-shear diagonal affine stays diagonal at every `t`" contract), drives one synchronized `animate_to` set (transform/corner_radius/background/elevation), and cross-fades each container's own direct children with an immediate-vs-staggered start (`Animated::animate_to`'s own future-`start: Instant` support, already real, confirmed by reading `animation.rs` directly). A separate, explicit `teardown` detaches the trigger via the already-real `Tree::detach` — `CompletionHandle`'s own queue-drain mechanism §7.6's text names is confirmed still unwired anywhere in this codebase, a real, stated gap, not silently worked around. New `engine-py::Window.begin_container_transform`/`end_container_transform`. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-md3` 7, up from 4)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; full pytest suite (78 passed, 1 skipped) and all fourteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M7 — MD3 Visual Fidelity is now fully complete.** All 5 phases (real MD3 motion tokens, elevation → real shadow rendering, dynamic color wired for real, shape morphing wired for real, container transform) closed the milestone's own original investigation: every §7 subsystem that was "built but disconnected" is now genuinely reachable from a real Python app, through the real render pipeline.

**Just closed:** M8 Phase 1 — engine-level paint culling (§11.8). `build_tree_scene` computes a real, canvas-space "currently visible" `Rect` once (the whole viewport) and threads it into `paint_node`; `paint_node` computes each node's own real composed absolute bounding box (all four local corners transformed, not just two, so this stays correct under any future rotation-capable `Affine`) right after `composed`/`w`/`h` are known, and skips all Vello scene-encoding — shadow, fill, interaction, and every recursive call into children — the instant that box doesn't `overlap` (`peniko::kurbo::Rect::overlaps`, verified directly in kurbo's own source) the visible rect. `paint_node` is private with exactly two call sites (itself, `build_tree_scene`), so this needed no external caller updates. New `paint_culling.rs` proves the real, distinguishing behavior this adds (not just "off-screen content doesn't rasterize," which would be true anyway): a parent positioned 10,000px off-screen, whose own child has a `transform` that would otherwise bring it back onto visible canvas, never paints that child at all — its whole subtree is skipped before the child's own transform is ever composed. A second test proves a node only partially overlapping the viewport still paints its real, visible portion — a bounding-box-overlap decision, not an all-or-nothing "must be fully inside" test. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-render` +2)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every prior test passed unmodified, confirming the no-op claim for on-screen content. This phase touches no Python-facing API; `pytest` (78 passed, 1 skipped) and all fourteen examples confirmed clean, a pure regression check. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M8 Phase 2 — `VirtualList` real scroll offset & clipping (§11.7). Real finding: `Tree::tick_all` only ever ticks `node.paint`/`node.interaction` — kind-specific `Animated<T>` fields (`SplitterState.position` is the existing precedent) are driven by their own dedicated mechanism, not the central tick, so new `VirtualListState.scroll_offset: Animated<f64>` follows the identical shape: a plain value set directly, not eased by default. Real finding: `vello_hybrid::Scene::push_layer`'s own `clip_path` is baked into absolute/device-space strips at the moment it's called (confirmed via direct read of its own source), so a clip pushed under the `VirtualList`'s own `composed` transform stays correctly anchored even though each child painted inside the layer goes on to set its own transform — the same real mechanism ripple's own clip (M4 Phase 5) already proves works. `paint_node`'s final child-recursion is now `NodeKind`-aware: only `VirtualList` pushes a real clip layer (a local rounded rect matching its own corner radius), composes the scroll offset into its own children's transform, and narrows `visible` to its own bounds (reusing Phase 1's own `bounds` value) before recursing — every other `NodeKind` keeps its exact prior recursion, since nothing else in this engine clips today. New `virtual_list_scroll.rs`: a real scroll offset genuinely shifts materialized children's own painted position (proven at the exact pixel-level overlap remaining after a partial scroll); scrolled-out content is genuinely clipped, not just repositioned. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-render` +2)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — the full pre-existing `virtual_list.rs` suite passed unmodified, confirming the default `scroll_offset: 0.0` is a true no-op. This phase adds no new Python-facing API; pytest (78 passed, 1 skipped) and all fourteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M8 Phase 3 — real scroll input wired to `VirtualList` (§11.7), **closing M8 entirely (all 3 phases)** and M4 Phase 8's own long-stated gap. `Tree::dispatch`'s `InputEvent::Scroll` arm now hit-tests at the event's own position (the same mechanism `PointerPressed`/`PointerReleased` already use), walks up the hit node's own parent chain to the nearest `NodeKind::VirtualList` (a scroll gesture can land on any of its materialized children, not just its own root pixel), and calls new `Tree::scroll_virtual_list_by`, which clamps the real offset against the list's own real content extent and viewport height. Checked `winit`'s own docs directly and found no explicit scroll-sign convention documented — rather than present a guess as verified, this phase states its own chosen, tested convention plainly (positive `y` increases the offset) and a plain `20px`-per-line conversion for `Lines` deltas. Real, in-scope finding along the way: `Window.add_virtual_list`'s own `height: auto()` never gave a real viewport height at all (every materialized item is `Position::Absolute`, contributing nothing to intrinsic size) — fixed with an optional `height` parameter defaulting to the `Window`'s own real height, the same shape `width` already uses, fully backward compatible (confirmed via grep: no existing caller passes either). New `engine-py::Window.scroll(node, delta_y)`, the same no-live-window-needed pattern `click()`/`hover()` already established. 4 new `engine-core` tests (offset clamping at both ends, an unscrollable short list, real dispatch-driven scrolling over a child, a true no-op for no `VirtualList` ancestor), all passed on the first run; new pytest test proves the real FFI call chain runs without error; new `examples/scrollable_list.py`. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 69, up from 65)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every prior test passed unmodified. `pytest` (79 passed, up from 78, 1 skipped) and all fifteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M8 — Virtualization & Culling is now fully complete.** All 3 phases (engine-level paint culling, `VirtualList` real scroll offset & clipping, real scroll input wired to `VirtualList`) closed the milestone's own original investigation: zero culling anywhere and a purely caller-driven `VirtualList` with no real scroll/clip/input are both now real, composing together in one genuine scrollable list.

**Just closed:** M9 Phase 1 — real completion-handle plumbing through the central tick. `Animated<T>::tick` gains a `completed: &mut Vec<CompletionHandle>` parameter (a shared accumulator, not a per-call return, so a caller ticking many fields in one pass collects every real completion without allocating per field), pushing `anim.on_complete` into it on the exact tick an animation genuinely finishes — read out of the borrowed `ActiveAnimation` before `self.active` is cleared, the same order the existing `anim.to.clone()` already used. New `animate_to_with_completion` sibling method actually attaches a real handle; `animate_to` itself stays byte-for-byte unchanged, avoiding any of its own ~15+ existing call sites. `PaintProperties`/`InteractionState`/`RippleState`'s own `tick` methods thread `completed` through uniformly; `Tree::tick_all` now returns `(bool, Vec<CompletionHandle>)` instead of a bare `bool`; `set_splitter_position`'s own inline tick (kind-specific, outside the central walk since M8 Phase 2) discards its own completions, a real, stated scope boundary. 3 new `engine-core` tests, all passed on the first run; 4 existing test call sites (2 in `tree.rs`, 2 in `engine-render`'s own `animated_rect.rs`/`transform_composition.rs`) updated for the new signatures. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 72, up from 69)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every prior test passed unmodified. This phase adds no Python-facing API; pytest (79 passed, 1 skipped) and all fifteen examples confirmed clean, a pure regression check. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M9 Phase 2 — Python-facing `on_complete` callbacks. New `dispatch::CompletionRegistry`/`SharedCompletions` mirror `HandlerMap`'s own real shape exactly, with one real difference: `run_completions` uses `HashMap::remove`, not `get` — a real CSS `transitionend`/JS Promise-style single fire, not a repeating subscription. `PyWindow`/`Node` gain `completions: SharedCompletions` (a `View`-created node gets a fresh, private instance instead, since `View` has no real per-frame render loop to ever drain a completion through — the same stated scope limit `theme` already accepted for `View`, M7 Phase 3); `completions` holds real `Py<PyAny>` callbacks, so `PyWindow`'s own `__traverse__`/`__clear__` now cover it too. `Node.animate` gains `on_complete: Option<Py<PyAny>>`; a new private, generic `animate_field` helper replaces each of its six match arms' own direct `.animate_to` call, registering a handle only inside whichever one arm actually matches (avoiding an orphaned, never-invoked callback on an unknown-property error). `App::run`'s own `on_frame` closure now binds `Tree::tick_all`'s real completion `Vec` and drains it via `run_completions`. New pytest tests (an FFI smoke test, and a real cyclic-GC test proving a `Window`-capturing `on_complete` callback is genuinely collected, mirroring `test_virtual_list.py`'s own analogous `materializers` test); new `examples/animation_completion.py` — found and fixed a real timing issue while writing it (a 100ms real-duration animation never completed within a reasonable `max_frames` budget in this headless environment's own unpredictable frame rate; switched to `duration_ms=0`, deterministic regardless of real wall-clock pacing). No `engine-core`/`engine-render` change this phase — full `cargo test --workspace`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean, a pure regression check. `pytest` (82 passed, up from 79, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M9 Phase 3 — real automatic teardown for container transform (§7.6), **closing M9 entirely (all 3 phases)**. `engine_md3::container_transform::begin` gains `on_complete: Option<CompletionHandle>` (pure `engine-core`, no `pyo3` awareness — §4), wired onto the destination's own driven `transform` animation only: all four driven properties share one `start`/`duration`, so any one completing means the whole transition is genuinely done. `Window.begin_container_transform` gains `on_complete: Option<Py<PyAny>>`, registered into `self.completions` the same real way `Node.animate` already does, and passed down as the resulting handle. Two stale doc comments corrected along the way: `container_transform.rs`'s own module doc comment, which explicitly named this exact gap as "confirmed-still-unwired... separate, larger, unscoped work," now states it's real; `CompletionHandle`'s own doc comment (`animation.rs`), which said "no allocator/registry exists yet," now names the real one (M9 Phase 2's `CompletionRegistry`). New `engine-md3` tests prove a real handle genuinely reaches `Tree::tick_all`'s own drain once the transition finishes, not before, and that `None` never reports a completion; new pytest FFI smoke test. Updated `examples/container_transform.py`: `on_complete` now calls `end_container_transform` automatically, replacing the manual call at the script's end — the real, live, closing proof this milestone's own investigation set out to enable. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-md3` 9, up from 7)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every prior test passed unmodified. `pytest` (83 passed, up from 82, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M9 — Animation Completion Callbacks is now fully complete.** All 3 phases (real completion-handle plumbing through the central tick, Python-facing `on_complete` callbacks, real automatic teardown for container transform) closed the milestone's own original investigation: `CompletionHandle`/`ActiveAnimation.on_complete` were real, exported, genuinely unused types since M3 Phase 5 step 9 — now wired end to end, from `Animated::tick`'s own real per-tick detection through `Tree::tick_all`'s real drain, `engine-py`'s own Python-facing registry, and into container transform's own real automatic teardown.

**Just closed:** M10 Phase 1 — real overlay dismissal (§11.3). New `Tree::dismiss_overlays_outside`/`dismiss_escapable_overlays` (private) wired into `Tree::dispatch`'s real `PointerPressed`/`Key::Escape` arms — the single most-repeatedly-carried-forward candidate across M6 through M9's own scoping, finally closed. **Real finding during implementation, not anticipated during scoping:** `Tree::close_overlay` originally used full `Tree::remove` (irreversible destruction) — never actually exercised by a live app before this phase (confirmed via grep, zero prior callers in `engine-py`). The moment dismissal made it reachable for real, the most realistic use case broke: right-click a menu open, dismiss it, right-click the same anchor again — `Node.set_context_menu`'s own registered, reusable content `NodeId` was destroyed on first dismissal, panicking the next reopen. Caught by this project's own real pytest-level verification (an *existing* test started panicking), fixed by changing `close_overlay` to detach, not destroy — the identical contract `set_context_menu` already committed to. A second real finding: the first draft of outside-click dismissal treated a press back on the overlay's own anchor as "outside," breaking the same reopen scenario differently — fixed by excluding the anchor's own bounds too. Two stale doc comments corrected (`overlay.rs`'s own module doc comment; `overlay_meta`'s own doc comment). 8 new `engine-core` tests + 1 existing test updated for the real contract change; 2 new pytest tests proving dismissal reaches through the real FFI functionally. Full `cargo test --workspace`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every other prior test passed unmodified. `pytest` (85 passed, up from 83, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M10 Phase 2 — same-tree safety checks for `set_context_menu`/`set_dock_handle` (§8). `Node.set_context_menu` and `PyWindow.set_dock_handle` both gain the same `Rc::ptr_eq` guard `Node.add_child`/`Window.begin_container_transform` already use, widening their return types from `()` to `PyResult<()>` (additive — confirmed via grep no Rust-level caller exists anywhere for either, only Python). `dock::set_dock_handle` itself is a free function taking raw `NodeId`s with no way to check same-tree on its own; the check lives at its one real caller, `PyWindow.set_dock_handle`, instead. New pytest tests prove both reject a foreign-`Window` `Node` with the same real error message `add_child`'s own analogous test already expects; existing tests kept passing unmodified. Full `cargo test --workspace`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (this phase touches no `engine-core`). `pytest` (87 passed, up from 85, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M10 Phase 3 — drop-zone highlight overlay for docking (§11.4), **closing M10 entirely (all 3 phases)**. New `Tree::position_overlay_over` (`engine-core`) is `open_overlay`'s own missing "cover an arbitrary rect" counterpart — `dock.rs`'s own module doc comment named this exact gap since M4 Phase 9 ("`open_overlay`'s own `inset` computation is hardcoded to place content *below* its anchor... it can't cover a target zone's own bounds"). Sets `Position::Absolute` with `inset` from the rect's own origin and an explicit `style.size` matching its width/height, independent of any anchor; deliberately doesn't attach the node or touch `self.overlays` (no anchor, no outside-click/Escape dismissal fits a drag-driven highlight) — that lifecycle is the caller's own. New `engine-py` `dock::drag_over` is the real `PointerMoved`-during-drag step this module previously had no need for (before this phase, only press/release mattered) — hit-tests the pointer, reuses `enclosing_zone` unchanged, shows the registered highlight over whatever zone it finds or hides it if none. `end_drag_at` gains one new unconditional step, before its own existing early returns, that always hides the highlight when a drag ends, for any reason. New `PyWindow.set_drop_zone_highlight` (the same `Rc::ptr_eq` same-tree guard M10 Phase 2 added to `set_dock_handle`, applied here too — first real reuse of that guard by a brand-new registration method) and `drag_panel_over`, the same no-live-window-needed synthetic-dispatch pattern `start_panel_drag`/`drop_panel_at` already establish. **Real finding during implementation, not anticipated in `PLAN.md`:** the first draft of the new pytest coverage reused `(200.0, 50.0)` from the existing, already-passing `test_dragging_a_registered_handle_moves_its_panel_for_real` as "a point inside the Right zone" — direct debugging revealed that point actually sits exactly on the Right zone's own right edge (taffy's default flex-shrink compresses it to 68px wide once three 100px-wide root children don't fit `Window`'s own 300px width), not inside it, meaning that *pre-existing* test's own "real, functional proof" doesn't actually discriminate a real move from a same-zone no-op (`panel` stays clickable either way) — a real, pre-existing gap in that test's own discriminating power, left as-is and out of this phase's own scope, not a regression this phase introduced. This phase's own new tests use `(160.0, 50.0)`, confirmed via the same debugging to land genuinely inside the zone. `examples/docking.py` registers a real highlight via `set_drop_zone_highlight`; its own doc comment, which previously named this exact gap, is corrected to state it's closed. New `engine-core` unit test proves `position_overlay_over` covers an arbitrary rect independent of any anchor. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 81, up from 80)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean — every prior test passed unmodified. `pytest` (93 passed, up from 87, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean, including `docking.py` with its new highlight. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M10 — Interaction & Overlay Completeness is now fully complete.** All 3 phases (real overlay dismissal via outside-click/Escape, same-tree safety checks for `set_context_menu`/`set_dock_handle`, and the drop-zone highlight overlay for docking) closed every real gap this milestone's own scoping named: dismissal wiring that had been carried forward, un-acted-on, across M6 through M9's own scoping notes; the one same-tree guard class `set_context_menu`/`set_dock_handle` hadn't yet received; and the one deliberately-stated, real placement-primitive gap `open_overlay` left unaddressed since M4 Phase 9.

**Just closed:** M11 Phase 1 — true bezier curve authoring (§11.10, §11.11). `polyline` (`crates/engine-py/src/canvas.rs`) renamed to `build_path` and widened from `points: Vec<(f64, f64)>` to `points: Vec<Vec<f64>>` — a 2-element inner sequence is a line-to (the only shape ever accepted before this phase, so every existing call site keeps working with zero Python-side changes), 4 is a quadratic (`quad_to`), 6 is a cubic (`curve_to`); any other length is a real `PyValueError`. `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` keep their exact signatures and parameter name, both still calling the one shared helper — no second "curve" mechanism added. Considered and rejected a custom `FromPyObject` tagged-point enum (pyo3 0.29's own more complex trait shape, real avoidable complexity with zero precedent anywhere in this codebase) in favor of plain arity-dispatch on `Vec<f64>`. New `engine-core` unit test proves the resulting hit-test math is genuinely curve-aware, not a chord approximation: a real `quad_to` curve's own true midpoint (50px from the naive straight chord between its endpoints) hits, while a point 2px from that chord but ~48px from the real curve misses — the exact adversarial pair a chord-only distance would get wrong. New pytest coverage proves the FFI authoring surface accepts real quadratic/cubic segments and rejects invalid point lengths. No `engine-core` production change, only the new test. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 82, up from 81)/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; `pytest` (96 passed, up from 93, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean, including the three pre-existing `stroke_path` call sites unmodified. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M11 Phase 2 — canvas redraw triggering: investigate and resolve (§11.10, §11.11), **closing M11 entirely (both phases)**. Investigated whether `Window.redraw_canvas`'s explicit, app-triggered-only design is a real gap or a deliberate, correct choice, rather than presupposing an answer. `crates/engine-py/src/app.rs`'s real winit frame loop, checked directly: `canvas_draws` never appears anywhere in it (confirmed via grep) — it isn't part of `WindowSetup`/`WindowRuntime`, the two structs carrying everything the per-frame closures can see, which only ever call `Tree::tick_all` and drain completions. `WindowSetup`'s own module doc comment already states why: every Python-callback field threaded into the frame loop (`handlers`, `completions`) is invoked only on a real, specific event (a dispatched activation, an animation actually completing) — never unconditional per-frame work; adding `canvas_draws` to that set would be the first case of blanket per-frame Python invocation anywhere in this codebase. `ARCHITECTURE.md` names no automatic-redraw requirement anywhere (confirmed via direct read of §11.10/§11.11 and a full grep). Real cost analysis: unconditional per-frame redraw would impose a real Python call, a fresh `CanvasContext`, and a full `Vec<DrawCommand>` rebuild every frame for every registered canvas, regardless of whether its content changed — exactly the class of per-frame overhead this codebase has consistently designed against elsewhere. **Resolution: no change needed** — the current app-triggered "invalidate, then explicitly redraw" model is the deliberate, correct choice for a retained-mode `Tree`, mirroring M7 Phase 5 Step 1's own "real finding: already existed, nothing new needed building" resolution shape. `BUILD_TRACKER.md`'s own M5 carried-forward "known gap" note (which stated the fact without resolving it) is corrected to record this resolution, closing the question for future scoping. No production code change; full suite/clippy/fmt/pytest (96 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged)/all sixteen examples re-confirmed clean as a formality. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M11 — Canvas Authoring Completeness is now fully complete.** Both phases (real bezier curve authoring for `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path`, and an honest investigation resolving the milestone's own remaining open question with no code change required) closed the single most-repeatedly-passed-over candidate across M8 and M10's own scoping — real curve authoring now exists where only straight polylines did, and the canvas redraw-triggering model is now a settled, documented design decision rather than an ambiguous carried-forward note.

**Just closed:** M12 Phase 1 — real variable-height extent support in `engine-core` (§11.7). `ItemExtent` gains `Variable` (a fieldless marker -- the real per-item data lives on `VirtualListState.resolved_offsets: BTreeMap<usize, f64>`, not the enum itself, since only `VirtualListState` has a `Tree`-mutation path to populate it). Chose storing already-*cumulative offsets* directly (index -> real top-offset, key `item_count` holding the real total extent) over storing raw per-item heights and summing on every query -- `engine-core` never computes a cumulative sum itself, only looks a resolved value up, matching this codebase's own "`engine-core` carries inert state, an `engine-py` method drives it" shape. New `VirtualListState::offset_of`/`total_extent` are the single shared source of truth both real `tree.rs` call sites (`set_virtual_list_window`'s own materialization positioning, the M8 Phase 3 scroll-clamp's own total-extent computation) now use in place of the old uniform `idx * item_extent`/`item_extent * item_count` formulas; `ItemExtent::value()` (no longer meaningful with a second variant) removed, its two callers rewritten. New `Tree::set_virtual_list_resolved_offsets` is the real way a caller supplies resolved offsets -- unused by `engine-core` itself, Phase 2's own entry point. `engine-py`'s `set_virtual_list_window` needed one small fix to keep compiling (an irrefutable `Fixed`-only pattern made exhaustive with an honest `unreachable!` arm for `Variable`, since `add_virtual_list` still only constructs `Fixed` until Phase 2). New `engine-core` tests: `Fixed` behavior unchanged (regression guard); a real, non-uniform resolved-offset set (heights 10/30/15/25, not an arithmetic sequence) proves real per-item positioning and real total-extent clamping for `Variable`; a `#[should_panic]` test proves an unresolved index panics clearly rather than returning a silent wrong answer. No `engine-py`/Python-facing behavior changes this phase. Full `cargo test --workspace` (`engine-core` 86, up from 82)/clippy `-D warnings` (one real `needless_range_loop` lint caught and fixed)/fmt clean; `pytest` (96 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean as a pure regression check. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M12 Phase 2 — Python-facing size-hint callback (§11.7), **closing M12 entirely (both phases)**. `Window.add_virtual_list` gains `size_hint: Optional[Callable[[int], float]]`; `item_extent` becomes optional — exactly one of the two must be given, a real `ValueError` otherwise. Real design decision: `size_hint` resolves *eagerly, once*, at `add_virtual_list` time (calling it exactly `item_count` times immediately, building the complete cumulative-offset table via Phase 1's `Tree::set_virtual_list_resolved_offsets`), not lazily per `set_virtual_list_window` call — a stateful, incrementally-extended lazy cache would be real, avoidable complexity ARCHITECTURE.md's own one-line "size-hint callback" text doesn't ask for; a real per-item height callback is cheap (plain arithmetic, no `Node` creation) but a real cumulative offset structurally requires knowing every preceding item's own height regardless, so this is a real, deliberate, stated `O(item_count)` cost, documented not hidden. `set_virtual_list_window` extracts a new, small, private `ResolvedItemHeights` snapshot (mirroring the pre-existing scalar-extraction shape) before building its materializer closure; each item's own real height for `Variable` is the difference between two adjacent resolved cumulative offsets. New pytest coverage proves `size_hint` is called exactly once per item, in order, at `add_virtual_list` time — and *not* re-invoked by `set_virtual_list_window` — plus the two-error-path/exception-propagation coverage `materialize` already established; the definitive non-uniform-positioning proof stays in Phase 1's own `engine-core` tests, matching this file's established "FFI wiring only" split (no direct position/size getter exists on `Node` from Python at all). New `examples/variable_height_list.py`: a real, live 500-row list with a genuinely non-uniform, repeating five-step row-height pattern, scrolled for real. Full `cargo test --workspace` (no `engine-core` change, `engine-py`-only)/clippy `-D warnings` (one real `too_many_arguments` lint on the now-8-parameter `add_virtual_list`, resolved with a scoped, justified `#[allow]`)/fmt clean; `pytest` (100 passed, up from 96, 1 skipped) and all seventeen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M12 — Variable-Height VirtualList Items is now fully complete.** Both phases (real variable-height extent support in `engine-core`, and the real Python-facing `size_hint` callback wiring it through to app code) closed the single most-repeatedly-passed-over candidate of all three real M12 candidates — deferred across M8's, M10's, and M11's own scoping before finally being picked, `ItemExtent` now has a real second variant, and a real, non-uniform-height list works end to end from Python, with a live example proving it.

**Just closed:** M13 Phase 1 — real `AppShell` composition (§11.2). New `Window.build_shell(menu_bar=None, toolbar=None, status_bar=None) -> Node`: checks each given region with the same `Rc::ptr_eq` same-tree guard `set_dock_handle`/`set_drop_zone_highlight` already established; creates one new `Flex Column` `Container` child of `self.root` (which stays a plain `Flex Row`, untouched); re-parents each given region into it in order; creates a new, empty `content` `Container` with `flex_grow: 1.0`; returns `content`. No new `engine-core` primitive needed, confirmed by investigation. **Real finding, caught live, not by pytest:** the first draft re-parented the given regions with the cheap `Tree::add_child` (correct only for a freshly-inserted node with no prior parent) — but every `add_*` method already attaches its result to `self.root` immediately, so `menu_bar`/`toolbar`/`status_bar` always already have a real parent, and the cheap path left a node listed as a child of *both* its old parent and the new shell. Not caught by any pytest test (none render a real frame) — caught by `examples/app_shell.py`'s own live run, which panicked with `accesskit`'s own `TreeUpdate includes duplicate child`, the exact same "caught by the live example, not pytest" pattern `dock_panel`'s own doc comment already names for an analogous M4 Phase 9 bug. Fixed with `Tree::try_add_child` (detaches first), the same mechanism `Node.add_child`'s own pyo3 wrapper already uses. New `tests/test_app_shell.py` (all regions attach for real; no regions still returns a usable `content`; `flex_grow` genuinely fills remaining space; foreign-`Window` region rejected) and new `examples/app_shell.py`. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (`engine-py`-only); pytest (104 passed, up from 100, 1 skipped) and all eighteen examples confirmed clean, including the real bug fix verified live. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M13 Phase 2 — real content navigation (§11.2, optionally §7.6), **closing M13 entirely (both phases)**. New `Node.remove()` — calls `Tree::remove(self.id)`, mirroring `add_child`'s own minimal shape, no return value (a `Node` handle Python already holds always refers to a real, present `NodeId`, the same assumption every other `Node` method makes). New `tests/test_remove.py`: a node genuinely detaches so a new sibling occupying the same real position is the one a real dispatched click reaches afterward (calling `Window.click` on the removed node's own stale handle would panic via `Tree::absolute_position`'s own `.expect()`, an internal-bug condition, not something to attempt from a test); removing a node with real children doesn't corrupt the rest of the tree. New `examples/navigation.py`: a real `AppShell` with two screens built into `content`, navigating between them (remove + add) proven by a real dispatched click landing on the second screen's own card. Deliberately does not attempt to combine this with `begin_container_transform` in the same script — `examples/container_transform.py` already proves that mechanism on its own; §11.2's own text states the two *compose*, not that this phase must re-prove container-transform itself. Full `cargo test --workspace`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (no `engine-core` change, `engine-py`-only — `Tree::remove` already existed and was already tested); `pytest` (106 passed, up from 104, 1 skipped) and all nineteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M13 — AppShell / Single-Page Navigation is now fully complete.** Both phases (real shell composition tying named chrome regions together, and the one missing `Node.remove()` primitive real navigation needs) closed the gap ARCHITECTURE.md §11.2 had fully specified but left entirely unimplemented — along the way, Phase 1 found and fixed a real tree-corruption bug (`add_child`'s own "no dedup" class, caught live by `accesskit`, not pytest) that would otherwise have shipped silently.

**Just closed:** M14 Phase 1 — real Checkbox (§5, §7.3). `NodeKind::Checkbox(CheckboxState { checked: bool, check_progress: Animated<f64> })` — the struct sketch only showed the animated half; §7.3's own text separately named `checked`. Design Principle 6 confirmed the engine must not auto-toggle `checked` on click (app-owned meaning) — the "click-to-toggle interaction" this phase's scoping named is the already-fully-generic `Click` dispatch + `Node.enable_interaction()` ripple mechanism, needing zero new dispatch code. `Tree::tick_all` gains one new arm ticking `check_progress` (the first kind-specific payload it ever tickets, confirmed via direct read that `SplitterState.position`/`VirtualListState.scroll_offset` are deliberately excluded, driven directly instead). `Tree::build_access_update` gains the real, automatic `checked` → `accesskit::Toggled` derivation ARCHITECTURE.md §7.3 promised — reading `checked` directly from `NodeKind::Checkbox`, not a second `AccessStates` copy that could drift. `paint_node` gains a `Checkbox` arm: the box paints like a `Rect`, then a real checkmark `BezPath` strokes on top with opacity driven directly by `check_progress` (a plain white mark, real but not yet theme-aware, the same precedent ripple's own hardcoded tint had before M7 Phase 3). `Node.animate`/`.get` gain their first real kind-payload arm (`"check_progress"`) — the "two-level dispatch" ARCHITECTURE.md §8 describes, not built until now; new `Node.set_checked(bool)` is the plain, non-animated write. New `Window.add_checkbox`. **Real finding, caught by pytest, not the implementation:** the first test draft called `.animate("check_progress", ...)` then immediately `.get(...)`, expecting the new value — but `Animated<T>` never updates `current` until a real tick runs (`App.run`'s own per-frame loop), the same real behavior every other `animate()`-dispatched field already has, not a Checkbox-specific bug. Fixed by testing what pytest can prove without a live render loop, leaving the "did a tick genuinely animate it" claim to the new `engine-render/tests/checkbox_paint.rs` pixel test (unchecked paints no mark; checked paints a real, fully-opaque white mark exactly on the real tick path). New `tests/test_checkbox.py` (7 tests) + new `examples/checkbox.py`. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (`engine-core` 88, up from 86, +2 `engine-render` pixel tests); pytest (113 passed, up from 106, 1 skipped) and all twenty examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M14 Phase 2 — real Slider (§5, §7.3). `NodeKind::Slider(SliderState { thumb_position: Animated<f64> })` — `thumb_position` *is* the real value, the identical shape `SplitterState.position` already has, no separate field. Confirmed via direct read that splitter dragging is entirely internal to `Tree::dispatch` (`PointerPressed` sets `self.dragging`, `PointerMoved` calls `update_drag`, `PointerReleased` clears it) — a better precedent than docking's own synthetic drag API, since a slider drag has no meaning-dependent decision engine-core can't make itself. New `Tree::set_slider_position` mirrors `set_splitter_position`'s own instant + immediate-tick shape, no sibling-resize step. `self.dragging` broadened (documentation, not type) to cover either kind; `update_drag` now matches on the dragged node's own real kind, splitting into `update_splitter_drag` (unchanged) and new `update_slider_drag` (the node's own real position/width *is* the whole track, no siblings). Horizontal-only for now. **Real finding while designing `tick_all`'s own scope:** unlike `SplitterState.position` (never exposed to `Node.animate()`), `thumb_position` *is* exposed (this phase's own second kind-payload `animate()` arm, for a real triggered move distinct from a drag) — that path needs central ticking or a nonzero-duration `animate()` call would never progress; caught while designing, not after. `tick_all` gains a `Slider` arm. `paint_node` gains a real track + thumb (a filled circle at `thumb_position * w`, the node's own real `background` color). `Node.animate`/`.get` gain `"thumb_position"`; new `Window.add_slider`. No `set_value`-style setter needed — the real drag path sets `thumb_position` directly inside `engine-core`, never through Python. New `engine-core` tests (mirroring the existing splitter-drag tests via a new `slider_scene()` helper): real dispatched drag moves the thumb live in two steps; clamps to `0.0..=1.0`; release ends the drag; `tick_all` genuinely animates toward a real target. Every existing splitter-drag test passed unmodified. New `engine-render/tests/slider_paint.rs`: thumb at `0.0` paints at the real left edge, `1.0` at the real right edge. New `tests/test_slider.py` (6 tests) + new `examples/slider.py`. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (`engine-core` 92, up from 88, +2 pixel tests); pytest (119 passed, up from 113, 1 skipped) and all twenty-one examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M14 Phase 3 — real `Change` EventKind + two-way binding sugar (§16.7), **closing M14 entirely (all 3 phases)**. `EventKind::Change`/`DispatchOutcome::Changed(NodeId)` are real now, fired two genuinely different ways per Design Principle 6 (engine-core never knows "meaning," only mechanics): mechanically, for a real Slider drag-release, detected inside `Tree::dispatch`'s own `PointerReleased` arm (checked *before* `self.dragging` is cleared, taking priority over `Activated`); directly, for a Checkbox, fired by new `Node.set_checked` via `call_handler` (widened from private to `pub(crate)` so `set_checked` could reuse the exact same dispatch mechanism, not a second parallel one) — `engine-core` never touches `checked` at all, so its own `Change` can only originate from wherever `checked` is actually written. New `Node.get_checked`/`Node.set_on_change` (the missing read-back getter and handler registration, mirroring `set_on_click`/`set_on_hover_*`). Two-way binding is a `View`/YAML-only concept, and `NodeKindSpec` only had `Rect`/`Container`/`Text` — a real, additional scope expansion reasoned through explicitly: `WidgetSpec` gains `checked: bool`/`value: f64`/`two_way: Option<String>` (all additive) and `NodeKindSpec` gains `Checkbox`/`Slider`, making them real, declarable `view.yaml` kinds for the first time, not just imperative-API-only. `View._attach` wires `on_change` handler names; `apply_binding_value` gains a `Value::Bool` branch calling `set_checked` (its own stale "only numeric... supported today" doc comment corrected); a new `TwoWayCallback` pyclass registers as the widget's own `Change` handler, reading the node's current value back and writing it into the bound `Signal`. **Real finding #1, caught only once the round trip was actually tested, not merely unit-tested in isolation:** a first draft required the two-way expression to be a *bare* `Expression::Ident` (matching ARCHITECTURE.md §16.7's own inline illustration, `{{ username }}` with no `.get()`) — but every binding's forward direction resolves a bare identifier to the raw Python object itself (an opaque `Value::Handle` for a `Signal`, not its value), so `apply_binding_value` rejected it before the two-way registration code ever ran; fixed by requiring `Expression::Call(Expression::Ident(name), "get")` instead, matching the `.get()` convention every other real binding in this codebase already uses. **Real finding #2, caught only by actually running the new example end to end, not by any unit test:** with finding #1 fixed, `set_checked(True)` fired `Change` → `TwoWayCallback` wrote the `Signal` → its own unconditional notify re-triggered the widget's own forward binding → which called `set_checked(True)` again → infinite recursion, each level individually caught and printed by `call_handler`'s own pre-existing non-fatal-handler policy (§9), which is exactly why `pytest` alone never caught it. The correct, general fix: `Signal.set`/`Signal.update` now skip notification entirely when the new value equals the current one — standard change-detection, verified in isolation (two new pure-Python `Signal` tests, no `View`/`Tree` involved) and against every existing `.set()`/`.update()` caller in the repo, none of which relied on always-notify semantics. New `engine-core` tests prove the mechanical Slider path; new `engine-spec` tests prove `Checkbox`/`Slider` parse and build correctly from `view.yaml`; new `tests/test_change_event.py` (5 tests) and `tests/test_two_way_binding.py` (5 tests, including a dedicated stderr-must-stay-clean regression test for the recursion fix) cover the FFI/round-trip surface; new `examples/two_way_binding.py` + `two_way_binding.yaml` demonstrate a real `Checkbox`/`Slider` view, both two-way bound, with no live window needed. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (`engine-core` 94, up from 92; `engine-spec` 33, up from 29) — every prior test passed unmodified; pytest (131 passed, up from 119, 1 skipped) and all twenty-two examples confirmed clean, including a second full run after the `Signal` fix. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M14 — Form Components & Two-Way Data Binding is now fully complete.** All 3 phases (real Checkbox, real Slider, real `Change` EventKind + two-way binding sugar) closed ARCHITECTURE.md §5/§7.3/§16.7's own long-deferred component gap — `Checkbox`/`Slider` are real `NodeKind`s with real paint and interaction, and a bound `Signal` now genuinely round-trips both directions through a declarative `view.yaml`, with two real, non-obvious bugs (a two-way expression shape that could never actually apply forward; an unconditional-notify feedback loop) caught only by end-to-end testing, not unit tests alone, and fixed at their real, general root cause rather than special-cased around.

**Just closed:** M15 Phase 1 — real `NodeKind::TextField` state + paint (§5, §16.7), the first of three milestones scoped together at the user's own explicit request ("scope the next milestone"). Presented as three real, investigated candidates: M15 = TextField/real keyboard text entry, the one component §16.7 names alongside `Slider`/`Checkbox` but M14 explicitly deferred (recommended and chosen); structured logging via `tracing` (§3, §9), not chosen for M13/M14's two slots either, chosen here as M16; a third candidate, a `VirtualList` 100k-row performance spike, **investigated and found already done** (`tests/test_virtual_list_benchmark.py` already passes at 0.4µs/call against a 200µs ceiling) — not silently scoped as new work, replaced with TextField's own deferred clipboard/IME slice promoted to M17. **Real finding:** `NodeKind::Text` has zero imperative Python constructor anywhere (YAML-only, confirmed via grep) — unlike `Checkbox`/`Slider`, so `Window.add_text_field` moved into this phase rather than Phase 2, matching the interactive-component precedent instead. **Real finding:** `Tree::set_access` had zero real callers from `engine-py` (confirmed via grep, every `add_*` method left every node at `Role::Unknown`) — `add_text_field` is the first real caller, setting `Role::TextInput` + `Action::Focus` at construction. **Real finding:** `InteractionState.focus_ring` is ticked but never painted anywhere in `engine-render` — rather than invent a second focus concept, the new real caret is gated on the live `tree.focused() == Some(id)`, reusing §10's already-real state directly (`paint_node` already receives `tree: &Tree`). **Real, scope-simplifying finding:** the pinned `parley = "0.11.1"` already ships `Cursor`/`Selection` with real `from_byte_index`/`.geometry()` — new `TextRenderer::draw_field` builds one shared `Layout` and derives a real selection-highlight rect, glyphs, and a real caret from it, in that real MD3 stacking order (behind text, then text, then caret on top). New `Node.is_focused()` (§10) — no Python-facing way to read live focus existed at all before this. New `engine-core` tests (`TextFieldState::new` seeds `cursor` at `content.len()`; `build_access_update` reports real `Role`/`value`/`Action::Focus`); new `engine-render/tests/text_field_paint.rs` (an unfocused field shows only its own fill; the same field, once genuinely focused, paints a real visible caret) — both passed on the first run. New `tests/test_text_field.py` (7 tests, including a real `Window.press_key("tab")` reaching the field and a second Tab genuinely moving focus away) + new `examples/text_field.py`. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (`engine-core` 96, up from 94, +2 `engine-render` pixel tests); pytest (138 passed, up from 131, 1 skipped) and all twenty-three examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M15 Phase 2 — real keyboard-driven editing (§8, §10). `Key` gains `Backspace`/`Delete`/`ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight`/`Home`/`End` (verified against `winit`'s own real `NamedKey` variants before adding); `InputEvent` gains `TextInput(String)`, mirroring `winit::event::KeyEvent.text: Option<SmolStr>`'s own real per-keypress produced text, previously discarded entirely. **Real design conflict, found while designing, not after:** `NamedKey::Space` is matched before any `TextInput` fallback could ever see it, but `Key::Space` already means "activate a focused node" (`Enter | Space => Activated`) — a focused `TextField` needs `Space` to insert a literal space instead. Resolved by having `Tree::dispatch`'s new `KeyPressed` handling check the focused node's own kind first, via a new private `dispatch_text_field_key` that claims Enter/Space/Backspace/Delete/arrows/Home/End for a focused `TextField` and lets Tab/Escape fall through unchanged (a focused field must still lose focus on Tab, still dismiss overlays on Escape). `EventKind::Change` fires only on a real content edit, never on pure cursor movement or a genuine no-op (`Backspace` at the real start, `Delete` at the real end) — `Change` means "the bound value changed," not "a key was pressed." Every mutation stays on real UTF-8 `char_indices` boundaries. `InputEvent` dropping `Copy` (kept `Clone`, needed for the new owned `String` payload) broke exactly one real call site (`app.rs`'s own `on_input` closure, which uses `event` twice) — found by attempting the build, not by guessing every call site in advance. New `Node.set_text` mirrors `set_checked`'s own shape exactly, including always firing `Change` — the same established convention, already protected against a two-way-binding feedback loop by M14 Phase 3's own `Signal`-level fix, no new fix needed. `Window.press_key` widened to the six new named keys; new `Window.type_text(text)` mirrors its own synthetic-dispatch pattern for `TextInput`. New `engine-core` tests (12, all passed first run): real insertion at an arbitrary cursor; Backspace/Delete both as a real edit and a genuine no-op; pure cursor movement never reports `Changed`; Space inserts rather than activates; Enter is consumed without inserting or activating; Tab still moves focus away; a real multi-byte UTF-8 character removed whole, not corrupted. `tests/test_text_field.py` gained 9 more tests (16 total, all passing), including that pure navigation and no-op edits don't fire `on_change`. Updated `examples/text_field.py` with real typing/navigation/deletion. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (`engine-core` 108, up from 96); pytest (147 passed, up from 138, 1 skipped) and all twenty-three examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M15 Phase 3 — selection & two-way binding (§16.7), **closing M15 entirely (all 3 phases)**. `dispatch_text_field_key` widened to take `shift: bool`: an arrow/`Home`/`End` held with `shift` extends the selection from wherever it started (`selection_anchor` seeded once, left alone on further extends); the same key *without* `shift`, while a real selection is active, collapses to its own near edge — real desktop-editor behavior, decided before writing, not the simpler-but-wrong "move one more character" default. New shared `Tree::delete_selection` deletes a real, non-collapsed selection; a zero-width one (`anchor == cursor`) clears itself and reports a genuine no-op, not an empty delete. `Backspace`/`Delete`/`Space`/`TextInput` all replace an active selection first, the same real behavior every desktop text editor has. `NodeKindSpec::TextField` reuses `WidgetSpec.text: Option<TextSpec>` verbatim — the exact same block `kind: Text` already requires, since `TextFieldState`'s own font/content fields are byte-for-byte identical, rather than adding a third, near-duplicate field the way `checked`/`value` each needed their own. `apply_binding_value` gains a `Value::Str` branch calling `set_text`; `TwoWayCallback` gains a `"text"` branch calling `get_text` — both mirroring the existing `"checked"` shape exactly. New `engine-core` tests (9, all passed first run): selection extension, correct near/far-edge collapse in both directions, shift+Home/End, every real edit replacing a selection, a zero-width selection treated as none. New `engine-spec` tests (4, one fixed after a real counting mistake caught by actually running it — "jane" is 4 characters, not 5): parse/build/error-path coverage for `kind: TextField`. New `tests/test_two_way_binding.py`/`test_text_field.py` coverage: a real `two_way: text` round trip via `set_text`; shift+arrow selection through `Window.press_key(shift=True)` followed by a real Backspace deleting the whole range; typing over a selection replacing it. Extended the existing `examples/two_way_binding.py`/`.yaml` with a real `TextField` alongside Checkbox/Slider, rather than a parallel duplicate. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (`engine-core` 117, up from 108; `engine-spec` 37, up from 33); pytest (150 passed, up from 147, 1 skipped) and all twenty-three examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M15 — TextField / Real Keyboard Text Entry is now fully complete.** All 3 phases (state/paint, real keyboard-driven editing, selection + two-way binding) closed §16.7's own long-deferred component gap — a real single-line `TextField` now has real content/cursor/selection state, real keyboard-driven editing (typing, Backspace/Delete, arrow/Home/End navigation, shift-extended selection), and a bound `Signal` genuinely round-trips both directions through a declarative `view.yaml`, the same real capability M14 already gave `Checkbox`/`Slider`. Mouse click-to-position/drag-selection and clipboard/IME remain real, explicitly stated, un-scoped gaps — the latter is M17, already scoped and next.

**Just closed:** M16 Phase 1 — real `tracing` subscriber wiring (§3, §9). **Real finding:** `tracing = 0.1.44` is already a real transitive dependency of `engine-py` (confirmed via `cargo tree -p engine-py -i tracing`, pulled in by `winit`/`accesskit_unix`/`zbus`) — depending on it directly adds no new crate to the tree; `tracing-subscriber` is genuinely new, its own `env-filter` feature enabled explicitly (not in its default set) for a real `RUST_LOG`-driven verbosity dial. **Real design risk, found before writing:** a global subscriber can only ever be installed once per process, and `App.run()` can legitimately be called more than once in one process (confirmed by an existing test) — `tracing_subscriber::fmt::try_init()` (returns a `Result`), not `init` (panics on a second call), is the load-bearing choice, called at `App::run`'s own real top, before even the "no windows" early return. One real span, `tracing::info_span!("app_run", windows = ...)`, established via RAII guard — the first this codebase uses, the vocabulary Phase 2's own call-site migration builds on. New pytest test calls `App().run()` a second time in the same process, real proof (not just reasoning) that re-init doesn't panic — passed first run. Manually confirmed `RUST_LOG=info python3 examples/checkbox.py` genuinely emits real, span-wrapped events, including from a transitive dependency (`wgpu_hal`'s own log line, a real bonus, previously silently discarded) — the default (no `RUST_LOG`) run stays exactly as quiet as before, confirmed across every example. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; pytest (151 passed, up from 150, 1 skipped) and all twenty-three examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M16 Phase 2 — migrate real call sites to `tracing` (§9), **closing M16 entirely (both phases)**. New `dispatch::log_uncaught_exception(err, py)` renders the full real traceback via `err.traceback(py)`'s own real `.format()` (`pyo3::types::PyTracebackMethods`, confirmed via direct source read — the one real way to get the same full text `PyErr::print` used to write straight to stderr, as an owned `String`), falling back to plain `Display` if the traceback is missing or fails to format; `call_handler`/`run_completions` (a real, third call site the original scoping paragraph never named) both call it. `app.rs`'s two `eprintln!` sites become `tracing::warn!`. Four real findings, none caught by unit tests alone: **(1)** three existing tests asserting `capsys.readouterr().err` failed with empty captures even though the event genuinely fired — `capsys` only ever worked by coincidence (`PyErr::print` happened to route through Python's own `sys.stderr` object; `tracing_subscriber`'s writer is a raw OS-level Rust write that bypasses it entirely) — fixed by switching four tests (the three plus `test_two_way_binding.py`'s own recursion regression test, which had a real, silent-weakening risk: `capsys`'s blindness would have made its `captured.err == ""` assertion trivially pass regardless of whether a real recursion error fired) to `capfd`. **(2)** `App::run`'s own subscriber install isn't the one guaranteed place a subscriber needs to be live — `Window.click`/`Node.set_checked`/`View.click` are all real, independently callable without `App::run()` ever running, and two tests using them depended on cross-file test *order* accidentally installing the subscriber first; fixed by extracting `dispatch::ensure_tracing_subscriber()`, called from both `App::run` and `log_uncaught_exception` itself (idempotent, so a second call site is free), re-verified by running the affected files in total isolation. **(3)** `tracing_subscriber::fmt`'s own default writer is stdout, not stderr (confirmed via direct source read) — fixed with `.with_writer(std::io::stderr)`. **(4)** switching to the builder chain (needed for `.with_writer`) silently dropped the free function's own special `EnvFilter::from_default_env()` wiring — every example started emitting unconditional INFO events; fixed by wiring `EnvFilter` explicitly. New `tests/test_tracing.py` (2 tests, real subprocess-based, since a subscriber's filter is captured once at construction and can't be tested by toggling `RUST_LOG` mid-process): default stays silent, `RUST_LOG=info` genuinely verbose with the real `app_run` span visible — both passed after the fix. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean; pytest (153 passed, up from 151, 1 skipped) and all twenty-three examples confirmed clean, including a manual `RUST_LOG=info` vs. default-quiet re-check after every finding. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M16 — Structured Logging via `tracing` is now fully complete.** Both phases (subscriber wiring, real call-site migration) closed §3/§9's own stated-but-unbuilt policy — a raising callback's traceback now reaches a real, structured `tracing::error!` event instead of a raw `PyErr::print` stderr write, with real `RUST_LOG`-driven verbosity and a real `app_run` span as the foundation for whatever this codebase logs next. Four real, non-obvious findings along the way, none caught by unit tests alone — every one caught by actually running real code end to end, not by reasoning about the API in advance.

**Just closed:** M17 Phase 1 — real clipboard copy/cut/paste (§8), the last of the three milestones scoped alongside M15/M16. **The genuine, stated unknown, resolved by actually testing it:** a throwaway probe confirmed `arboard = "3.6.1"` genuinely connects to a real, live clipboard in this environment — kept afterward as a real, permanent regression test (`engine-py`'s own first-ever Rust unit test), gracefully skipping, not failing, if a future environment has none. **Real, non-obvious finding, changing the whole design:** `winit`'s own `KeyEvent.logical_key` is documented "affected by all modifiers except Ctrl" — a real Ctrl+C press produces `Character("c")`, identical to a bare `c`, meaning **before this phase, a real Ctrl+C press on a focused `TextField` inserted a literal "c" character**, a genuine pre-existing latent bug this phase's own Ctrl-modifier detection fixes as a real side effect of correct ordering, not a separate patch. `InputEvent` gains `Copy`/`Cut`/`PasteRequested` (zero-payload intent signals, true no-ops in `Tree::dispatch` — the real work happens in `engine-py`'s own raw-event handling, matching dock-drag/theme-switch's own established shape). New `Tree::text_field_selected_text`/`cut_text_field_selection` (pure read; read + delete, reusing `delete_selection`). Paste reuses the existing `InputEvent::TextInput` mechanism completely — no new insertion path. **Real design gap, found while writing pytest coverage, not anticipated:** a real Cut is called directly on `Tree`, never through `Tree::dispatch`, so it never got `DispatchOutcome::Changed`'s own automatic `Change` firing the way keyboard editing does — fixed by adding an explicit `call_handler` call to both `Window.cut()` and the real winit-driven path, caught by a test that would otherwise have silently passed with `cut()` never firing `Change` at all. A real Cut also writes to the clipboard *before* deleting the selection, only actually deleting once that write succeeds — a failed write must never destroy the user's own selected text. New `Window.copy()`/`cut()`/`paste(text)`: deliberately **hermetic** (never touch the real OS clipboard) — a real Ctrl+C only ever originates from an actual OS-level keyboard event, and there is no synthetic way to drive that specific path from Python, a real, stated scope boundary. New tests: `engine-core` (5), `engine-platform` (3), the real permanent clipboard round-trip test (`engine-py`), `tests/test_clipboard.py` (8) — all passed after the real Change-firing fix. New `examples/clipboard.py`. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (`engine-core` 122, up from 117; `engine-platform` 9, up from 6; `engine-py` 1, new); pytest (161 passed, up from 153, 1 skipped) and all twenty-four examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**Just closed:** M17 Phase 2 — real IME composition preview (§8), **closing M17 entirely (both phases) and the whole M15/M16/M17 sequence**. **Real, load-bearing finding, confirmed via direct source read, that would have silently dead-ended the whole feature:** `winit::window::Window::set_ime_allowed`'s own doc comment states plainly "IME is not allowed by default" — without calling it, `WindowEvent::Ime` never fires at all; fixed by calling it once, at the one real window-creation site. Same doc comment: "during the preedit phase the window will NOT get `KeyboardInput` events" — composing and plain typing are already mutually exclusive at the `winit` level, nothing this codebase needs to coordinate itself. `TextFieldState` gains `preedit: Option<String>` — purely visual, uncommitted, never spliced into real `content`. `engine-platform`'s `WindowEvent::Ime(ime)` translates `Preedit(text, _)` (dropping `winit`'s own sub-cursor-range detail, a real, deliberate scope narrowing) to a new `InputEvent::ImePreedit(String)` (empty string means "cleared," matching `winit`'s own real convention) and `Commit(text)` directly to the existing `InputEvent::TextInput(text)` — no new variant needed for a real commit at all, it reaches the identical mechanism a plain keypress already uses (M15 Phase 2). `Tree::dispatch`'s new `ImePreedit` arm sets/clears the focused field's own `preedit`, always `DispatchOutcome::None` (a composition preview isn't committed content); the existing `TextInput` arm additionally clears a stale `preedit` on every real insertion, defensive. `TextRenderer::draw_field` splices `preedit` into a *display-only* copy of the content at `cursor` when composing (`content` itself never mutated), reusing the same `Selection::geometry` mechanism the selection highlight already established (M15 Phase 1) to paint a real underline under that span — a thin rect at each bound's own bottom edge instead of a translucent fill; the caret shows at the end of the spliced-in preedit while composing, not the raw, frozen cursor underneath it. **Confirmed, not assumed:** `engine-py::app.rs`'s `on_input` closure already calls `tree.dispatch(root, event.clone(), ...)` unconditionally before its own raw-event match — since `ImePreedit`'s mutation happens entirely inside `Tree::dispatch` and always returns `DispatchOutcome::None`, no additional handling was needed in `app.rs` at all, unlike `Copy`/`Cut`/`PasteRequested` (M17 Phase 1), which are pure intent signals needing real OS I/O only `engine-py` can do. **Real, honest deviation from `PLAN.md`:** the plan called for a new `engine-platform` translation test, but none was added — the `Ime` match arm is trivial inline logic (unlike `translate_clipboard_shortcut`, a genuine pure function worth isolating), the same "not manufactured ahead of a real need" scope `ThemeChanged`'s own translation already established with no dedicated test of its own. New `engine-core` tests (4, all passed first run): a real preedit sets/clears the focused field without touching `content`; an empty string clears an active preview; no focused field is a true no-op; a real `TextInput` commit clears a stale preedit. New `engine-render/tests/text_field_paint.rs` pixel tests (2): a composing field's own render differs from an otherwise pixel-identical, non-composing field, proving a real underline exists; a defensive `Some(String::new())` preedit paints byte-identical to a real `None`. **Confirmed, not assumed:** no new Python-facing FFI surface or pytest coverage was needed — a real IME composition event only ever originates from an actual OS input method, the same real scope boundary M17 Phase 1 already established for a real Ctrl+C/X/V keypress; `examples/text_field.py`'s own docstring updated to point at `text_field_paint.rs`'s new pixel tests as the definitive proof instead of naming clipboard/IME as an unscoped gap. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (`engine-core` 126, up from 122; `engine-render` 4 in `text_field_paint.rs`, up from 2); pytest (161 passed, unchanged from M17 Phase 1, confirming no new Python surface was needed, 1 skipped) and all twenty-four examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

**M17 — Clipboard & IME for TextField is now fully complete.** Both phases (real clipboard copy/cut/paste, real IME composition preview) closed §8's own stated gap — a focused `TextField` now has real Ctrl+C/X/V clipboard support and a real, live IME composition preview with a genuine underline, the last of the deferred capabilities M15's own status line explicitly named. This also closes the entire three-milestone sequence (M15 TextField, M16 structured logging, M17 clipboard/IME) scoped together at the user's own explicit request. Two real, load-bearing findings caught along the way, neither obvious from the API surface alone: a pre-existing latent bug where Ctrl+C inserted a literal "c" character (M17 Phase 1, `winit`'s own `logical_key` being Ctrl-blind), and `Window::set_ime_allowed` defaulting to `false` (M17 Phase 2), which would have silently made real IME composition never fire at all had it gone unnoticed.

**CI fix, found checking CI on the freshly-pushed M15/M16/M17 commits (2026-09-17):** all eight commits were pushed together, so this was the very first CI run to include any of them. `test_rust_log_info_genuinely_raises_the_real_verbosity` (`tests/test_tracing.py`, M16 Phase 2) failed on the Linux `test` job — a real, previously-unvalidated gap, not a regression from M17. Investigated, not assumed: `App::run` had exactly one `tracing::info_span!` and zero bare `tracing::info!` events anywhere in `engine-py` — the test only ever passed locally because a real display let a real `wgpu` adapter get created, and `wgpu_hal`'s own incidental INFO-level logging happened to land inside the `app_run` span's context. CI's `test` job deliberately has no Xvfb (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s own header comment), so `App::run` always takes the "no display available" early-exit path there, before ever reaching adapter creation — never producing a real `INFO` line at all. **Fix:** a real, deliberate `tracing::info!("starting a real app run session")` now fires unconditionally right after the `app_run` span is entered, before any early-exit path — genuine, permanent instrumentation (a real observability gap, not just test fragility: `RUST_LOG=info` previously gave a user nothing first-party to look at on a successful run). Reproduced the CI failure locally first (`env -u DISPLAY -u WAYLAND_DISPLAY pytest tests/test_tracing.py -v`), confirmed it failed identically, then confirmed the fix passes both with and without a display. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; pytest (161 passed, 1 skipped) run twice, with and without a display; all twenty-four examples re-run with a display, `examples/animate_rect.py` (CI's own script) additionally re-run with no display. See `LOG.md`.

**Up next:** M18 Phase 1 — real click-to-position for `TextField` (§8, §10, §11.9, §11.10), the first of two milestones scoped together at the user's own explicit request ("scope the next milestone"). Two real, investigated candidates were presented: mouse interaction for `TextField` (recommended and chosen, alongside the second); declarative-authoring completeness (hot-reload, §16.4; view composition via `include:`, §16.6) — the user chose both, scoped together the same way M15/M16/M17 were. `TextField` mouse interaction is the single most-repeatedly-named deferred gap in this file — M15's own status line, and M17's own scoping paragraph both explicitly named it "a further, real, un-scoped candidate beyond even M17," confirmed still true via a fresh grep today (zero pointer-driven cursor/selection code anywhere in `Tree::dispatch`). Declarative-authoring completeness is a real "built but disconnected" gap of the exact shape M7 (MD3 Visual Fidelity) had before it was built: confirmed via direct read, `engine-spec::watch::ViewWatcher`'s own module doc comment states outright "wiring that poll into a real running `engine-py` `App` is deliberately deferred," and confirmed via grep, `engine-py::view.rs`'s `View` pyclass has no `reload`/`reconcile` method at all — not even a manual one exists yet, despite `Reconciler` (§16.4) being real and unit-tested inside `engine-spec` since M3 step 5. `include:` composition (§16.6) is equally real and unimplemented: confirmed via grep, `WidgetSpec` (`engine-spec::spec.rs`) has no `include` field anywhere.

**Real finding during M18's own investigation, before any code:** `engine-core` has zero `engine-render`/`parley` visibility by design (§4's crate-boundary rule, unbroken since M3) — `Tree::dispatch` cannot itself turn a pixel point into a byte offset, the same real boundary M15's own scoping already named. Confirmed via direct read: `GpuState.text_renderer: TextRenderer` (`engine-py::app.rs`) is real and already reachable per-window (used every frame by `build_tree_scene`), but the `on_input` closure's own `runtimes_for_input.borrow()` is an immutable `RefCell::borrow()` — a real hit-test call would need `&mut self.font_cx`/`&mut self.layout_cx` the same way `draw_field` already does, so reaching `TextRenderer` from `on_input` needs a real, small borrow-shape adjustment, not yet resolved, left for Phase 1's own implementation. Precedent exists for "`engine-py` calls a `Tree` method directly, bypassing `Tree::dispatch`'s `DispatchOutcome` set, for something needing a capability `engine-core` structurally can't have" — M17 Phase 1's clipboard methods (`text_field_selected_text`/`cut_text_field_selection`) already established exactly this shape for real OS clipboard access; click-to-position plausibly follows the same pattern (real hit-testing happens in `engine-py`, which then calls a new, plain `Tree` method to set the resulting cursor/selection). `NodeKind::Canvas`'s own custom hit-test callback (§11.10) is a real but *different* precedent — a closure the app supplies at construction time, not a live computation from per-frame glyph shaping — so this is genuinely new architectural work, not a reuse of an existing mechanism.

**Real finding during M19's own investigation, before any code:** `ViewWatcher::watch` (`engine-spec::watch.rs`) wraps a real `notify::RecommendedWatcher` and is fully tested (`watcher_detects_a_real_write_to_the_watched_file`), but its own module doc comment states plainly that wiring its `poll_changed()` into a real running `App`'s frame loop was deliberately deferred at the time it was written (§14 step 5-era). `Reconciler::reconcile` (`engine-spec::reconcile.rs`) is likewise real and unit-tested (keyed diffing by `id` + `NodeKind`, preserving `NodeId`/focus/in-flight animations across a reload — confirmed via direct read of its own tests) but is called from nowhere in `engine-py` at all — `View::new` calls `Reconciler::load` exactly once, at construction, and never again. `include:` composition (§16.6) carries real, stated security requirements in its own ARCHITECTURE.md text — path confinement (no `../` escaping the view directory), cycle detection (a file cannot transitively include itself), and a depth limit — none of which exist today since the feature itself doesn't exist; these aren't optional hardening, they're the same "confinement to the untrusted-input discipline §8 already established" pattern this codebase has followed since M3.
