## Milestone 10 — Interaction & Overlay Completeness

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 3 phases done (2026-09-17).** The user chose this direction explicitly over two other real, investigated candidates (Canvas Authoring Completeness — `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` still confirmed to only build straight polylines (`polyline()`), even though the underlying hit-test math already supports true bezier curves, `Window.redraw_canvas` still explicitly app-triggered only; Variable-Height `VirtualList` Items — `ItemExtent` still has exactly one real variant, `Fixed`, re-confirmed today). This is the single most-repeatedly-carried-forward candidate across M6 through M9's own scoping, confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `OverlayMeta.dismiss_on_outside_click`/`dismiss_on_escape` (`crates/engine-core/src/overlay.rs`) are real data, stored correctly since M3 step 13, but genuinely never read by any dispatch mechanism (confirmed via grep — `overlay_meta` has exactly the one real reader, `dispatch::open_context_menu`'s own re-open guard, never a dismissal check). `overlay.rs`'s own module doc comment is stale, still claiming "real pointer/keyboard `InputEvent`/`AppHandler` dispatch and hit-testing... don't exist anywhere in this codebase yet" — true at M3 step 13, false since M4 built all of it. `Node.set_context_menu` (`node.rs`) and `dock::set_dock_handle` still have no `Rc::ptr_eq` same-tree check at all (confirmed via grep — only `Node.add_child`, M6 Phase 1, and `Window.begin_container_transform`/`end_container_transform`, M7 Phase 5, have one) — a real, small correctness gap M6 Phase 1's own investigation first surfaced and explicitly deferred. No drop-zone highlight overlay exists for docking — `Tree::open_overlay`'s own real positioning is anchor-relative only (placed below a single anchor node's bounds), not "cover an arbitrary target rect," which is what highlighting a drop zone during a drag actually needs — confirmed via direct read of `open_overlay`'s own implementation, not assumed from the Known Gaps note alone.

### Phase 1 — Real Overlay Dismissal (§11.3) ✅
- Step 1: `Tree::dispatch`'s real `PointerPressed`/`PointerReleased` and `KeyPressed` arms close any currently-open overlay whose own `OverlayMeta` opts into `dismiss_on_outside_click`/`dismiss_on_escape`, reusing the already-real `Tree::hit_test`/`Tree::close_overlay` — a real press outside every open overlay's own subtree, or a real `Key::Escape`, closes it; `overlay.rs`'s own stale module doc comment corrected to reflect that real dispatch has existed since M4 — ✅ (wired to `PointerPressed` only, not `PointerReleased`, matching real desktop convention; consumes the dismissing press rather than also clicking through. Two real findings while verifying end to end: `close_overlay` originally destroyed its own content node — never exercised by a live app before this phase — breaking the realistic "dismiss then reopen the same anchor" case, fixed by detaching instead, matching `set_context_menu`'s own existing contract; outside-click dismissal's first draft also wrongly treated a press back on the overlay's own anchor as "outside," fixed by excluding it too. 8 new `engine-core` tests + 1 existing test updated for the real contract change; 2 new pytest tests. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; pytest (85 passed, up from 83, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Same-Tree Safety Checks for `set_context_menu`/`set_dock_handle` (§8) ✅
- Step 1: `Node.set_context_menu` and `dock::set_dock_handle` gain the same `Rc::ptr_eq` same-tree guard `Node.add_child`/`Window.begin_container_transform` already use, rejecting a `Node`/handle from a foreign `Window`'s own `Tree` with a real `EngineError` instead of silently storing a foreign `NodeId` that could alias an unrelated real node the next time it's read back — ✅ (`Node.set_context_menu` checked directly; `dock::set_dock_handle` itself is a free function with no `Node` context of its own, so the check lives at its one real caller, `PyWindow.set_dock_handle`, instead — both widened from `()` to `PyResult<()>`, additive, confirmed via grep no Rust-level caller exists for either. 2 new pytest tests, both passed on the first run; existing tests unmodified. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (no `engine-core` touched); pytest (87 passed, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples unaffected. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — Drop-Zone Highlight Overlay for Docking (§11.4) ✅
- Step 1: A real way to position an overlay to cover an arbitrary target rect (a dock zone's own real computed bounds), not just anchor-relative-below — the concrete primitive gap `open_overlay`'s own real implementation confirms is missing — ✅ (new `Tree::position_overlay_over(content, rect)` in `engine-core`, next to `open_overlay`: sets `Position::Absolute` with `inset` from the rect's own origin and an explicit `style.size` matching its width/height, independent of any anchor; deliberately doesn't attach the node or touch `self.overlays` — a rect-covering highlight has no anchor and no outside-click/Escape dismissal, so attach/detach lifecycle is the caller's own. New `engine-core` unit test proves it covers an arbitrary rect and resizes to it, independent of any anchor.)
- Step 2: Wires that primitive into the real drag-in-progress path (`dock::start_drag`/the real `PointerMoved`-during-drag dispatch) so the zone currently under the pointer shows a real, live highlight, closing the milestone — ✅ (new `DockState.highlight: Option<NodeId>`; `set_drop_zone_highlight` registers it, detaching it from root first — the same `set_context_menu`-style "starts hidden" contract, since `add_rect` already attached it to root immediately; new `dock::drag_over` is the real `PointerMoved`-during-drag step this module previously had no need for, reusing `enclosing_zone` unchanged to find the enclosing zone (if any), showing the highlight over it or hiding it if none; `end_drag_at` gains one new unconditional step, before its own existing early returns, that always hides the highlight when a drag ends, for any reason. New `PyWindow.set_drop_zone_highlight` (the same `Rc::ptr_eq` guard M10 Phase 2 added to `set_dock_handle`) and `drag_panel_over`, the same synthetic-dispatch pattern `start_panel_drag`/`drop_panel_at` already establish. **Real finding, not anticipated in `PLAN.md`:** the first pytest draft reused `(200.0, 50.0)` from an existing test as "inside the Right zone" — direct debugging showed that point actually sits exactly on the zone's own flex-shrunk right edge, not inside it, revealing that *pre-existing* test's own "functional proof" doesn't actually discriminate a real move from a same-zone no-op; left as-is, out of this phase's own scope. New tests use `(160.0, 50.0)`, confirmed to land genuinely inside. `examples/docking.py` registers a real highlight; its own doc comment, which named this exact gap, is corrected. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 81, up from 80); pytest (93 passed, up from 87, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 11 — Canvas Authoring Completeness

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** The user chose this direction explicitly over two other real, investigated candidates (Variable-Height `VirtualList` Items — `ItemExtent` still has exactly one real variant, `Fixed`, re-confirmed today via direct read of `node.rs`; `AppShell`/Single-Page Navigation, §11.2 — a newly-surfaced gap, never previously named in any prior milestone's own scoping: ARCHITECTURE.md §11.2 fully specifies an `AppShell` struct with named menu-bar/toolbar/dock/status-bar/content regions, confirmed via grep to have zero implementation anywhere in this codebase, not even a stub). This is the single most-repeatedly-passed-over candidate across M8 and M10's own scoping, confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` (`crates/engine-py/src/canvas.rs`) both build their `kurbo::BezPath` through a private `polyline()` helper using only `move_to`/`line_to` — no `quad_to`/`curve_to` exposed to Python at all, confirmed via direct read. The underlying hit-test machinery already generalizes to true curves: `CustomHitTest::Path` stores a full `BezPath`, and `tree.rs` already imports `kurbo::ParamCurveNearest` for its distance-to-path math, which works identically for line/quad/cubic segments — only the *authoring* surface is polyline-only, not the math underneath it. `Window.redraw_canvas` also remains a purely app-triggered `PyWindow` method — confirmed via grep, zero automatic per-frame invocation anywhere in `App::run`'s own frame loop — but whether that's a real gap or a deliberate, correct choice for this framework's retained-mode `Tree` model (an immediate-mode "redraw everything every frame" policy would waste real work for canvas content that hasn't changed) is itself an open question this milestone's own Phase 2 investigates rather than presupposes.

### Phase 1 — True Bezier Curve Authoring (§11.10, §11.11) ✅
- Step 1: `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` gain a real way to author curved segments (quadratic/cubic bezier), not just straight polylines — exposing the same curve vocabulary `kurbo::BezPath` already supports internally and `CustomHitTest::Path`'s own distance math already handles, closing the concrete authoring gap `polyline()`'s own doc comment names (§11.10's own stated "a bezier curve within N pixels of the point" example currently only ever gets a straight-line approximation) — ✅ (`polyline` renamed to `build_path`, widened from `Vec<(f64, f64)>` to `Vec<Vec<f64>>` — 2 numbers is a line-to (fully backward-compatible with every existing call site), 4 is `quad_to`, 6 is `curve_to`, any other length a real `PyValueError`; `stroke_path`/`set_hit_test_path` keep their exact signatures, both still calling the one shared helper. Rejected a custom `FromPyObject` tagged-point enum — pyo3 0.29's own more complex trait shape, real avoidable complexity — in favor of plain arity-dispatch. New `engine-core` unit test proves the resulting hit-test is genuinely curve-aware: a real curve's own true midpoint, 50px from the naive straight chord between its endpoints, hits; a point 2px from that chord but ~48px from the real curve misses — the adversarial pair a chord-only distance would get wrong. New pytest coverage proves the FFI surface accepts real quadratic/cubic segments and rejects invalid point lengths. No `engine-core` production change, only the new test. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 82, up from 81); pytest (96 passed, up from 93, 1 skipped) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean, including the three pre-existing `stroke_path` call sites unmodified. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Canvas Redraw Triggering: Investigate and Resolve (§11.10, §11.11) ✅
- Step 1: Determine whether `redraw_canvas`'s explicit, app-triggered-only design is a real gap or a deliberate, correct choice for this framework's retained-mode `Tree` model, and either wire real automatic redraw for whatever specific case genuinely needs it, or formally close the question with a stated, justified "no change needed" — closing the milestone either way — ✅ (confirmed structural, not an oversight: `canvas_draws` never appears in `app.rs`'s own real winit frame loop — not part of `WindowSetup`/`WindowRuntime`, the two structs carrying everything the per-frame closures can see, which only call `Tree::tick_all` and drain completions. `WindowSetup`'s own doc comment already states every Python-callback field threaded into the frame loop is invoked only on a real, specific event, never unconditional per-frame work — adding `canvas_draws` would be the first blanket per-frame Python invocation in this codebase. `ARCHITECTURE.md` names no automatic-redraw requirement. Real cost analysis: unconditional per-frame redraw would impose a real Python call, a fresh `CanvasContext`, and a full `Vec<DrawCommand>` rebuild every frame for every registered canvas regardless of whether content changed — exactly the per-frame overhead this codebase consistently designs against elsewhere. **Resolution: no change needed** — the current app-triggered model is deliberate and correct for a retained-mode `Tree`, mirroring M7 Phase 5 Step 1's own "already existed, nothing new needed" resolution shape. `BUILD_TRACKER.md`'s own M5 carried-forward gap note corrected to record this. No production code change; full suite/clippy/fmt/pytest/all examples re-confirmed clean as a formality. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 12 — Variable-Height VirtualList Items

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** The user chose this direction explicitly over two other real, investigated candidates (Form Components & Two-Way Data Binding, §16.7 — a newly-surfaced, substantial gap: ARCHITECTURE.md §16.7 fully specifies two-way binding sugar (a `TextField`'s text auto-writing back to its bound `Signal`), entirely unimplemented, confirmed via grep zero hits for "two_way"/"TwoWay" anywhere in `engine-spec`/`engine-py`; can't be built yet regardless, since the `NodeKind`s it needs (`TextField`/`Slider`/`Checkbox`) don't exist at all (confirmed via grep) and `EventKind` (`input.rs`) still has only `Click`/`HoverEnter`/`HoverExit`, no `Change` variant — real, but substantially larger in scope than the other two candidates, new MD3 components rather than wiring existing data; `AppShell`/Single-Page Navigation, §11.2 — confirmed via grep to still have zero implementation anywhere, despite ARCHITECTURE.md's own detailed struct sketch). This is the single most-repeatedly-passed-over candidate of all three real M12 candidates investigated, confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `ItemExtent` (`crates/engine-core/src/node.rs`) still has exactly one real variant, `Fixed(f64)`, re-confirmed today via direct read — its own doc comment already states the architectural intent verbatim, unchanged since M8: "fixed, or a size-hint callback for variable-height items... no consumer needs the callback-based variant yet." Two real call sites in `tree.rs` still assume uniform spacing, confirmed via direct read: `set_virtual_list_window`'s own materialization math positions every item at `idx as f64 * item_extent` (line 816), and the M8 Phase 3 scroll-clamping math computes total scrollable content extent as `item_extent.value() * item_count` (line 850) — both are real, concrete primitive gaps a variable per-item height breaks, not just a config-surface limitation. `engine-py::PyWindow.add_virtual_list`/`set_virtual_list_window` (`window.rs`) both only expose `item_extent: f64`, and `set_virtual_list_window`'s own `let ItemExtent::Fixed(v) = state.item_extent;` (line 838) is an irrefutable pattern only because `ItemExtent` has exactly one variant today — the exact spot a second variant would force a real match arm.

### Phase 1 — Real Variable-Height Extent Support in `engine-core` (§11.7) ✅
- Step 1: `ItemExtent` gains a real way to represent variable, per-item extents (not a callback stored inside `engine-core` itself — §4's own pyo3-agnostic boundary rules that out, the same real reason `materialize` lives in `engine-py`, not here); `VirtualListState`'s own materialization math (`idx * item_extent`) and the M8 Phase 3 scroll-clamping math (`item_extent * item_count`) both generalize to real per-item cumulative sums over whatever extents are actually known, closing the concrete primitive gap `ItemExtent`'s own doc comment has named since M8 — ✅ (`ItemExtent::Variable` — a fieldless marker; the real per-item data lives on new `VirtualListState.resolved_offsets: BTreeMap<usize, f64>`, storing already-*cumulative* offsets, not raw heights, so `engine-core` never sums anything itself, only looks a resolved value up — key `item_count` holds the real total extent. New `VirtualListState::offset_of`/`total_extent` are the single shared source of truth both `tree.rs` call sites now use, replacing the old uniform formulas; `ItemExtent::value()` removed, its two callers rewritten. New `Tree::set_virtual_list_resolved_offsets` is Phase 2's own entry point into this storage. New tests: `Fixed` behavior unchanged; a real, non-uniform resolved-offset set proves real per-item positioning and total-extent clamping for `Variable`; a `#[should_panic]` test proves an unresolved index panics clearly. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 86, up from 82); pytest (96 passed, 1 skipped, unchanged) and all sixteen examples confirmed clean — a pure regression check, this phase touches no Python-facing behavior. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Python-Facing Size-Hint Callback (§11.7) ✅
- Step 1: `Window.add_virtual_list`/`set_virtual_list_window` gain a real way to supply per-item heights from Python — a size-hint callback, mirroring `materialize`'s own per-index "resolve once, cache" callback shape (`materializers: HashMap<NodeId, Py<PyAny>>`) rather than inventing a second storage mechanism — wiring Phase 1's new `engine-core` capability through to real app code, closing the milestone — ✅ (new `size_hint: Optional[Callable[[int], float]]`, `item_extent` now optional — exactly one of the two required, a real `ValueError` otherwise. Resolves *eagerly, once*, at `add_virtual_list` time — calling `size_hint` exactly `item_count` times immediately, building the complete cumulative-offset table via `Tree::set_virtual_list_resolved_offsets` — a real, deliberate, stated `O(item_count)` cost, not a lazy incremental cache (real avoidable complexity this milestone's own one-line architecture text doesn't ask for). New private `ResolvedItemHeights` snapshot feeds `set_virtual_list_window`'s materializer closure each item's own real height (the difference between two adjacent resolved cumulative offsets) — extracted up front, mirroring the pre-existing scalar-extraction shape, since the closure can't also borrow `tree` live from inside itself. New pytest coverage: `size_hint` called exactly once per item, in order, at `add_virtual_list` time, never re-invoked by `set_virtual_list_window`; both/neither-given error paths; exception propagation, matching `materialize`'s own contract. New `examples/variable_height_list.py`: a real, live 500-row list with a genuinely non-uniform, repeating five-step row-height pattern, scrolled for real. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings` (one real `too_many_arguments` lint, resolved with a scoped, justified `#[allow]`)/fmt clean (no `engine-core` change, `engine-py`-only); pytest (100 passed, up from 96, 1 skipped) and all seventeen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 13 — AppShell / Single-Page Navigation (§11.2)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** Scoped together with M14 at the user's own explicit request ("scope milestone 13 and 14"); the user picked both directions from three real, investigated candidates (this one and Form Components & Two-Way Data Binding, both presented as recommended defaults and confirmed; a third, structured logging via `tracing`, §3/§9, was investigated but not chosen for either slot — see M12's own closing "Up next" paragraph). Confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `AppShell` (ARCHITECTURE.md §11.2) is fully specified — a persistent root layout with named `menu_bar`/`toolbar`/`dock`/`status_bar` regions and exactly one stable `content` region, where "navigating" means replacing `content`'s own children, an ordinary, already-supported tree mutation (`Tree::remove`/`add_child`, both real since M3/M6) — but has zero implementation anywhere, confirmed via grep (zero hits for `AppShell` in any crate or in this file's own history). Two real, concrete gaps the implementation will need to resolve, confirmed via direct read: `PyWindow` has no Python-facing way to create a plain `NodeKind::Container` at all — only `Container` in this whole codebase is the window's own root, built once in `PyWindow::new` (`window.rs:219`) — though `Window.add_rect` with a transparent background is already the established practical substitute apps use for a plain layout box (confirmed via every docking/canvas example's own `background=(0, 0, 0, 0)` pattern); and the window's own root `Style` is hardcoded `FlexDirection::Row` (`window.rs`, `PyWindow::new`), which conflicts with a real header/content/footer *column* shell layout, so shell composition needs its own dedicated sub-container, not a reuse of the root's own direction. §11.2's own text confirms no new `engine-core` primitive is needed at all — `taffy`'s existing flexbox and the already-real `add_child`/`remove` cover everything specified; this is purely a Python-facing composition + navigation feature.

### Phase 1 — Real AppShell Composition (§11.2) ✅
- Step 1: A real `Window`-level way to build the shell's named regions (menu bar, toolbar, status bar, and the stable `content` swap region) in one call, as ordinary flex-composed containers positioned by a real header/content/footer column layout — reusing `add_rect`'s own established transparent-container pattern, no new `engine-core` primitive (confirmed by investigation), closing the concrete "fully specified, zero implementation" gap — ✅ (new `Window.build_shell(menu_bar=None, toolbar=None, status_bar=None) -> Node`, returning `content`. **Real finding, caught live by `examples/app_shell.py`'s own `accesskit` validation, not pytest:** the first draft re-parented already-attached regions with the cheap `Tree::add_child`, leaving each listed as a child of both its old parent and the new shell — fixed with `Tree::try_add_child`, which detaches first. New `tests/test_app_shell.py` (4 tests) + new `examples/app_shell.py`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; pytest (104 passed, up from 100, 1 skipped) and all eighteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real Content Navigation (§11.2, optionally §7.6) ✅
- Step 1: A real `Window`-level "navigate" operation — replaces `content`'s own children for real (`Tree::remove` + `add_child`, both already real), optionally choreographed through the already-real `begin_container_transform`/`end_container_transform` (§7.6) for a cross-fade/transform transition between screens, matching §11.2's own "not a general multi-screen router... matching the single-page-app model" text — closing the milestone — ✅ (new `Node.remove()`, mirroring `add_child`'s own minimal shape, calling the already-real `Tree::remove`. New `tests/test_remove.py`: a genuine detach proven via a new sibling taking the same real position; removal of a subtree doesn't corrupt the rest of the tree. New `examples/navigation.py`: two real screens built into a real `AppShell`'s own `content`, navigated between via remove+add, proven by a real dispatched click landing on the second screen's own card. Deliberately doesn't combine this with `begin_container_transform` in the same script — `container_transform.py` already proves that mechanism on its own. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (no `engine-core` change); pytest (106 passed, up from 104, 1 skipped) and all nineteen examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 14 — Form Components & Two-Way Data Binding (§7.3, §16.7)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 3 phases done (2026-09-17).** Scoped together with M13 at the user's own explicit request; see M13's own status line for the full three-candidate context. Confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: ARCHITECTURE.md §5's own `NodeKind` struct sketch already names `Slider(SliderState)`/`Checkbox(CheckboxState)` with complete real field shapes (`SliderState.thumb_position: Animated<f64>`, `CheckboxState.check_progress: Animated<f64>`) — confirmed via grep, neither variant exists in `engine-core::node.rs` today, whose own module doc comment has named `Slider`/`Checkbox` as deferred since M3 step 3. `EventKind` (`input.rs`) still has only `Click`/`HoverEnter`/`HoverExit` — no `Change` variant, confirmed via direct read — the exact variant §16.7's own text says a two-way binding's auto-generated handler needs. Two-way binding sugar itself (§16.7) is entirely unimplemented, confirmed via grep, zero hits for `two_way`/`TwoWay` anywhere in `engine-spec`/`engine-py`. **Deliberately excludes `TextField`** despite §16.7's own text naming it alongside `Slider`/`Checkbox`: real keyboard text entry (character input, cursor, selection, IME) is a substantially larger, genuinely different undertaking than a click/drag-driven component — no real text-input dispatch mechanism exists anywhere in this codebase today (confirmed via grep) — and remains a real, separate, un-scoped future candidate, not silently dropped. `Checkbox`/`Slider` both reuse entirely real, existing mechanisms: `Node.enable_interaction()` (M4 Phase 5) is the already-generic ripple/state-layer opt-in any interactive node can use, and `Tree::set_splitter_position`/`splitter_geometry` (§11.5) are the real, direct precedent for a component's own pointer-driven drag math (`Slider`'s thumb position, the identical "drag moves a 0.0..=1.0 position along an axis" shape `SplitterState.position` already has).

### Phase 1 — Real Checkbox (§5, §7.3) ✅
- Step 1: `NodeKind::Checkbox(CheckboxState)` with real paint (a checkmark draw path, animated via `check_progress`) and real click-to-toggle interaction reusing the already-real `Click` dispatch and `Node.enable_interaction()`'s own ripple/state-layer mechanism — ✅ (`CheckboxState { checked: bool, check_progress: Animated<f64> }`; `tick_all` gains its first-ever kind-specific payload tick; `build_access_update` derives real `Toggled` directly from `checked`, no duplicated field. `paint_node` strokes a real checkmark `BezPath`, opacity driven by `check_progress` (plain white, not yet theme-aware). `Node.animate`/`.get` gain their first real kind-payload dispatch arm (`"check_progress"`); new `Node.set_checked(bool)`; new `Window.add_checkbox`. Real finding caught by pytest: `Animated<T>` never updates `current` until a real tick runs, not a Checkbox-specific bug — fixed by testing only what's provable without a live render loop, leaving the animation claim to the new `engine-render/tests/checkbox_paint.rs` pixel test. New `tests/test_checkbox.py` (7 tests) + `examples/checkbox.py`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 88, up from 86, +2 pixel tests); pytest (113 passed, up from 106, 1 skipped) and all twenty examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real Slider (§5, §7.3) ✅
- Step 1: `NodeKind::Slider(SliderState)` with real paint (track + thumb, animated via `thumb_position`) and real drag-to-set interaction, the same pointer-driven position math `Tree::set_splitter_position`/`splitter_geometry` already established for `SplitterState.position` — ✅ (`thumb_position` *is* the real value, no separate field; `Tree::set_slider_position` mirrors `set_splitter_position`'s own instant + immediate-tick shape. `self.dragging` broadened to cover either kind; `update_drag` now dispatches on the dragged node's own real kind (`update_splitter_drag`/new `update_slider_drag`, the node's own width alone as the track, no siblings). Real finding: `thumb_position` *is* exposed to `Node.animate()` (unlike `SplitterState.position`), so `tick_all` needed a new arm too, or a real triggered move would never progress — caught while designing. `paint_node` gains a real track + thumb; new `Window.add_slider`; `"thumb_position"` reaches `animate`/`.get`. New `engine-core` tests mirror the existing splitter-drag tests exactly (every one of those kept passing unmodified); new `engine-render/tests/slider_paint.rs` (thumb at 0.0/1.0 paints at the real edges). New `tests/test_slider.py` (6 tests) + `examples/slider.py`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 92, up from 88, +2 pixel tests); pytest (119 passed, up from 113, 1 skipped) and all twenty-one examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — Real `Change` EventKind + Two-Way Binding Sugar (§16.7) ✅
- Step 1: `EventKind` gains `Change`, dispatched by `Checkbox`/`Slider` on a real, genuine edit (a toggle, a drag release) — ✅ (`EventKind::Change`/`DispatchOutcome::Changed(NodeId)`, fired two ways per Design Principle 6: mechanically inside `Tree::dispatch`'s `PointerReleased` arm for a real Slider drag-release, checked before `self.dragging` clears; directly by new `Node.set_checked` via `call_handler` (widened to `pub(crate)`) for a Checkbox, since `engine-core` never touches `checked` itself. New `Node.get_checked`/`Node.set_on_change`. New `engine-core` tests (`dispatch_release_ending_a_real_slider_drag_produces_changed`, `dispatch_release_with_no_slider_drag_in_progress_never_produces_changed`) prove the mechanical path; new `tests/test_change_event.py` (5 tests) proves the direct path and FFI wiring.)
- Step 2: `engine-spec`'s `_attach` (§16.2) gains real `two_way: true` binding sugar (§16.7) for `Checkbox`/`Slider`'s own natural edit event — a one-way binding plus an auto-generated `Change` handler that writes the new value back into the bound `Signal`, no new dispatch path — closing the milestone (`TextField` explicitly out of scope, per this milestone's own status line) — ✅ (real, additional scope: `WidgetSpec.checked`/`value`/`two_way` + `NodeKindSpec::Checkbox`/`Slider` make them declarable in `view.yaml` for the first time, needed since two-way binding is a `View`-only concept. `apply_binding_value` gains a `Value::Bool` branch calling `set_checked`; new `TwoWayCallback` pyclass registers as the widget's own `Change` handler, reading the node's value back and writing it into the bound `Signal`. **Real finding #1:** the two-way expression must be a Signal's own `.get()` call (`Expression::Call(Expression::Ident(name), "get")`), not a bare identifier as ARCHITECTURE.md's own illustration shows — a bare identifier resolves to the raw `Signal` object itself, which `apply_binding_value` can't apply forward at all; only caught once the round trip was actually tested. **Real finding #2:** with #1 fixed, a real round trip recursed infinitely (`set_checked` → `Change` → `TwoWayCallback` → `Signal.set` → unconditional notify → the widget's own forward binding → `set_checked` again → ...), each level silently caught and printed by the pre-existing non-fatal-handler policy — only caught by actually running `examples/two_way_binding.py` end to end, not by any unit test. Fixed at the general root cause: `Signal.set`/`Signal.update` now skip notification when the value is unchanged. New `tests/test_two_way_binding.py` (5 tests, incl. a dedicated stderr-must-stay-clean regression test) + 2 new `Signal`-only tests in `tests/test_view_binding.py`; new `examples/two_way_binding.py` + `two_way_binding.yaml`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 94, up from 92; `engine-spec` 33, up from 29); pytest (131 passed, up from 119, 1 skipped) and all twenty-two examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

scope-simplifying finding:** `parley = "0.11.1"` (also pinned, confirmed via direct source read) ships a real, purpose-built `editing` module (`parley::editing::{Cursor, Selection}`) with `from_point`/`from_byte_index`, `next_visual`/`previous_visual`/`line_start`/`line_end` (each with a real `extend: bool` for shift-selection), and `geometry`/`geometry_with` for real caret/highlight rects — the hard visual/BiDi-aware cursor math already exists, not hand-rolled from scratch as it looked at first glance. **Real, stated scope boundary this still leaves:** `engine-core` has no `parley` dependency (§4's crate-boundary rule, unbroken since M3) and gaining one just for this would violate it, so `TextFieldState`'s own real cursor/selection *mutation* (Left/Right/Home/End/Backspace/Delete) is scoped as plain UTF-8 char-boundary logic inside `engine-core` — correct for LTR text (what every real `TextField` use case in this codebase's own examples needs: usernames, search boxes, form fields), not grapheme-cluster- or BiDi-visual-order-aware the way `parley::editing::Selection` itself is. `engine-render` only reconstructs a `Layout`/`Selection` from that plain state each frame, purely to compute real caret/highlight paint geometry — the same "engine-core holds inert data, engine-render re-derives it" split `TextState` (§14 step 4) already established, reused rather than invented. **Deliberately excludes mouse click-to-position and mouse-drag selection**: real positioning needs the actual per-glyph shaping data that only exists inside `engine-render`'s own per-frame render loop, and reaching it cleanly from `Tree::dispatch` (which has zero `engine-render`/`parley` visibility, by design) is real, separate architectural work, not sketched anywhere yet — a further, real, un-scoped candidate beyond even M17. **Deliberately excludes clipboard and IME**, promoted instead to their own M17 (following this milestone).

### Phase 1 — Real `TextField` State & Paint (§5, §16.7) ✅
- Step 1: `NodeKind::TextField(TextFieldState)` with real, plain byte-offset state (`content: String`, `cursor: usize`, `selection_anchor: Option<usize>`) and real paint — text rendered through the existing `TextRenderer`/`parley` pipeline (`crates/engine-render/src/text.rs`) reused as-is, plus a real caret line and real selection-highlight rect computed each frame from a `parley::editing::Cursor`/`Selection` reconstructed from the plain state, purely for painting. Real keyboard-focus integration (§10's already-real `Tab`/focus model) makes a `TextField` focusable; no editing yet — this phase proves the visual/state scaffolding, the same "state defined, paint working" shape `Checkbox`/`Slider` Phase 1 each already established — ✅ (`TextFieldState::new` seeds `cursor` at `content.len()`. New `TextRenderer::draw_field` builds one shared `parley::Layout` and paints a real selection-highlight rect, then glyphs, then a real caret on top (via `Cursor`/`Selection::geometry`), gated on the live `tree.focused() == Some(id)` — real finding: `InteractionState.focus_ring` is ticked but never painted anywhere, so this reuses §10's real focus state directly rather than inventing a second concept. `paint_node` gains a `TextField` arm painting a real box fill first (unlike `NodeKind::Text`, which has no separate box). New `Window.add_text_field` — real finding: the first real `engine-py` caller of `Tree::set_access` anywhere (`Role::TextInput` + `Action::Focus`), since every other `add_*` method left a node at `Role::Unknown`. New `Node.get_text()`/`Node.is_focused()` (the latter a real, missing, generically-useful capability — no Python-facing way to read live focus existed at all before this). New `engine-core` tests (`TextFieldState::new`'s own seed; `build_access_update`'s real `Role`/`value`/`Action::Focus`); new `engine-render/tests/text_field_paint.rs` (2 pixel tests: unfocused shows only the fill, focused paints a real visible caret) — both passed on the first run. New `tests/test_text_field.py` (7 tests, incl. a real Tab reaching the field and a second Tab moving focus away) + new `examples/text_field.py`. Full suite/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (`engine-core` 96, up from 94, +2 pixel tests); pytest (138 passed, up from 131, 1 skipped) and all twenty-three examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real Keyboard-Driven Editing (§8, §10) ✅
- Step 1: `winit::event::KeyEvent.text` reaches `engine-platform::translate_key`/a real `InputEvent` variant for the first time (currently discarded); `Tree::dispatch`'s keyboard arm inserts produced characters into a focused `TextField`'s own `content` at `cursor`, and real Backspace/Delete/Left/Right/Home/End (UTF-8 char-boundary aware via `char_indices`, not parley's own grapheme-cluster movement — the real, stated scope boundary above) mutate `content`/`cursor` directly inside `engine-core`, mirroring `set_slider_position`'s own "engine-core owns the real mutation" shape. `EventKind::Change` (M14 Phase 3) fires on every real edit, reusing the exact existing mechanism, no new dispatch path. New `Window.add_text_field`/`Node.get_text`/`Node.set_text` — ✅ (`Key` gains `Backspace`/`Delete`/`ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight`/`Home`/`End`; `InputEvent` gains `TextInput(String)` (dropped `Copy`, kept `Clone` — fixed the one real call site that broke, `app.rs`'s own `on_input` closure). **Real design conflict found while designing:** `NamedKey::Space` is matched before any `TextInput` fallback could see it, but `Key::Space` already means "activate" — resolved via a new private `dispatch_text_field_key`, checked first when a `TextField` is focused, claiming Enter/Space/Backspace/Delete/arrows/Home/End (Tab/Escape still fall through to the generic handling). `Change` fires only on a real content edit, never on pure cursor movement or a genuine no-op. New `Node.set_text` mirrors `set_checked` exactly, including always firing `Change` (already protected against a feedback loop by M14 Phase 3's own `Signal` fix). `Window.press_key` widened; new `Window.type_text`. New `engine-core` tests (12, all passed first run, incl. a real multi-byte UTF-8 character removed whole); `tests/test_text_field.py` gained 9 more (16 total). Updated `examples/text_field.py` with real typing/navigation/deletion. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 108, up from 96); pytest (147 passed, up from 138, 1 skipped) and all twenty-three examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — Selection & Two-Way Binding (§16.7) ✅
- Step 1: Shift+arrow selection extension (keyboard-only, the same char-boundary logic Phase 2 established) and a real Backspace/Delete-with-selection (deletes the selected range, not just one character) — ✅ (`dispatch_text_field_key` widened to take `shift`; without it, a real active selection collapses to its own near edge instead of moving one more char — real desktop-editor behavior. New shared `Tree::delete_selection`, called first by `Backspace`/`Delete`/`Space`/`TextInput`, replacing an active selection; a zero-width one is treated as none. 9 new `engine-core` tests, all passed first run.)
- Step 2: `NodeKindSpec::TextField` + `WidgetSpec` fields making `TextField` declarable in `view.yaml` for the first time (mirroring M14 Phase 3's own real `Checkbox`/`Slider` extension), and real `two_way: text` binding sugar wiring `TextField`'s own `Change` into `View._attach`'s existing `TwoWayCallback` mechanism, no new binding-resolution path — closing the milestone — ✅ (real finding: `WidgetSpec.text: Option<TextSpec>` already existed for `kind: Text`, and its four fields are byte-for-byte what `TextFieldState` needs, so `kind: TextField` reuses that block instead of a third near-duplicate field. `apply_binding_value` gains a `Value::Str` branch calling `set_text`; `TwoWayCallback` gains a `"text"` branch calling `get_text` — both mirroring the existing `"checked"` shape. 4 new `engine-spec` tests (one fixed after a real counting mistake caught by running it). New `tests/test_two_way_binding.py`/`test_text_field.py` coverage for the real round trip and shift+arrow selection editing. Extended the existing `examples/two_way_binding.py`/`.yaml` with a real `TextField`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 117, up from 108; `engine-spec` 37, up from 33); pytest (150 passed, up from 147, 1 skipped) and all twenty-three examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 16 — Structured Logging via `tracing` (§3, §9)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** Scoped together with M15/M17 at the user's own explicit request; see M15's own status line for the full candidate context — investigated and presented but not chosen for either of M13/M14's two slots or M15/M17's TextField-adjacent pair, chosen here on its own. Confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `ARCHITECTURE.md` §3's own Technology Stack table names `tracing` outright ("Structured logs, correlate with frame timing"), and §9's own text is explicit — "a raised Python exception is caught, its full traceback logged via `tracing::error!` (§3), and the render loop continues" — but `crates/engine-py/src/dispatch.rs::call_handler` (confirmed via direct read) actually calls `err.print(py)`, plain `PyErr::print` to stderr, not `tracing::error!`; `crates/engine-py/src/app.rs` has two more real, confirmed `eprintln!` sites (no-GPU-adapter and no-display exit paths) with the identical gap. No `tracing`/`tracing-subscriber` dependency exists anywhere in the workspace, confirmed via grep across every `Cargo.toml`. This is real infrastructure work, not a new user-visible feature — `PyErr::print`'s own real value (the *full* CPython-style traceback, not a one-line message) must survive the migration, not be lost for the sake of "using `tracing`."

### Phase 1 — Real `tracing` Subscriber Wiring (§3, §9) ✅
- Step 1: Add `tracing`/`tracing-subscriber` to the workspace; wire a real subscriber once, at `App::run`'s own real entry point (`engine-py::app.rs`) — the one place every per-frame/callback code path already funnels through — establishing the real span/event vocabulary this codebase will use going forward (a span per `App::run` invocation at minimum) — ✅ (real finding: `tracing` was already a real transitive dependency via `winit`/`accesskit_unix`/`zbus`; `tracing-subscriber`'s `env-filter` feature enabled explicitly for real `RUST_LOG` support. `App::run` calls `tracing_subscriber::fmt::try_init().ok()` at its own top — `try_init`, not `init`, is load-bearing since `App.run()` can legitimately be called more than once per process. One real span, `app_run`, established. New pytest test proves a second real `.run()` call doesn't panic; manually confirmed `RUST_LOG=info` genuinely emits real events (including from `wgpu_hal`) while the default stays quiet. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; pytest (151 passed, up from 150, 1 skipped) and all twenty-three examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Migrate Real Call Sites to `tracing` (§9) ✅
- Step 1: `dispatch.rs::call_handler`'s `err.print(py)` and `app.rs`'s two `eprintln!` sites become real `tracing::error!`/`tracing::warn!` events, preserving the *full* real traceback text `PyErr::print` already gives (not losing detail for the sake of migrating) — verified with a real test capturing emitted `tracing` events (not just "stderr has some text"), proving a raising handler now emits a real structured event — closing the milestone — ✅ (also migrated `run_completions`'s own real third call site, not named in the original scoping. Four real findings along the way, none caught by unit tests alone: `capsys` can't see Rust's own raw stderr writes at all, fixed by switching four affected tests to `capfd`; `App::run` alone isn't the guaranteed subscriber-install point, fixed with a shared `ensure_tracing_subscriber()` also called from the real logging call site itself; the builder chain's own default writer is stdout, fixed with an explicit `.with_writer(std::io::stderr)`; that same builder switch silently dropped `RUST_LOG` support, fixed with an explicit `EnvFilter::from_default_env()`. New `tests/test_tracing.py` (2 real subprocess-based tests, the only valid way to test a once-per-process subscriber's own filter). Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; pytest (153 passed, up from 151, 1 skipped) and all twenty-three examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 17 — Clipboard & IME for TextField (§8)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** Scoped together with M15/M16 at the user's own explicit request, following M15 (`TextField`'s own core keyboard-editing milestone) the same way M14 Phase 3 followed M14 Phase 1/2 — a real, promoted slice of scope M15's own status line explicitly defers, given its own full milestone rather than left as a bare deferred-scope note. Two real, distinct capabilities, confirmed unimplemented via grep (zero hits for `arboard`/`clipboard`/`Ime` in any `Cargo.toml` or `engine-platform`/`engine-core` source): real clipboard copy/cut/paste for a focused `TextField`'s own selection, and real IME composition preview — `winit::event::Ime::{Enabled, Preedit(String, Option<(usize, usize)>), Commit(String), Disabled}` is real and pinned (confirmed via direct source read of `winit = "0.30.13"`) but nothing in `engine-platform`'s own `WindowEvent` translation reached it at all before this milestone. Both depended on M15 actually existing first (a real `TextField` to paste into / compose text in) — genuinely sequenced, not just presented in this order. Phase 2's own real, load-bearing finding (`Window::set_ime_allowed` defaulting to `false`, confirmed via direct source read) would have silently made the whole IME feature never fire at all had it gone unnoticed — see that phase's own step detail below.

### Phase 1 — Real Clipboard Copy/Cut/Paste (§8) ✅
- Step 1: A real clipboard crate (candidate: `arboard`, needs verifying it actually builds and runs against this dev/CI environment's own real display server before committing to it — a genuine, stated unknown, the same "verify at implementation time" discipline `winit::window::Theme`'s own unsupported-on-this-X11-session finding already established for a comparable platform capability) wired to Ctrl+C/Ctrl+X/Ctrl+V on a focused `TextField`'s own real selection — ✅ (confirmed real via a throwaway probe, kept as a real permanent regression test. Real finding: Ctrl+C previously inserted a literal "c" — `winit`'s own `logical_key` is Ctrl-blind — fixed by checking `control_key()` before the `TextInput` fallback. New `InputEvent::Copy`/`Cut`/`PasteRequested` (true no-ops in `Tree::dispatch`, real work in `engine-py`'s own raw-event handling); new `Tree::text_field_selected_text`/`cut_text_field_selection`; Paste reuses the existing `TextInput` mechanism. Real gap found while testing: a direct `cut_text_field_selection` call never fired `Change` the way dispatched edits do — fixed with an explicit `call_handler` call in both `Window.cut()` and the real winit path. A real Cut writes to the clipboard before deleting, only deleting once that write succeeds. New `Window.copy()`/`cut()`/`paste()` are deliberately hermetic (no synthetic way exists to inject a real Ctrl-modified OS keystroke). New tests: `engine-core` (5), `engine-platform` (3), a real permanent clipboard round-trip test, `tests/test_clipboard.py` (8). New `examples/clipboard.py`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 122, up from 117; `engine-platform` 9, up from 6; `engine-py` 1, new); pytest (161 passed, up from 153, 1 skipped) and all twenty-four examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real IME Composition Preview (§8) ✅
- Step 1: `winit::event::Ime::{Preedit, Commit}` reach a new real `InputEvent` variant through `engine-platform`, rendering a real preedit underline at the cursor and committing real text via Phase-2-of-M15's own existing character-insertion mechanism on `Commit` — closing the milestone — ✅ (**real, load-bearing finding, confirmed via direct source read, that would have silently dead-ended the whole feature:** `winit::window::Window::set_ime_allowed`'s own doc comment states plainly "IME is not allowed by default" — without calling it, `WindowEvent::Ime` never fires at all; fixed by calling it once, at the one real window-creation site. Same doc comment: "during the preedit phase the window will NOT get `KeyboardInput` events" — composing and plain typing are already mutually exclusive at the `winit` level. `TextFieldState` gains `preedit: Option<String>` — purely visual, uncommitted, never spliced into real `content`. `Ime::Preedit(text, _)` (dropping `winit`'s own sub-cursor-range detail, a real, deliberate scope narrowing) becomes a new `InputEvent::ImePreedit(String)` (empty string means "cleared," matching `winit`'s own real convention); `Ime::Commit(text)` reaches the existing `InputEvent::TextInput(text)` directly — no new variant needed, it's the identical mechanism a plain keypress already uses. `Tree::dispatch`'s new `ImePreedit` arm sets/clears the focused field's own `preedit`, always `DispatchOutcome::None`; the existing `TextInput` arm additionally clears a stale `preedit` on every real insertion, defensive. `TextRenderer::draw_field` splices `preedit` into a display-only copy of the content at `cursor` when composing (`content` itself never mutated), reusing the same `Selection::geometry` mechanism the selection highlight already established (M15 Phase 1) to paint a real underline under that span; the caret shows at the end of the spliced-in preedit while composing. Confirmed, not assumed: `engine-py::app.rs`'s `on_input` closure already calls `tree.dispatch(root, event.clone(), ...)` unconditionally, so no additional handling was needed there at all. Honest deviation from `PLAN.md`: no dedicated `engine-platform` translation test was added, the inline `Ime` match being too trivial to warrant isolating, matching `ThemeChanged`'s own precedent. New `engine-core` tests (4) + new `engine-render/tests/text_field_paint.rs` pixel tests (2): a composing field's render differs from an otherwise pixel-identical non-composing field; an empty-string preedit paints identically to `None`. Confirmed, not assumed: no new Python-facing FFI surface or pytest coverage was needed — a real IME event only ever originates from an actual OS input method, the same scope boundary M17 Phase 1 established for Ctrl+C/X/V. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 126, up from 122; `engine-render` 4 in `text_field_paint.rs`, up from 2); pytest (161 passed, unchanged, 1 skipped) and all twenty-four examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

## Milestone 18 — TextField Mouse Interaction (§8, §10, §11.9, §11.10)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** Scoped together with M19 at the user's own explicit request ("scope the next milestone"), chosen alongside declarative-authoring completeness rather than instead of it — the user chose "both, scoped together," matching the M15/M16/M17 precedent. The single most-repeatedly-named deferred gap in this whole file: M15's own status line and M17's own scoping paragraph both explicitly called mouse click-to-position/drag-selection "a further, real, un-scoped candidate beyond even M17" — re-confirmed real today via a fresh grep, zero pointer-driven cursor/selection code exists anywhere in `Tree::dispatch`. Confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: `engine-core` has zero `engine-render`/`parley` visibility by design (§4's crate-boundary rule, unbroken since M3), so `Tree::dispatch` cannot itself turn a click point into a byte offset — the hard part was never "is this possible," it's "which crate does the real hit-test math, and how does its answer reach `Tree`." `GpuState.text_renderer: TextRenderer` (`engine-py::app.rs`) is real and already reachable per-window, but `on_input`'s own `runtimes_for_input.borrow()` is an immutable `RefCell::borrow()` — a real hit-test call needs `&mut self.font_cx`/`&mut self.layout_cx`, the same as `draw_field` already requires, so this phase needs a real, small borrow-shape change, left for its own implementation rather than pre-decided here. Precedent: M17 Phase 1's `text_field_selected_text`/`cut_text_field_selection` already established "`engine-py` calls a plain `Tree` method directly, bypassing `Tree::dispatch`'s `DispatchOutcome` set, for a capability `engine-core` structurally can't own" — the real shape this milestone plausibly follows, not `NodeKind::Canvas`'s own custom-hit-test-callback precedent (§11.10), which is a different shape (an app-supplied closure, not a live per-frame glyph-shaping computation).

### Phase 1 — Real Click-to-Position (§8, §10, §11.9, §11.10) ✅
- Step 1: A real pointer click landing inside a focused `TextField` computes the nearest real character boundary at that point (via `parley::editing::Cursor::from_point`, already confirmed real and pinned at M15's own scoping — `crates/engine-render/src/text.rs` already builds the identical `Layout` this needs, reused rather than duplicated) and moves the field's own `cursor` there, clearing any active selection — the same real "click somewhere in a text field, the caret jumps there" behavior every desktop text editor has, currently entirely absent — ✅ (**real, bigger-than-scoped finding:** `Tree::dispatch`'s own `PointerPressed` arm never touched `self.focused` at all before this phase — focus was Tab-driven or explicit only. Fixed by adding real click-to-focus scoped specifically to `TextField`, reusing `set_focus_to` verbatim. New `Tree::hit_test_local` (widening `hit_test_at`'s own internal return type from `Option<NodeId>` to `Option<(NodeId, Point)>`) exposes the click's own already-transform-aware local point, previously computed internally but discarded. New `Tree::set_text_field_cursor` clamps to a real char boundary and clears any selection. New `engine-render::TextRenderer::hit_test_position` (built on an extracted `build_field_layout` helper shared with `draw_field`, so a hit-test can never drift from what was painted) hit-tests against `state.content` alone, deliberately ignoring an active IME preedit — a stated scope simplification. `engine-py::app.rs`'s `on_input` closure widened its one `RefCell::borrow()` to `borrow_mut()` to reach `TextRenderer` mutably, confirmed safe. **Real, pleasant scope-simplifying finding, deviating from `PLAN.md`:** no new `Window.click_at`-style FFI entry point was needed — the existing `Window.click(node)` (M4 Phase 1 step 3) already dispatches a real press/release pair through `Tree::dispatch`, reaching the new click-to-focus path for free; byte-offset positioning itself has no hermetic Python surface at all, since `Window` owns no `TextRenderer`, matching the same real scope boundary M17 Phase 1/2 established for clipboard/IME. New tests: `engine-core` (7), `engine-render` (3, pure unit tests, no GPU needed), `tests/test_text_field.py` (2). Updated `examples/text_field.py` with a real `Window.click(field)` demonstration. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 133, up from 126; `engine-render` 7 in `text_field_paint.rs`, up from 4); pytest (163 passed, up from 161, 1 skipped) and all twenty-four examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real Drag-to-Select (§8, §10) ✅
- Step 1: A real press-and-drag starting inside a focused `TextField` extends a real selection as the pointer moves, reusing the exact `selection_anchor`/`cursor` pair keyboard shift-selection already established (M15 Phase 3) — closing the milestone. Needs live per-frame glyph positions during the drag, which `engine-core` structurally can't hold (§4) — the real drag-tracking state machine's own home (`engine-core`'s existing `self.dragging` mechanism vs. new `engine-py`-side per-window state) is a genuine open design question, left for this phase's own investigation, not pre-decided here — ✅ (**the open design question, answered:** `self.dragging`/`update_drag` (Splitter/Slider) is pure geometry inside `engine-core`, but text drag-selection needs the exact real per-glyph hit-test Phase 1 established only `engine-render` can do — the drag-tracking state machine had to live in `engine-py` instead, a new plain `WindowRuntime.text_drag: Option<NodeId>` field. New `Tree::extend_text_field_selection` (built on a factored-out `char_boundary` helper shared with `set_text_field_cursor`) seeds `selection_anchor` at the current cursor only on the first move, then keeps extending — a genuinely different operation from a plain click's collapse-to-cursor. `app.rs`'s `on_input` closure gained a new `PointerMoved` arm (previously unmatched anywhere, falling to `_ => {}`) and a shared `text_field_hit_offset` helper factoring out Phase 1's own inline hit-test logic, reused by both `PointerPressed` and the new arm. **Real, deliberate, stated scope boundary:** a drag that leaves the field's own bounds mid-drag pauses the selection update until the pointer re-enters, rather than clamping to the nearest edge the way some real desktop editors do — a further, real, un-scoped refinement. **Confirmed, not assumed:** no hermetic Python-facing test surface exists for this at all — `Window` owns no `TextRenderer`, the same real scope boundary click-to-position/clipboard/IME already established; `pytest`'s own count stayed exactly unchanged, confirming it. New `engine-core` tests (4): the anchor seeds on first move and never moves again on repeated extends; clamping; a true no-op on a non-`TextField`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 137, up from 133); pytest (163 passed, unchanged, 1 skipped) and all twenty-four examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

**M18 — TextField Mouse Interaction is now fully complete.** Both phases (real click-to-position, real drag-to-select) closed the single most-repeatedly-named deferred gap this whole file carried forward from M15's own status line — a `TextField` now has real mouse interaction alongside its existing real keyboard-driven editing (M15), matching every other real desktop text field's own basic behavior. Two real, bigger-than-scoped findings caught along the way: `PointerPressed` never touched real focus for *any* node kind before Phase 1 (fixed, scoped to `TextField` only); the real drag-tracking state machine couldn't live inside `engine-core` at all (§4), settling the open design question Phase 1 explicitly left for Phase 2 to answer.

## Milestone 19 — Declarative Authoring Completeness: Hot-Reload & Composition (§16.4, §16.6)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** Scoped together with M18 at the user's own explicit request; see M18's own status line for the full two-candidate context. Confirmed real by direct investigation, not assumed: both capabilities are fully specified in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §16 but confirmed unwired — the same "built but disconnected" shape M7 (MD3 Visual Fidelity) had before it was built. `engine-spec::watch::ViewWatcher` wraps a real, tested `notify::RecommendedWatcher` (`watcher_detects_a_real_write_to_the_watched_file` already passes), but its own module doc comment states plainly that wiring its `poll_changed()` into a real running `App`'s frame loop "is deliberately deferred." `engine-spec::reconcile::Reconciler` is likewise real and unit-tested (keyed diffing by `id` + `NodeKind`, preserving `NodeId`/focus/in-flight animations across a reload, confirmed via direct read of its own tests), but confirmed via direct read of `engine-py::view.rs`, `View`'s own `#[pymethods]` have no `reload`/`reconcile` method at all — not even a manual, app-triggered reload exists yet; `View::new` calls `Reconciler::load` exactly once, at construction, never again. `include:` composition (§16.6) is equally real and unimplemented — confirmed via grep, `WidgetSpec` (`engine-spec::spec.rs`) has no `include` field anywhere — and carries real, stated security requirements in ARCHITECTURE.md's own text (path confinement against `../` escaping the view directory, cycle detection, a depth limit) that don't exist today since the feature itself doesn't, the same untrusted-input discipline §8 already established for every other user-supplied structure this engine parses.

### Phase 1 — Real Hot-Reload Wiring (§16.4) ✅
- Step 1: `ViewWatcher` reaches a real, live `View`/`App` for the first time — watching the loaded `view.yaml`, polling for changes on the main thread between frames (mirroring `ViewWatcher`'s own doc comment: "the caller's own frame loop calls `poll_changed()` once per tick"), and calling `Reconciler::reconcile` on a real change to patch the live `Tree` in place, preserving focus/scroll/in-flight animation for unchanged nodes exactly as `Reconciler`'s own existing tests already prove in isolation — a real, first Python-facing entry point for reconciliation, not just an internal `engine-spec` primitive — ✅ (**real, significant, previously-unregistered finding:** `View` has no live-window/render-loop concept of its own at all — confirmed via direct read of `view.rs`'s own module doc comment and, decisively, `examples/two_way_binding.py`'s own docstring ("there's no `App.run()` here"); `Window`/`App` never reference `View` at all, confirmed via grep. "Wire `ViewWatcher` into a real running `App`'s frame loop," the scoping paragraph's own literal words, wasn't literally buildable — no such frame loop exists to hook into. The honest translation: new `View.poll_reload() -> PyResult<bool>`, a real, explicit method the caller invokes, matching §16.4's own "between frames" intent as closely as `View`'s real, stated architecture allows. `View` also gained a remembered `path: String` (previously discarded after the initial read) and `watcher: Option<ViewWatcher>` (non-fatal `None` on an unusual filesystem, matching the no-GPU/no-display precedent). **Real, stated scope boundary:** patches structure/paint/layout only — `bindings:`/`handlers:`/`two_way:` stay `_attach`'s own separate responsibility, since re-running it automatically risked double-subscribing a `Signal` and needed remembering a `viewmodel` this phase didn't take on. New `tests/test_hot_reload.py` (3 tests): no change before any write; a real edit reaches the live `Tree`; an unchanged widget keeps its real `NodeId` across a reload (mutated pre-reload, read back fresh post-reload) — the real end-to-end proof of `Reconciler`'s own documented claim, reached through the Python FFI surface for the first time. New `examples/hot_reload.py` + `hot_reload.yaml`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean, no new Rust-level tests needed (this phase's real coverage is at the FFI/integration level); pytest (166 passed, up from 163, 1 skipped) and all twenty-five examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real View Composition via `include:` (§16.6) ✅
- Step 1: `WidgetSpec` gains a real `include:` field; loading expands each included file's own `WidgetSpec` tree in place, before validation, as ordinary children indistinguishable from inline ones — with real path confinement (no `../` escaping the view directory), cycle detection (a file cannot transitively include itself), and a depth limit, the same real guards ARCHITECTURE.md's own §16.6 text names explicitly — closing the milestone — ✅ (`WidgetSpec`'s own `deny_unknown_fields` + required `id`/`kind` meant a bare `{include: path}` mapping can't deserialize into it directly — resolved by expanding `include:` on the *raw* `serde_yaml_ng::Value` tree first (new `engine-spec::include` module, mirroring `watch.rs`'s own one-module-per-capability precedent), then deserializing the fully-expanded result — `WidgetSpec`/`build.rs`/`reconcile.rs` stay completely unchanged, and validation genuinely runs on the final tree, exactly per §16.6's own text. New `parse_view_with_includes(yaml, base_dir: Option<&Path>)`; path confinement via `Path::canonicalize` (resolves symlinks too, a real not superficial check); cycle detection via a `visited` stack; `MAX_INCLUDE_DEPTH = 8`. `Reconciler::load`/`reconcile` widened with a third `base_dir: Option<&Path>` parameter, matching the existing `sheet`/`scheme` precedent exactly — 9 existing call sites updated. **Real, additional fix caught while wiring `View::new`, not by a test:** a second, separate `parse_view(&yaml)` call there (collecting `bindings`/`declared_handlers`/`two_way`) also needed to become include-aware — otherwise a binding or handler declared inside an *included* file would never be collected at all, a real, silent gap that would have shipped alongside the main feature. New `engine-spec::include` tests (9): a real two-file splice; nested includes resolve relative to their own file; no `base_dir` fails clearly; an absolute path, a real `../` escape, a self-include cycle, and the depth limit are all rejected; `include:` alongside another key is rejected; content with no `include:` parses byte-for-byte identically to plain `parse_view`. New `tests/test_view_composition.py` (2 tests) + `examples/view_composition.py`/`.yaml`/`confirm_dialog.yaml`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-spec` 46, up from 37); pytest (168 passed, up from 166, 1 skipped) and all twenty-seven examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

**M19 — Declarative Authoring Completeness is now fully complete.** Both phases (real hot-reload wiring, real view composition via `include:`) closed §16.4/§16.6's own "built but disconnected" gap — `ViewWatcher`/`Reconciler` were both already real, tested `engine-spec` primitives with zero wiring into anything live; a real `View.poll_reload()` and a real `include:` field now reach them through the actual Python FFI surface for the first time. The single most significant finding across both phases: `View` has no live-window/render-loop concept of its own at all, confirmed via its own module doc comment and `examples/two_way_binding.py`'s own explicit docstring — the scoping paragraph's own literal words ("wire into a real running `App`'s frame loop") weren't literally buildable, since no such frame loop exists; `poll_reload`'s real, explicit, caller-invoked shape is the honest translation of that intent given `View`'s actual, pre-existing architecture.

**Up next:** M20 Phase 1 — real Checkbox/Slider component color theming (§7.1, §7.3), the first of two milestones scoped together at the user's own explicit request ("scope the next milestone"), presented as two real candidates found by investigating the *current live source*, not just this file's own history (every previously-named TextField/mouse-interaction gap had already been closed by M18). `engine-render`'s own current source (`lib.rs`/`text.rs`) still carries three separate, explicit "real but not yet theme-aware" comments, each naming a genuinely hardcoded color: `Checkbox`'s checkmark (`Color::from_rgba8(0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF)`, M14 Phase 1), `Slider`'s track (`Color::from_rgba8(0x79, 0x74, 0x7A, 0xFF)`, M14 Phase 2), and `TextField`'s text/caret/selection (a fixed `0x1C1B1F` literal computed in `paint_node`'s own `TextField` arm, M15 Phase 1) — confirmed real via direct source read, not assumed from the comments alone. M7 Phase 3 already built the real mechanism for exactly this class of problem (`InteractionState.tint: Color`, resolved once by `Window.set_theme` and pushed into every opted-in node via `Tree::set_all_interaction_tints`) for ripple/hover only — this milestone extends the identical, already-proven mechanism to these three components' own remaining hardcoded colors. The user chose both this and real wheel packaging, scoped together the same way M15/M16/M17 and M18/M19 were.

**Real finding during M20's own investigation, before any code:** `Slider`'s own thumb already paints with the node's own real, per-node `PaintProperties.background` (already themeable by an app that sets it directly) — only the fixed-gray *track* needs a real fix, narrowing this milestone's own Slider-side scope. A real, deliberate, bounded design choice: this milestone reuses the exact same already-resolved "on-surface" role `set_theme` already computes for ripple/hover, rather than resolving a second, more specific MD3 role per component (e.g. "on-primary" for a checkmark against a filled, primary-colored box) — inventing per-component role resolution is real, separate, larger design work, matching this codebase's own repeated "wire exactly what's needed today, not ahead of a confirmed need" precedent (the identical calibration `InteractionState.tint` itself was scoped with at M7 Phase 3). Each new field (`CheckboxState.mark_tint`/`SliderState.track_tint`/`TextFieldState.text_tint`) defaults to the exact historical hardcoded literal it replaces, so a node whose app never calls `set_theme` sees zero visual change — the same real, byte-for-byte-identical-default discipline `InteractionState.tint` already established.

**Real finding during M21's own investigation, before any code:** ARCHITECTURE.md's own §13 Packaging section carries unusually detailed, prescriptive real-world guidance, not just a placeholder note — a direct account of a genuine TRE v1 production bug: `maturin`'s default `--auditwheel repair` behavior vendors system libraries into the wheel, renaming their sonames, and TRE v1's own built wheel ended up with a second, renamed copy of `libxkbcommon`/GTK alongside the system copies `winit`/GTK `dlopen` at runtime — a double-loaded-library segfault only caught via `LD_DEBUG=libs` under CI's own exact conditions, invisible to a plain `maturin develop`. This project links the identical class of libraries (`winit`, `accesskit_winit`, `arboard`) — confirmed via direct read of `pyproject.toml`'s own `[tool.maturin]` section, which has no `auditwheel`/`manylinux` configuration of any kind yet, the same real, live exposure ARCHITECTURE.md's own text warns about. ARCHITECTURE.md's own text states the real decision point explicitly: "a manylinux-repaired portable wheel (test the *exact* built artifact end-to-end... before trusting it) or a system-linked wheel for known targets (`--auditwheel skip --compatibility linux`)... don't let the default silently decide this for you." The full cross-platform packaging matrix was explicitly deferred "until the API surface has stabilized past the spike stage" — a real, judgment-based scoping question at M20/M21's own scoping time, now nineteen completed milestones past that spike stage.

## Milestone 20 — MD3 Component Color Theming (§7.1, §7.3)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** Scoped together with M21 at the user's own explicit request ("scope the next milestone"). Found by investigating the *current live source* directly, not this file's own history — every previously-named TextField/mouse-interaction gap had already been closed by M18. `engine-render`'s own source (`lib.rs`/`text.rs`) still carries three separate, explicit "real but not yet theme-aware" comments, each naming a genuinely hardcoded color: `Checkbox`'s checkmark (`Color::from_rgba8(0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF)`, M14 Phase 1), `Slider`'s track (`Color::from_rgba8(0x79, 0x74, 0x7A, 0xFF)`, M14 Phase 2), and `TextField`'s text/caret/selection (a fixed `0x1C1B1F` literal in `paint_node`'s own `TextField` arm, M15 Phase 1) — confirmed real via direct source read. M7 Phase 3 already built the real mechanism for exactly this class of problem (`InteractionState.tint: Color`, resolved once by `Window.set_theme` via `DynamicTheme::from_seed`/`ThemeState.on_surface()`, pushed into every opted-in node via `Tree::set_all_interaction_tints`) for ripple/hover only — this milestone extends the identical, already-proven mechanism to these three components' own remaining hardcoded colors, reusing the same already-resolved "on-surface" role rather than resolving a second, more specific MD3 role per component (e.g. "on-primary" for a checkmark) — real, separate, larger design work this milestone deliberately doesn't take on. `Slider`'s own thumb already paints with the node's own real, per-node `background` (already themeable directly) — only the track needs fixing.

**Note on continuity:** starting with this milestone, the session driving `BUILD_TRACKER.md` is operating under a standing, user-granted autonomous-development authorization (the user is away and unreachable) — push after every milestone, and keep investigating/scoping/building the backend's own real remaining gaps indefinitely without further per-milestone confirmation. Applies from M20 onward; every prior milestone in this file was scoped and built with the user present and asking after each one.

### Phase 1 — Real Checkbox/Slider Component Theming (§7.1, §7.3) ✅
- Step 1: `CheckboxState` gains `mark_tint: Color` (default the exact historical white literal), `SliderState` gains `track_tint: Color` (default the exact historical gray literal) — both threaded through `paint_node`'s existing hardcoded-literal paint sites in place of the literals, and pushed a real resolved color by a widened `Tree::set_all_interaction_tints`-style mechanism called from `Window.set_theme`. A node whose app never calls `set_theme` must see zero visual change from today — the same real, byte-for-byte-identical-default discipline `InteractionState.tint` already established at M7 Phase 3 — ✅ (New, separate `Tree::set_all_component_tints`, not a widened `set_all_interaction_tints` — `mark_tint`/`track_tint` are plain fields every real `CheckboxState`/`SliderState` always has, not an optional opt-in capability the way `InteractionState` is, so conflating the two would change an already-tested method's own documented behavior. **Real bug caught before it shipped, not by a test:** the first design read `ThemeState::on_surface()` unconditionally at `add_checkbox`/`add_slider` construction time, mirroring `Node.enable_interaction`'s own real precedent — but `on_surface()`'s own no-theme-set default is black, while `Checkbox`/`Slider`'s own real historical defaults are white/gray; applying it unconditionally would have silently replaced every un-themed checkbox/slider's real default color with black. Fixed with a new `ThemeState::is_set()` accessor gating the construction-time read. `Window.set_theme` and the real live OS `ThemeChanged` path both call the new method alongside the existing one, reusing the identical already-resolved tint. New `engine-core` test (1) + `engine-render` pixel tests (3): an un-themed slider still paints the real historical gray track; a themed checkbox/slider each paint the real resolved tint. Updated `examples/theme.py` with both real timing cases (a checkbox created before `set_theme`, re-tinted by the push; a slider created after, themed at construction). Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 138, up from 137; `engine-render` 3 in `checkbox_paint.rs`, up from 2, 4 in `slider_paint.rs`, up from 2); pytest (168 passed, unchanged, 1 skipped — colors aren't Python-observable via `Node.get()`, confirming no new FFI surface was needed) and all twenty-seven examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real TextField Component Theming (§7.1, §7.3) ✅
- Step 1: `TextFieldState` gains `text_tint: Color` (default the exact historical `0x1C1B1F` literal), threaded through `TextRenderer::draw_field`'s own `at.color` (currently a fixed literal computed inline in `paint_node`'s `TextField` arm) and pushed the same real way as Phase 1's two fields — closing the milestone — ✅ (**real, confirmed continuation of Phase 1's own finding:** `0x1C1B1F` is not equal to `on_surface()`'s own no-theme-set black default either — the identical construction-time bug pattern, fixed the identical way (`ThemeState::is_set()`-gated). `Tree::set_all_component_tints` gained a `TextField` arm, closing the milestone's own real mechanism. Widened the existing `engine-core` test to cover `TextField`; new `engine-render` pixel test reuses the same real diff-based proof M17 Phase 2's own preedit-underline test established (two otherwise-identical fields, default vs. a real different `text_tint`, must paint genuinely different pixels — precise glyph-pixel prediction isn't needed). Updated `examples/theme.py` with a `TextField` created after `set_theme`. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-render` 8 in `text_field_paint.rs`, up from 7); pytest (168 passed, unchanged, 1 skipped) and all twenty-seven examples confirmed clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

**M20 — MD3 Component Color Theming is now fully complete.** Both phases closed all three "real but not yet theme-aware" gaps `engine-render`'s own source still carried — `Checkbox`'s checkmark, `Slider`'s track, and `TextField`'s text/caret/selection all now resolve to a real MD3 "on-surface" color via the exact same `Window.set_theme`/`Tree::set_all_component_tints` mechanism M7 Phase 3 originally built for ripple/hover, reused rather than reinvented for each component. The single real finding repeated across both phases: a naive "read the current theme at construction time" (mirroring `Node.enable_interaction`'s own precedent) would have silently replaced each component's own real, historical default color with `on_surface()`'s own black no-theme default, since none of the three defaults (white, gray, dark) coincide with black the way `InteractionState.tint`'s own default already did — caught and fixed with a new `ThemeState::is_set()` gate before either phase shipped, not discovered by a test after the fact.
