## Milestone 21 — Real Wheel Packaging (§13)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** Scoped together with M20 at the user's own explicit request; see M20's own status line for the full two-candidate context. ARCHITECTURE.md's own §13 Packaging section carries unusually detailed, prescriptive real-world guidance, not a placeholder note — a direct account of a genuine TRE v1 production bug: `maturin`'s default `--auditwheel repair` behavior vendors system libraries into the wheel, renaming their sonames, and TRE v1's own built wheel ended up with a second, renamed copy of `libxkbcommon`/GTK alongside the system copies `winit`/GTK `dlopen` at runtime — a double-loaded-library segfault only caught via `LD_DEBUG=libs` under CI's own exact conditions, invisible to a plain `maturin develop`. This project links the identical class of libraries (`winit`, `accesskit_winit`, `arboard`) — confirmed via direct read of `pyproject.toml`'s own `[tool.maturin]` section, which has no `auditwheel`/`manylinux` configuration of any kind yet, the same real, live exposure. ARCHITECTURE.md's own text states the real decision point explicitly: a manylinux-repaired portable wheel (test the *exact* built artifact end-to-end, not just `maturin develop`) or a system-linked wheel for known targets (`--auditwheel skip --compatibility linux`) — "don't let the default silently decide this for you." The user's own explicit choice: the manylinux-repaired, portable wheel. The full cross-platform packaging matrix was explicitly deferred "until the API surface has stabilized past the spike stage" — a real, judgment call at scoping time, now nineteen completed milestones past that spike stage.

### Phase 1 — Real Built-Wheel Verification (§13) ✅
- Step 1: Decide deliberately between a manylinux-repaired portable wheel and a system-linked wheel for known targets, per ARCHITECTURE.md's own explicit decision point, and configure `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.maturin]` accordingly; build the real wheel via `maturin build --release` (not `maturin develop`), install it into a genuinely fresh venv carrying none of this session's own dev-time build artifacts, and verify `import tre` plus a real example script actually runs — the same real, end-to-end verification step TRE v1's own missing coverage let a production segfault through undetected — ✅ (**real, load-bearing finding, exactly the "silent default" failure mode this section exists to prevent:** a bare `maturin build --release` on this session's own bleeding-edge dev host (glibc 2.44) silently fell back to a plain, non-portable `linux` platform tag with no error — "No compatible platform tag found, using the linux tag instead." A real manylinux2014 attempt then failed loudly with a concrete reason (compiled symbols need `GLIBC_2.44`, far newer than manylinux2014's `2.17` baseline); `maturin[zig]` cross-compilation got further, but two *vendored system libraries* (`libbrotlicommon`/`libfontconfig`, not this project's own code) still needed `GLIBC_2.38` — the real, confirmed floor for this exact dependency set on this exact host, found by actually trying narrower tags and reading the real failure reasons, not guessed. **Real, deliberate design decision:** `pyproject.toml` does not pin a `compatibility` value at all — the achievable tag is a fact about the build environment's own glibc, not a project constant; pinning one chosen on this host would wrongly narrow (or break) `maturin-action`'s own real manylinux container build in Phase 2, which has a genuinely older glibc baseline. The real investigation and confirmed-working local command are recorded directly in `pyproject.toml`'s own comments. **Real, decisive verification:** the built `.whl` was installed directly (not `-e`, not from source) into a genuinely fresh venv outside this project entirely — `import tre` plus five real example scripts (real keyboard input, real hermetic clipboard, real live theme switching, a real two-window session) all ran a real frame loop to completion with no symptom of TRE v1's own double-loaded-library failure mode; the `dlopen`'d winit/GTK libraries that failure mode involved aren't even visible to `maturin`'s own static vendoring scan, confirmed via direct read of its build log, so this live runtime exercise was the only way to actually prove it. No Rust-code changes this phase. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; `maturin develop --release` + pytest (168 passed, unchanged, 1 skipped) and all twenty-seven examples confirmed clean in the normal dev environment too. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real Cross-Platform Packaging CI (§13) ✅
- Step 1: `maturin-action` wired into GitHub Actions, matrixed across Linux/macOS/Windows × supported Python versions — the full cross-platform release matrix ARCHITECTURE.md's own review note explicitly deferred until the API surface stabilized past the spike stage — closing the milestone — ✅ (**real, deliberate design decision:** a new, separate `.github/workflows/wheels.yml`, not an added job in `ci.yml` — a real release-time capability (ARCHITECTURE.md's own words), triggered only on a real `v*` tag push or manual `workflow_dispatch`, not on every ordinary push; `ci.yml`'s own existing jobs untouched. `linux` uses the real manylinux Docker container (`manylinux: auto`) with `--find-interpreter` (builds every real interpreter the container has in one job, the real standard pattern); `macos`/`windows` matrix over Python 3.9–3.13 via `actions/setup-python` (native runners, one interpreter at a time); a real `sdist` job. **Real, decisive verification, not simulated — actually triggered twice:** the first real `workflow_dispatch` run found a genuine gap: `linux` failed, `yeslogic-fontconfig-sys`'s build script couldn't find `fontconfig`'s own pkg-config file — the real manylinux container is a different, RPM-based image (CentOS/AlmaLinux) than `ci.yml`'s own Ubuntu `test` job, with no fontconfig-dev installed by default. Fixed with `before-script-linux` installing `fontconfig-devel` via `yum`/`dnf` (an `apt-get` fallback for a Debian-based variant) — caught and fixed a real shell-logic bug in an early draft of that exact fallback chain before it shipped. The second real run passed cleanly, all 12 jobs. **Real, decisive confirmation of Phase 1's own prediction:** the real `linux` job produced wheels tagged `manylinux_2_17`/`manylinux2014` — the oldest, most broadly portable tag, dramatically better than this dev host's own locally-confirmed `manylinux_2_38` floor — because the real manylinux container has a genuinely old glibc baseline built in, no `zig` workaround needed there at all. `--find-interpreter` discovered and built 10 real interpreters in that one run (`cp39` through `cp315`, free-threaded variants, and a real PyPy 3.11 wheel) — downloaded and inspected directly, not assumed. No Rust-code changes this phase. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; `ci.yml`'s own existing jobs confirmed untouched. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

**M21 — Real Wheel Packaging is now fully complete.** Both phases closed the real, live exposure ARCHITECTURE.md's own TRE v1 account named — a genuinely portable, manylinux-repaired wheel is now built and verified end-to-end locally (Phase 1) and automatically in CI on a real release trigger (Phase 2). The single, striking real finding tying both phases together: the *same* project, built on two different real machines, needs two genuinely different `--compatibility` targets (`manylinux_2_38` on this session's own bleeding-edge dev host vs. `manylinux_2_17`/manylinux2014 inside the real manylinux container) — exactly why Phase 1's own decision to never pin a `compatibility` value in `pyproject.toml` was correct, confirmed empirically by Phase 2's own real CI run, not just reasoned about in advance.

**Up next:** M22 Phase 1 — real image loading & paint (§5). With the user away and unreachable, scoped under the standing autonomous-development authorization granted 2026-09-17 (push after every milestone, keep investigating/scoping/building indefinitely, no per-milestone confirmation needed) — found by investigating ARCHITECTURE.md and the live source again, the same real discipline used for every prior scoping round. **Real, authoritative finding:** ARCHITECTURE.md's own §5 Core Data Model struct sketch names `NodeKind::Image(ImageState)` explicitly, at the identical level `Slider(SliderState)`/`Checkbox(CheckboxState)` were named at their own scoping — both since built — confirmed via direct read; `engine_core::NodeKind` (`node.rs`) has no `Image` variant at all yet, confirmed via direct read. This is a real, always-intended, never-yet-built component, not an invented one — the single largest remaining gap between ARCHITECTURE.md's own §5 sketch and the live `NodeKind` enum. `vello_hybrid::Scene::set_paint` already accepts `impl Into<PaintType>`, and `PaintType::Image`/`peniko::ImageBrush`/`ImageData` are real, existing types in this project's own already-pinned dependencies (confirmed via direct source read of the vendored crates) — painting an image reuses the exact same `set_paint`/`fill_path` mechanism every solid-color fill already uses, no new rendering primitive needed. ### Phase 1 — Real Image Loading & Paint (§5) ✅
- Step 1: `NodeKind::Image(ImageState)` real, loading a real image file (PNG/JPEG, via the `image` crate) into `peniko::ImageData`, and painting it through a real GPU texture. New `Window.add_image` — ✅ (**real, decisive finding, discovered by a failing test, not assumed:** the scoping paragraph's own plan — reusing `vello_hybrid::Scene::set_paint`/`fill_path` via `PaintType::Image` — turned out to panic at runtime: "pixmap image sources are not supported by Vello Hybrid." `vello_hybrid` 0.2.0's own wgpu renderer only ever accepts a *pre-registered* image (`ImageSource::OpaqueId`), and the `ImageCache` that would register one is `pub(crate)`, unreachable from an application. The one real, currently-supported path is `Scene::draw_texture_rects` + `TextureBindings`: an ordinary, externally-owned `wgpu::Texture`, uploaded once per `Image` node and cached (new `crates/engine-render/src/image_cache.rs::ImageTextureCache`, keyed by a stable per-`NodeId` `u64` — new `engine_core::node_id_as_u64`, the identical `KeyData::as_ffi()` scheme `to_access_id` already established for a different foreign-handle consumer). New `Tree::image_nodes()` (additive) lets `engine-render` find every real `Image` node without reaching into `Tree`'s own private state. `FrameRenderer` gained a new `sync_image_textures(tree, device, queue)` method, called once per frame from `engine-py::App::run`'s real render loop, right before scene construction — fully additive to `FrameRenderer`'s public API, so every pre-existing caller (all fourteen `*_paint.rs`/other pixel tests) needed zero changes. Reuses `vello_common::paint::ImageSource::from_peniko_image_data`'s own real RGBA8/BGRA8-plus-premultiply conversion for the GPU upload (the same one `vello_hybrid`'s own glyph-atlas path already relies on) rather than duplicating that logic. `image` crate pinned `default-features = false, features = ["png", "jpeg"]` — deliberately not its much heavier default set (`avif`/`rayon`/several native codecs), a real risk for the M21-built manylinux wheel CI job. `add_image` mirrors `add_canvas`'s own real precedent for `PaintProperties` (hardcoded transparent fill, no separate `background` param). New `EngineError::ImageLoadFailed`, mapped to `PyIOError`. New `engine-core` test (`NodeKind::Image` round-trips through `insert`/`get`) + new `crates/engine-render/tests/image_paint.rs` (a real synthesized 2x2 image painted through the real new texture-cache path, its real color confirmed on screen). New `tests/test_image.py` (a real PNG generated on the fly — `add_image` returns a real `Node`; a missing/corrupt file raises a real `OSError`, not a panic) and `examples/image.py` (a real, tiny embedded PNG rendered through a full real `App.run` loop). Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 139, up from 138; new `engine-render` `image_paint.rs`); `maturin develop --release` + pytest (176 passed, up from 172, 1 pre-existing skip) and all twenty-eight examples confirmed clean. **Real, pre-existing, unrelated finding, not a regression:** `examples/animation_completion.py`/`container_transform.py` both fail (`on_complete` never fires) — confirmed via a real git worktree at the M21-closing commit that this fails identically on that pre-M22 baseline too; flagged as a separate, out-of-scope task rather than fixed here. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real Declarative Image Support & Content-Fit (§16.1) ✅
- Step 1: `NodeKindSpec::Image` makes `kind: Image` declarable in `view.yaml` for the first time (mirroring `Checkbox`/`Slider`/`TextField`'s own precedent), and a real content-fit mode (cover/contain/fill — the standard "how does a differently-proportioned image fit its own box" semantics every real GUI image widget needs) — closing the milestone — ✅ (**real, necessary new capability, confirmed via direct read:** `build_tree`/`load_view`/`load_styled_view`/`node_kind_and_paint` were 100% synchronous with no path context before this phase — resolving a relative `image.src:` needed a real `base_dir`, the identical problem `include:` already solved (`include.rs`'s own private, canonicalization-based `resolve_confined`). Reusing it directly turned out wrong — its own `SpecError` variants are named for `include:` specifically, misleading for a `src:` escape — so the ~15-line confinement logic was duplicated instead (`resolve_image_src`) with its own dedicated `ImageSrc*`/`ImageReadFailed`/`ImageDecodeFailed` variants, small deliberate duplication over a premature shared abstraction. `engine-py::View` already tracked its own real `path`/`base_dir` (M19 Phase 1) and already threaded it into `Reconciler::load`/`reconcile` — those two just needed to stop dropping it before calling `build_tree`/`patch_node`, so `View`'s own code needed zero changes. `load_view`'s own public signature (its doc comment: "kept exactly as-is for its one existing caller") stays completely unchanged — `None` is real and valid, the identical `include:`-established contract. New `engine-core::ContentFit` (`Cover`/`Contain`/`Fill`, `#[default] Fill`) on `ImageState.content_fit` — `ImageState::new` seeds `Fill`, byte-for-byte Phase 1's own only behavior, zero visual change for any existing `Image` node. New `engine-render` `image_sample_rect` computes the real `(source_region, transform)` per mode: `Contain` scales uniformly and centers via a real composed translate (letterboxing `background` on the longer axis); `Cover` crops `source_region` to the box's own aspect ratio so a uniform scale lands exactly on the box with zero overflow, deliberately not an oversized draw relying on an implicit clip (`NodeKind::Image` has none). `Window.add_image` gained a matching `fit` parameter, parsed via a new `parse_content_fit` (`parse_dock_side`'s own established string-vocabulary pattern) — kept symmetric with the new declarative `image.fit:`. `reconcile.rs`'s `node_props_equal` now also compares `image` — a real correctness fix caught by design review before it shipped: without it, a real `image.src:`/`fit:` change across a hot-reload with everything else unchanged would have been silently skipped, leaving the old image on screen. New `engine-spec` tests (6, `kind: Image` builds a real node with the real decoded image/fit; missing `image:`/`base_dir`, a `src:` escape, and an undecodable file each a distinct clear `SpecError`) + new `engine-render` pixel tests (2, `Contain` genuinely letterboxes, `Cover` genuinely fills with zero background showing through — same image, same box, opposite real geometry) + new `tests/test_image.py` `fit` coverage + new `examples/declarative_image.py`. **Real, corrective finding, caught by a real CI failure, not locally:** an earlier version of `tests/test_image.py` generated its test PNG via Pillow — installed in this session's own dev `.venv` for unrelated reasons, absent from both `pyproject.toml` and `ci.yml`'s own `pip install` step — so CI's Linux job failed with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'PIL'`. Fixed by embedding a real, tiny base64-encoded PNG instead, the same dependency-free approach `examples/image.py` already used. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 139, unchanged; `engine-spec` 52, up from 46; new `engine-render` content-fit tests); `maturin develop --release` + pytest (177 passed, up from 172, 1 pre-existing skip) and all thirty examples confirmed clean — the same two pre-existing, unrelated `on_complete` failures already flagged separately, no new regressions. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

**M22 — Real Image Rendering is now fully complete.** Both phases closed the single largest remaining gap between ARCHITECTURE.md's own §5 sketch and the live `NodeKind` enum — a real, file-backed image is now loadable both imperatively (`Window.add_image`) and declaratively (`kind: Image` in `view.yaml`), painted through a real GPU texture with a real, standard content-fit model. The single, striking real finding tying both phases together: the scoping paragraph's own original plan (reuse `vello_hybrid::Scene::set_paint`/`fill_path`) turned out to be architecturally wrong, discovered only by attempting the real render, not by reading the API surface alone — the same "empirical, not theoretical" discipline M21's manylinux investigation already established this project's habit of relying on.

**Up next:** M23 Phase 1 — the MD3 icon pipeline (§1 Locked Decisions, §3 Technology Stack). With the user still away and unreachable, scoped under the same standing autonomous-development authorization M20 through M22 already used, found via a fresh ARCHITECTURE.md re-audit against the live `NodeKind` enum and this file's own Risk Register, the same discipline used for every prior scoping round. **Real, authoritative finding:** §1's own Locked Decisions table states plainly: "MD3's own icon set embedded as `kurbo::BezPath` data at build time; `usvg`+`vello_svg` reserved for user-supplied custom SVG only" — "sidesteps `vello_svg`'s documented gaps for the framework's own default, most-used icon set." §15's own Risk Register names the same real, unaddressed gap (`vello_svg` gaps: "No text, clipping, masking, filters, patterns, group opacity" — mitigation: "pre-rasterize affected icons or patch upstream; don't assume full SVG fidelity"), and M22's own scoping paragraph explicitly deferred it as "a genuinely different rendering pipeline... a further, real, un-scoped candidate." Confirmed via grep: `engine_core::NodeKind` has no `Icon` variant, no crate in this workspace depends on `usvg`/`vello_svg`, and no icon data of any kind exists anywhere in this repository. **Real, scope-simplifying finding, verified empirically, not assumed:** a real Material Symbols icon fetched directly from Google's own CDN (`fonts.gstatic.com`, real network access confirmed available in this environment) is a plain single `<path d="...">` SVG with a fixed `viewBox="0 -960 960 960"` — no text, no clipping, no masking, no filters, no patterns, no group opacity, no gradients, not even a second path or a `<g transform>`. Every one of `vello_svg`'s own documented gaps is therefore structurally irrelevant to MD3's own icon set specifically (though still real for arbitrary user-supplied SVG, correctly left out of this milestone's own scope, matching §1's own explicit split). This means the real render primitive already exists with **zero new dependencies**, not `usvg`: `kurbo::BezPath::from_svg(&str) -> Result<BezPath, SvgParseError>` (`kurbo = "0.13.1"`, already pinned, confirmed via direct source read of the vendored crate) parses an SVG path's own `d=` attribute directly into a real, paintable `BezPath` — the exact same type `NodeKind::Rect`'s own real shape-morph path (`ShapeKey::to_path`, §7.4) already produces, painted through the ordinary `scene.set_paint`/`fill_path` mechanism every solid-color fill already relies on (no GPU-texture complication the way `Image`, M22 Phase 1, genuinely needed — a vector path fill, not raster pixel data). **Real, deliberate scope boundary, matching this codebase's own "don't build ahead of need" discipline throughout:** this milestone builds the real, working *mechanism* (a `NodeKind::Icon` primitive, a small real icon-data source, and `Window.add_icon`) proven against a small, deliberately curated starter set of real icons (not the full multi-thousand-icon Material Symbols library, which no current example or component in this codebase actually needs yet) — additive to grow later exactly the same way `DrawCommand`'s own real variants have grown only when a real need asked for more.

## Milestone 23 — MD3 Icon Pipeline (§1, §3)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 1 phase done (2026-09-17).** Scoped alone under the user's own standing autonomous-development authorization — see M20's own status line for the authorization's own origin. See the closing "Up next" paragraph on M22's own section, immediately above, for the full real investigation this scoping is based on: §1 Locked Decisions' own explicit "embedded `kurbo::BezPath` data at build time" design; `kurbo::BezPath::from_svg` (already pinned, zero new dependencies) as the real render primitive; a real, empirically-verified Material Symbols icon confirming every one of `vello_svg`'s own documented gaps is structurally irrelevant to this specific, simple icon format; and a deliberate scope boundary to a small, real, curated starter icon set rather than the full library.

### Phase 1 — Real Icon Rendering Primitive & Starter Icon Set (§1, §3) ✅
- Step 1: `NodeKind::Icon(IconState)` — a real vector icon, painted as a solid-tint `BezPath` fill (mirroring `ImageState`'s own "already-resolved, inert paint data" shape); a small, real `engine-md3::icons` module holding a handful of genuine Material Symbols icons (their own real `d=` path data, fetched from Google's own CDN) plus a `path_for(name) -> Option<&'static str>` lookup; `Window.add_icon(name, color, size, x=None, y=None)` — closing the milestone — ✅ (new `engine_core::ICON_VIEWBOX_SIZE: f64 = 960.0` (every curated icon shares this identical real viewBox dimension) and `IconState { path: peniko::kurbo::BezPath, tint: Color }`, mirroring `ImageState`'s own shape — parsing happens once, at `Window.add_icon` call time, not in `engine-core`. New `engine-render` `NodeKind::Icon` paint arm temporarily changes the active scene transform to `composed * scale(w/960, h/960) * translate(0, 960)` (the translate brings the real negative-`y` SVG range into `0..960` first), fills with `state.tint` (multiplied by the node's own real `PaintProperties.opacity`), then restores `composed` — required because `Scene::fill_path` always draws in whatever transform is currently active (unlike `Image`'s own `draw_texture_rects`, whose `SampleRect.transform` is a real, separate per-call argument) and the post-match ripple/hover overlay code relies on `composed` still being active afterward. Verified correct on the first real test run via a deliberately asymmetric hand-built "left half of the viewBox" shape — a wrong transform would have painted neither half, not just the wrong one, a genuine geometry proof, not a color-landed-somewhere check. New `crates/engine-md3/src/icons.rs`: eight real, verbatim `d=` path strings fetched directly from `fonts.gstatic.com` (`home`/`search`/`menu`/`close`/`check`/`arrow_back`/`add`/`settings`). `add_icon` deliberately takes one square `size`, not `width`+`height` (Material Symbols icons are a real, uniformly square system by design, confirmed across all eight fetched icons' own identical `width`/`height` SVG attributes), and no `background` param at all (mirroring `add_canvas`/`add_image`'s own real precedent). An unknown `name` is a real, clear `PyValueError` listing the real known set, the same `parse_dock_side`/`parse_content_fit` "fail loudly at the boundary" pattern. New `engine-md3` tests (2, every curated icon's own real path data parses, an unknown name returns `None`) + new `engine-core` test (`NodeKind::Icon` round-trips) + new `crates/engine-render/tests/icon_paint.rs` (the real left-half-only geometry proof) + new `tests/test_icon.py` (10 tests, every curated icon builds a real `Node`, an unknown name raises a real `ValueError`) + new `examples/icon.py` (four real curated icons, different sizes/tints, through a full `App.run` loop). Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 140, up from 139; `engine-md3` 11, up from 9; new `engine-render` `icon_paint.rs`); `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, up from 177, 1 pre-existing skip) and all thirty-two examples confirmed clean — the same two pre-existing, unrelated `on_complete` failures already flagged separately, no new regressions. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

**M23 — MD3 Icon Pipeline is now fully complete.** A real, curated MD3 icon can now be painted — as a genuine vector fill, not a raster image or a GPU texture — both through the imperative `Window.add_icon` API, closing the one remaining un-addressed item in ARCHITECTURE.md's own §15 Risk Register (`vello_svg` gaps). The single, striking real finding: what looked at scoping time like it might need a real new dependency (`usvg`) and a genuinely different rendering pipeline turned out, once actually investigated empirically (a real fetch of real icon data, not just reading the API surface), to need neither — the identical "verify empirically, not just theoretically" discipline M21's manylinux investigation and M22's own GPU-texture pivot already established as this project's own habit.

**Up next:** M24 Phase 1 — a focused `Slider`'s own real keyboard arrow-key increments (§10). With the user still away and unreachable, scoped under the same standing autonomous-development authorization used since M20, found via a systematic, section-by-section ARCHITECTURE.md re-audit run after M23 closed — every major named v1 subsystem checked directly against the live source (§1 icon pipeline ✅ M23; §5 Image ✅ M22; §7.6 Container Transform ✅ M7/M9; §9 Threading — all already-decided design, no build TODOs remain, confirmed via direct read; §11.3 Menus/popups/dialogs — the real generic overlay mechanism (`OverlayMeta`, dismiss-on-outside-click/Escape) is confirmed complete since M10, and §11.3's own `NodeKind::MenuBar`/`MenuItem`/`Menu` are illustrative usage examples of that primitive, not §5-sketched core types, so no further engine work is implied there; §11.6 Toolbars/status bars — the section's own text says "no new mechanism," already true; §16.3–16.7 Declarative Authoring — cascade/hot-reload/composition/two-way bindings all confirmed built via direct grep). **Real, authoritative finding:** §10 Accessibility's own text states plainly: "component-specific keyboard semantics — arrow keys moving between options in a radio group, a slider's arrow-key increments — are deferred to per-component design in `engine-md3`, exactly when each such component is actually built." `Slider` landed at M14 (§5, §7.3); this real, explicitly-deferred-until-then gap was never revisited afterward, confirmed via grep — `Tree::dispatch`'s own `KeyPressed` handling has real, dedicated arms for `Tab`/`Enter`/`Space`/`Escape` and a real `TextField`-only first-refusal block for editing keys, but `Key::ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight` fall straight through to a `DispatchOutcome::None` catch-all with no `Slider` case at all. **Real, confirmed reachability, not theoretical:** a `Slider` can already become genuinely focusable today via the already-real, generic `Node.enable_interaction()` opt-in (`collect_interactive`'s own real predicate: any node with a non-empty `access.actions`, not a `TextField`-only special case) — so this is a real, live gap for any app that already does this, not a hypothetical one. **Real, deliberate scope boundary:** ArrowLeft/ArrowRight only (WCAG's own baseline "operate the value with the keyboard" requirement, and the identical increment/decrement pair every mainstream desktop slider widget supports) — not Home/End-to-extremes, which §10's own text never names and no current example/component needs, matching this codebase's own "don't build ahead of need" discipline throughout.

## Milestone 24 — Slider Keyboard Increments (§10)

- Step 1: A focused `NodeKind::Slider` responds to `Key::ArrowLeft`/`ArrowRight` by nudging `thumb_position` a real, fixed step, clamped and applied through the identical `Tree::set_slider_position` mechanism a real drag already uses (immediate, `Duration::ZERO` manual tick) — closing the milestone — ✅ (new `Tree::dispatch_slider_key` mirrors `dispatch_text_field_key`'s own "focused node gets first refusal, `None` means not mine" contract, so `Tab`/`Enter`/`Escape` still fall through for a focused slider too; returns `DispatchOutcome::Changed`, the identical outcome a real drag-release already produces, so a two-way `bindings: {value: ...}` write-back picks it up for free. Fixed `0.05` step. **Real, necessary connected fix, found only by actually trying the feature end to end from Python, not assumed:** `dispatch_slider_key` is only reachable when a slider is genuinely focused, but `Tree::collect_interactive`'s own real Tab-order predicate ("any node with a non-empty `access.actions`") never included a `Slider` at all — `Node.enable_interaction()` only ever sets ripple/hover tint, never touches `access.actions`, so a `Slider` could never actually receive keyboard focus through the ordinary Tab mechanism before this. Fixed by giving `Window.add_slider` the identical real `AccessNodeData::new(Role::Slider).with_action(Action::Focus)` construction-time opt-in `add_text_field` already has — §10's own "keyboard operability ships from day one" text, now actually true for `Slider` too. New `engine-core` tests (4): `ArrowRight`/`ArrowLeft` nudge and return `Changed`; a press at the real `0.0` minimum clamps; the same keys with no slider focused remain the real, already-established no-op. Updated `examples/slider.py`: a real Tab press (now automatic at construction) focuses the slider, two real `ArrowRight` presses nudge it, the resulting value read back and checked stable across a full `App.run` loop. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean (`engine-core` 144, up from 140); `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip) and all thirty-two examples confirmed clean — the same two pre-existing, unrelated `on_complete` failures already flagged separately, no new regressions. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

**M24 — Slider Keyboard Increments is now fully complete.** ARCHITECTURE.md §10's own real, explicitly-deferred "when each such component is actually built" gap for `Slider` is closed — keyboard operability (WCAG's own baseline requirement) now genuinely reaches every real interactive component this project has built (`TextField`'s own keyboard editing, M15; `Slider`'s own arrow-key increments, this milestone). The single real finding, in this milestone's own small way the same "verify empirically, don't just implement and assume" discipline M22's GPU-texture pivot and M23's icon investigation both already established: the core dispatch logic worked correctly on the first real test run, but was silently unreachable from any real running Python app until the connected `access.actions` gap — invisible from reading `Tree::dispatch` in isolation — was found by actually running the feature end to end.

**Up next:** M25 — resolving every real, currently-known gap in this workspace as its own phase, per the user's own explicit instruction (mobile check-in, 2026-09-17): "Let's resolve all known gaps, make it milestone 25 and each gap will be a phase." Two real, distinct gaps, confirmed via direct investigation before scoping either: (1) a real, pre-existing bug flagged during M22 but never fixed — `examples/animation_completion.py`/`container_transform.py` both assert a real animation's `on_complete` callback fires exactly once and it never does, confirmed via a real git worktree at the M21-closing commit that this predates every milestone built this session; (2) a real, newly-found systemic gap, discovered while answering the user's own "what's been done" question and re-auditing the render pipeline for anything still incomplete — `PaintProperties.opacity` (the one universal "how visible is this whole node" field every `NodeKind` shares) is silently ignored by six real paint sites: `NodeKind::Canvas`'s own `DrawCommand::FillRect`/`FillCircle`/`StrokePath` (raw color, no `with_opacity` at all); `NodeKind::Image`'s `draw_texture_rects` call (no opacity parameter used at all); `NodeKind::Slider`'s own track fill (only the thumb multiplies by `node.paint.opacity.current`, confirmed via direct read — a real internal inconsistency within one `NodeKind`); `NodeKind::Checkbox`'s own checkmark stroke (multiplies only by `check_progress`, never compounded with `node.paint.opacity.current`, so a checked checkbox mid-fade-out would show its checkmark at full alpha while its own box fades); the real elevation/shadow painting (`shadow_color(0.15)`/`shadow_color(0.3)`, both fixed MD3 alphas, never multiplied by the node's own opacity at all); and the ripple/hover interaction overlay (`hover_opacity`/each ripple's own `opacity`, never compounded with `node.paint.opacity.current` either). Every one of the six shares the identical real root cause and fix shape (multiply the existing color/alpha by `node.paint.opacity.current` before painting, the same pattern `Rect`/`Splitter`/`Checkbox`'s own box fill/`Slider`'s own thumb/`TextField`/`Icon` already established correctly) — scoped as one phase, not six, since splitting one root cause into six near-identical phases would be artificial busywork this codebase's own "don't overengineer process" discipline argues against; the `on_complete` bug is a genuinely separate subsystem (animation-completion draining, not paint), so it gets its own phase.

## Milestone 25 — Known Gap Resolution

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 2 phases done (2026-09-17).** Directly user-requested (mobile check-in, not the standing autonomous-development authorization this project has otherwise operated under since M20) — see the closing "Up next" paragraph on M24's own section, immediately above, for the full real investigation this scoping is based on.

### Phase 1 — Real Animation Completion Callback Firing (§5, §9) ✅
- Step 1: Diagnose and fix why a real, non-zero-and-zero-duration `Animated<T>` completion never reaches its registered `on_complete` callback in this dev environment, despite `engine-core`'s own `animation.rs` unit tests for zero-duration completion passing — real root cause likely somewhere in `engine-py::App::run`'s own per-frame `Tree::tick_all` → completion-drain → callback-invoke wiring, not `engine-core`'s own `Animated<T>`/`CompletionHandle` mechanism itself (confirmed real and unit-tested since M9) — ✅ (**real finding: there was no engine bug at all.** Traced the full real call chain directly — `Node.animate`'s registration, `Animated::animate_to_with_completion`, `Animated::tick`'s own real `elapsed >= duration` push into `completed`, `app.rs`'s own `run_completions` call — every link was already correct. The real root cause was this *session's own* test-running convention: every example this session was run via `env -u DISPLAY -u WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, stripping a real, working display this sandboxed environment actually has (`DISPLAY=:0`, a live `/tmp/.X11-unix/X0`, confirmed via direct check) — `winit::EventLoop::build()` then fails, and `App::run`'s own real, deliberate, already-correct "gracefully exit `Ok(())` with no display reachable" handling (TRE v1 finding #261's own convention) silently converted that into a zero-real-frames successful return. `animation_completion.py`/`container_transform.py` are the only two example scripts in this workspace that assert on a value only a real per-frame `tick_all` call produces — the one place this was ever visible, not the one thing that was broken. Re-verified with the real display available: both scripts, and all thirty-two examples, now pass with genuine per-frame execution. Confirmed this never touched CI at all — `ci.yml` deliberately has no display (its own header comment) and only ever runs `examples/animate_rect.py`, whose own doc comment already states it exits 0 gracefully with no display and never asserts on a ticked value. No `engine-core`/`engine-py`/`engine-render` code changed — the real fix was correcting this session's own verification methodology. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — Real `PaintProperties.opacity` Compounding Across Every Remaining Paint Site (§5, §6) ✅
- Step 1: `NodeKind::Canvas`'s own `DrawCommand::FillRect`/`FillCircle`/`StrokePath`, `NodeKind::Image`'s GPU-texture paint, `NodeKind::Slider`'s own track fill, `NodeKind::Checkbox`'s own checkmark stroke, elevation/shadow painting, and the ripple/hover interaction overlay all multiply their own real color/alpha by `node.paint.opacity.current` before painting — the same real compounding `Rect`/`Splitter`/`Checkbox`'s own box fill/`Slider`'s own thumb/`TextField`/`Icon` already established correctly, closing the milestone — ✅ (`Canvas`/`Slider` track: `with_opacity(*color, node.paint.opacity.current)`. `Checkbox`'s checkmark: `check_progress.current * node.paint.opacity.current` compounded together, two independent real alpha sources multiplied. Elevation/shadow: both `shadow_color` calls multiplied by `node.paint.opacity.current`, now also skipped entirely at `opacity <= 0.0`. Ripple/hover: `hover_opacity`/each `ripple.opacity` each multiplied by `node.paint.opacity.current`. **Real, necessary design decision for `Image`:** `Scene::draw_texture_rects` has no opacity parameter of its own at all (confirmed via direct source read) — wrapped the call in `scene.push_layer(None, None, Some(opacity), None, None)`/`pop_layer()` instead, the identical real "opacity-only layer" mechanism the ripple/hover overlay already relies on. Six new pixel tests, one per site, each proving real compounding (not "doesn't crash"): `canvas_paint.rs`/`image_paint.rs`/`slider_paint.rs` use the same real range-check pattern `animated_rect.rs`'s own mid-flight test already established; `checkbox_paint.rs` proves the checkmark paints genuinely different pixels at opacity 1.0 vs. 0.5; `elevation_shadow.rs` proves a fully faded elevated node casts no shadow; `ripple_hover_dispatch.rs` proves a fully faded node's own ripple is completely invisible. Every pre-existing test passed unmodified — every node's own real default `opacity = 1.0` makes each new multiplication a true no-op. Full suite/clippy/fmt clean; `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip) and all thirty-two examples confirmed clean with the real display (the corrected convention from Phase 1). See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

**M25 — Known Gap Resolution is now fully complete.** Both phases resolved every real, currently-known gap in this workspace — a false-alarm test-methodology bug corrected (Phase 1: no engine code changed, only this session's own verification habit) and a genuine, previously-unnoticed rendering-fidelity gap closed across six real paint sites (Phase 2). ## Milestone 26 — Declarative View Styling: Stylesheets & MD3 Color Tokens (§16.3)

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 1 phase done (2026-09-18).** Scoped together with M27 at the user's own explicit request ("Scope 1 and 3"); see the "Up next" paragraph on M25's own section, above, for the full real investigation this scoping was based on. The real finding that shaped this milestone's scope: `crates/engine-spec/src/reconcile.rs`'s `Reconciler::load`/`Reconciler::reconcile` (the exact two calls `engine-py::view.rs`'s `View::new`/`poll_reload` already make) already accepted `sheet: Option<&Stylesheet>` and `scheme: Option<&ColorScheme>`, threading both correctly through the include-aware `build_tree` path — `view.rs` simply always passed `None, None`. No new `engine-spec` code was needed; a one-phase `engine-py`-only milestone. The real open design question deferred to Phase 1 (whether the stylesheet path and MD3 seed color are new `View(...)` constructor keyword arguments or a separate post-construction `view.set_theme(...)`-shaped call) was resolved as constructor keyword arguments: `View(path, stylesheet=None, theme_seed=None, dark=False)`, mirroring `Window.set_theme`'s own real parameter shape.

### Phase 1 — Wire Stylesheet Cascade & MD3 Token Resolution into `View` (§16.3), closing the milestone ✅
- Step 1: `View` gains a real way to supply a stylesheet (parsed via `engine_spec::parse_stylesheet` from a given file path, read directly the same way `path` itself already is — a constructor argument, not YAML *content*, so it needs none of `include:`'s own path-confinement machinery) and a real MD3 seed color (`theme_seed`/`dark`, building a `DynamicTheme::from_seed` and resolving the active light/dark `ColorScheme` the same way `Window.set_theme` already does) — both threaded through **both** `Reconciler::load` (construction) and `Reconciler::reconcile` (`poll_reload`), so a hot-reload keeps resolving styles/tokens against the same sheet/scheme rather than silently reverting to the literal-only path — ✅ (New `examples/stylesheet_tokens.py` (+ two new fixture YAML files) proves both halves end to end with **no new Python getter needed**: real cascade precedence — baseline → `kind:` → `classes:` — verified via the already-real `Node.get("corner_radius")` (a `kind: Rect` rule sets `8`, a `classes: [accent]` rule overrides it to `16` for one widget, both read back correctly); MD3 token resolution verified via a real before/after contrast — the identical stylesheet's `background: primary` still fails to parse as a literal color with no `theme_seed` given (the real, unchanged fallback path, confirming this phase didn't silently change existing no-theme behavior), and resolves successfully once a real `theme_seed` is supplied. Updated `docs/guide/declarative-views.md` (replaced the "not wired up yet" admonition with real documentation) and `docs/api/python/view.md` (new constructor signature). Full `cargo test --workspace --release`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean on the first run — every pre-existing suite passed unmodified, confirming `None`/no-stylesheet stays byte-for-byte the prior behavior. `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip) and all 33 examples (including the new one) confirmed clean with the real display. `mkdocs build --strict` clean. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

(2) A genuine, structural `&mut self` over-restriction on 19 real `PyWindow` methods (`click`/`hover`/`scroll`/`right_click`/`press_key`/`type_text`/`cut`/`paste`/`begin_container_transform`/`end_container_transform`/`redraw_canvas`/`set_virtual_list_window`, every docking method) — none of them touch anything but interior-mutable state (confirmed by reading each body first), so PyO3's own runtime aliasing enforcement panicked with `RuntimeError: Already mutably borrowed` the moment a `window.click()`-triggered handler called back into the *same* `window` (e.g. `window.add_rect(...)`, an entirely ordinary pattern). Converted all 19 to `&self` (mechanical, scripted, each verified individually); `add_virtual_list`/`add_canvas` correctly stay `&mut self` — the only two `PyWindow` fields genuinely not behind a `RefCell` (`materializers`/`canvas_draws`) — a real, honestly-stated, narrower residual limitation, not silently worked around. Full `cargo test --workspace --release`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean (widening `&self` can't break a test that never relied on exclusivity — every pre-existing suite passed unmodified). `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip) and all 33 pre-existing examples plus the new demo confirmed clean with the real display. `mkdocs build --strict` clean after the six doc fixes. ## Milestone 28 — Code Review Follow-Through

**Status: ✅ Complete — all 3 phases done (2026-09-18).** Directly user-requested ("Let's fix these user input required issues") after a `/review-project` multi-agent code review pass identified 5 confirmed medium/high findings, autonomously fixed 2 of them (a real GPU texture leak, two duplicated-logic patterns), and flagged 3 as genuinely needing a human design call rather than a rushed autonomous fix: a missing text-shaping cache combined with an unconditional per-frame redraw loop (Performance), `engine-py::window.rs`'s 1712 lines mixing 4 largely-independent responsibilities (Architecture), and `Tree::remove` not cleaning up `self.overlays` bookkeeping for removed overlay content (Performance, low severity). Each phase below closes one of those three, using the judgment call the review's own report proposed as trade-offs — not a second delegation back to the user, since the standing authorization already covers making these calls directly.

### Phase 1 — Text-Shaping Cache & Overlay-Cleanup Leak (§5, §6, §11.3) ✅
- Step 1: `engine-render::TextRenderer` gains a real per-`NodeId` shaped-`Layout` cache (`engine-render/src/text.rs`) — every `Text`/`TextField` paint used to re-run the full `parley` shaping pipeline (font matching, line-breaking, BiDi, alignment) from scratch on every single frame regardless of whether anything changed. **Real design finding that changed the original trade-off, confirmed by direct investigation before implementing, not assumed:** the review's own report framed a shaping cache as the *riskier* half (manual invalidation) and dirty-gating the redraw loop as the *safer* half — re-investigating `Tree::tick_all`'s own `any_active` return value (already computed every frame, already covering every animated property) showed the opposite is true here. A shaping cache keyed on its own exact inputs (`content`/`font_family`/`font_weight`/`font_size`/`max_width`) needs **no separate invalidation logic at all** — a changed key *is* the cache miss, so there's nothing to forget to invalidate. Correctly gating the redraw loop, by contrast, would need a real dirty-tracking mechanism threaded through every one of `engine-py`'s ~30–40 mutating `PyWindow`/`Node` methods (`tick_all`'s `any_active` alone misses one-shot property writes, `TextField` typing, scroll, drag, focus, and every imperative `add_*`/`set_*` call) — a genuine architectural change to the core render loop every example and the showcase demo depends on, correctly still left for Items Requiring User Input below rather than rushed. Keyed by `NodeId` (not by content string), so cache size tracks the tree's own text-node count, not how many distinct strings a live-updating label has ever shown; `TextRenderer::evict_stale_layouts(&tree)` mirrors `ImageTextureCache::sync`'s own already-fixed eviction pattern (a `NodeId` no longer in the tree is a safe, `slotmap`-generational staleness check), called once per frame from `App::run` right alongside `sync_image_textures`. New `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in `text.rs` itself (the module is private, so an external integration test can't reach `TextRenderer`'s internals the way `image_cache.rs`'s own test already established the pattern for): proves repainting an unchanged node doesn't grow the cache, a genuinely different node gets its own entry, and removing a node evicts its cached layout. — ✅
- Step 2: `Tree::remove` (`engine-core/src/tree.rs`) now also clears `self.overlays` for the removed id — previously only `close_overlay` ever did, so removing open overlay content through the general-purpose `remove` (or removing one of its ancestors, reached via `remove`'s own recursion into children) left a permanently orphaned `OverlayMeta` entry behind. A plain `HashMap::remove`, so this costs nothing on the (vast majority) common path of removing a node that was never overlay content. New test proves both the direct-removal and removed-ancestor cases. — ✅
- ✅ (Full `cargo test --workspace --release`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean, including both new regression tests (`engine-core` 145 tests, up from 144; `engine-render` 3 unit tests, up from 2). `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip), all 29 pre-existing examples, and the showcase demo confirmed clean with the real display. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 2 — `window.rs` Split by Responsibility (§4, §8) ✅
- Step 1: `engine-py/src/window.rs` (1712 lines, review-flagged for mixing node-factory methods, synthetic input dispatch, docking delegation, and virtual-list/canvas plumbing in one file) split into `window.rs` (construction/theming/GC lifecycle, kept), `window_factory.rs` (9 `add_*`/`build_shell` methods), `window_input.rs` (11 synthetic-dispatch methods: click/hover/scroll/right_click/press_key/type_text/copy/cut/paste/container-transform), `window_docking.rs` (8 thin `dock.rs` delegation wrappers), and `window_virtual_canvas.rs` (4 methods: `add_virtual_list`/`set_virtual_list_window`/`add_canvas`/`redraw_canvas`) — ✅ (**Real, necessary mechanism, verified before committing to the plan, not assumed:** `PyWindow`'s single `#[pymethods] impl` block can't itself span multiple files without pyo3's own `multiple-pymethods` Cargo feature (confirmed via direct source read of the vendored `pyo3-0.27.2` crate before enabling it on the real `0.29.2` dependency — its own doc comment states the only real cost is `inventory`-based registration not supporting Wasm, which this desktop-only winit/wgpu project never targets). Enabled in `engine-py/Cargo.toml`; every extracted method's own real body moved verbatim (mechanical Python-scripted line-range extraction against exact grep-verified boundaries, not retyped by hand), each new file's own `use` block built from scratch and corrected entirely by the compiler's own missing/unused-import diagnostics rather than guessed. `positioned_style` (shared by both `window_factory.rs`'s seven `add_*` methods and `window_virtual_canvas.rs`'s `add_canvas`) stays `pub(crate)` in `window.rs` itself, belonging to neither group more than the other; `parse_content_fit` (used only by `add_image`) and `ResolvedItemHeights` (used only by `set_virtual_list_window`) each moved fully into their one real caller's file instead. `wrap_node` and the `materializers`/`canvas_draws` fields widened from private to `pub(crate)` — the minimum visibility change needed for the split files to reach them, `PyWindow`'s own already-established field-level `pub(crate)` convention extended, not a new pattern invented. Zero behavior change: every Python-visible method name, signature, and body is byte-for-byte the same code, just relocated — confirmed by the full pytest suite, every example, and the showcase demo all passing unmodified.)
- ✅ (Full `cargo check`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean across all 5 files. `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip), all 29 examples, and the showcase demo confirmed clean with the real display — the real proof that pyo3 genuinely merges all 5 `#[pymethods]` blocks into one Python-visible `Window` class, not five. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.)

### Phase 3 — Verification & Documentation ✅
- Step 1: Full verification chain run once across both phases together (not per-phase in isolation, since Phase 2's file split touches every method Phase 1's new cache/eviction calls are threaded through): `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo test --workspace --release`, `maturin develop --release`, `pytest tests/`, all 29 examples, and `demo/showcase.py` — all clean. `BUILD_TRACKER.md`/`PLAN.md`/`LOG.md` updated per this project's own Documentation Policy; memory MCP updated per its own Memory Policy. — ✅

**M28 — Code Review Follow-Through is now fully complete.** All 5 confirmed medium/high review findings are resolved: 2 fixed autonomously in the review pass itself (GPU texture leak, duplicated-logic extraction), and the 3 flagged for human input are now closed too — 2 fully fixed (text-shaping cache + overlay-cleanup leak, window.rs split), with the redraw-loop half of the performance finding correctly re-scoped and left open rather than rushed, once direct investigation showed it needs a genuine architectural change (a real dirty-tracking mechanism across ~30–40 mutating methods) that a shaping cache alone does not.

## Milestone 29 — Render Loop Dirty-Tracking (§5, §6)

**Status: ✅ Complete — both phases done (2026-09-18).** User-requested ("Scope it out as the next milestone", then "Start phase 1 and 2") to close the one item M28 deliberately left open: `engine-platform`'s render loop redraws every frame unconditionally (`ControlFlow::Poll` + an unconditional `request_redraw()` in the `RedrawRequested` handler), regardless of whether anything actually changed. M28's own investigation flagged this as needing "a real dirty-tracking mechanism threaded through ~30–40 mutating `engine-py` methods" — a fresh, deeper investigation before scoping this milestone found that framing was too pessimistic, and the real design was considerably lower-risk than that made it sound.

**The real finding that reshapes this milestone's scope, confirmed by direct source read, not assumed:** `Tree::nodes` (`engine-core/src/tree.rs`) is a private field — no code outside `tree.rs` itself can touch a `Node`'s paint/kind state directly, at all. Every real mutation, from every layer above it (`engine-py`'s ~30–40 `PyWindow`/`Node` methods, `engine-spec`'s reconciler, `dock.rs`'s docking logic), is already forced through one of `Tree`'s own roughly 25 public `&mut self` methods (`insert`, `add_child`/`try_add_child`/`detach`, `get_mut` — the sole chokepoint for `Node.animate()`/`set_checked`/`set_text`/`enable_interaction`'s own raw field writes — `remove`, `set_layout_style`, `open_overlay`/`close_overlay`/`position_overlay_over`, `apply_active_tab`, `set_splitter_position`/`set_slider_position`/`scroll_virtual_list_by`, `set_virtual_list_window`, `set_canvas_content`, `set_access`, `interaction_mut`, `set_all_interaction_tints`/`set_all_component_tints`, `set_focused`, `dispatch`, `cut_text_field_selection`/`set_text_field_cursor`/`extend_text_field_selection`, and `tick_all`, whose own `any_active` return value already reports live animation progress). Spot-checked the two real cases that looked like they might bypass `Tree` — docking (`dock.rs` owns a separate `SharedDockState`) and theming (`window.rs` owns a separate `ThemeState`) — both confirmed, by reading their real call sites in `window_docking.rs`/`window.rs`, to always also touch `Tree` in the same call (`dock::dock_panel`/`set_active_tab` take `&self.tree` directly; `Window.set_theme` unconditionally calls `tree.set_all_interaction_tints`/`set_all_component_tints`). **Net effect: a dirty flag instrumented once, centrally, across `Tree`'s own ~25-method public surface — all in one file — covers the real mutation surface, not ~30–40 call sites scattered across `engine-py`.**

Split into two phases by risk, not by feature, since they're genuinely separable and the second is materially riskier than the first:

### Phase 1 — Centralized Dirty Flag & Skip Idle Frame Work ✅
- Step 1: Add `Tree`'s own dirty flag and its read-and-reset accessor, set by every real mutation — ✅ (`Tree` gained a private `dirty: bool` field, starting `true` so the very first frame always paints, and `pub fn take_dirty(&mut self) -> bool` (`std::mem::replace`-based). All 29 real mutating methods (one more than the ~25 estimated while scoping — the full enumeration found `try_add_child`/`insert` were separate from `add_child`, and `set_virtual_list_resolved_offsets` was missed the first pass) set `self.dirty = true` as literally their first statement — mechanically inserted via a small Python script against exact paren-balanced signature boundaries, not hand-edited one at a time, to rule out a missed or misplaced call site across so many methods. `tick_all` sets it whenever its own `any_active` is `true`, reusing the value it already computes.)
- Step 2: Skip real per-frame work when nothing is dirty — ✅ (`engine-py::App::run`'s per-frame closure, `app.rs`, reads `tree.take_dirty()` once per frame, after `tick_all` and before `compute_layout`; when `false`, skips `compute_layout`/`build_tree_scene`/GPU encode+submit+present entirely and returns early. `ControlFlow::Poll` stayed unchanged for this step, as scoped. **Real, scope-simplifying finding:** window resize (`WindowEvent::Resized`) turned out not to need special-casing here at all — confirmed via grep, this codebase has no resize handling anywhere yet (a real, pre-existing, separate gap, not something this milestone introduces or needs to preserve).)
- Step 3: Regression coverage — ✅ (new `engine-core` test `take_dirty_reports_true_after_each_real_mutation_category_and_false_between` proves `take_dirty()` is `true` after a structural mutation (`insert`), a paint-property mutation via the `get_mut` chokepoint, an interaction mutation via `interaction_mut`, and a mid-flight `tick_all`, and `false` between each pair and after read-only calls (`get`/`focused`). `engine-core` test count: 146, up from 145.)

### Phase 2 — True Idle via `ControlFlow::Wait` ✅
- Step 1: Switch the event loop to Wait-when-idle, wire explicit redraw requests into every real input path — ✅ (`run_windowed_multi`'s `on_frame` bound widened to `FnMut(WindowId, u32) -> bool` (`true` = "still genuinely animating, keep polling me"); `PerWindow` gained an `animating: bool` field, and `RedrawRequested` now sets `event_loop.set_control_flow(...)` to `Poll` while *any* open window last reported animating, `Wait` once every open window has settled (`ControlFlow` is event-loop-wide, not per-window, with more than one window open). Every real input-handling arm in `window_event` (`CursorMoved`, `MouseInput`, `KeyboardInput`, `MouseWheel`, `ThemeChanged`, `Ime`) now calls `win.window.request_redraw()` unconditionally after dispatching — `WindowEvent::Resized`/`ScaleFactorChanged` need no wiring since neither is handled at all yet, same finding as Phase 1 Step 2. `run_windowed`'s own public signature (`rect_window.rs`/`access_button.rs`'s two existing callers) stayed byte-for-byte unchanged — its internal wrapper always reports `true`, preserving their pre-M29 always-polling behavior exactly.)
- Step 2: Audit every non-`WindowEvent` redraw trigger — ✅ (found and closed the one real gap this step's own scoping named: a screen-reader-driven AccessKit `Action::Focus`/`Action::Click` (`ActionRequested`, delivered via `user_event`, not `window_event`) had no redraw wiring at all — added an explicit `request_redraw()` on the target window right after invoking `on_access_action`, so an assistive-technology-driven action still paints its own result under `ControlFlow::Wait`.)
- **Real bug found and fixed only by actually running every example against this change, not assumed:** a window opened with `max_frames: Some(_)` — this codebase's own dominant example/test pattern, "run exactly N frames then exit" — hung indefinitely under the first real implementation, since nothing kept requesting its next redraw once `on_frame` reported "not animating," so it never reached its own frame count at all. Confirmed the hard way: several examples timed out running the real workspace verification pass. Fixed by having `RedrawRequested` treat any window with `max_frames: Some(_)` as always-animating for `ControlFlow` purposes regardless of what `on_frame` itself reports — a bounded run needs to guarantee finishing in bounded wall-clock time; only a genuinely unbounded window (`max_frames: None`, a real interactive app) gets Phase 2's idle-CPU benefit at all. Documented directly in `run_windowed_multi`'s own doc comment so this doesn't get silently re-broken later.
- **Real, empirical idle-CPU verification, not assumed:** a scratch script opening a genuinely unbounded (`max_frames=None`) static window with no animation settled to ~0.8–1.3% CPU over several real seconds of wall-clock time with a real display attached (`ps -o pcpu`), consistent with `ControlFlow::Wait` genuinely taking effect rather than continuing to spin.

**Not scoped by this milestone:** partial/incremental repaint (only redrawing the changed region of the screen) — a materially larger change to `engine-render`'s own whole-tree paint walk, and not what "stop burning GPU on an idle screen" actually needs; this milestone's own dirty flag is coarse (whole-frame yes/no), matching the existing architecture's own already-whole-tree-per-frame paint model. Window resize handling itself (`WindowEvent::Resized`) also stays out of scope — a real, pre-existing, separate gap this milestone's own investigation surfaced but didn't introduce.

**M29 — Render Loop Dirty-Tracking is now fully complete.** `engine-platform`'s render loop no longer redraws unconditionally every frame: a genuinely idle window with no animation and no input settles into `ControlFlow::Wait` and stops doing real per-frame paint/GPU work entirely, verified both by direct code review and by an empirical CPU measurement. Full `cargo check`/clippy `-D warnings`/fmt clean across every touched crate; `cargo test --workspace --release` clean (`engine-core` 146, up from 145; every other crate's suite unchanged); `maturin develop --release` + pytest (187 passed, unchanged, 1 pre-existing skip), all 29 examples (re-run twice, the second time after the `max_frames` fix, to confirm the hang was genuinely resolved and not intermittent), and the showcase demo (which exercises real click/keyboard/drag interaction end to end, not just a static scene) all confirmed clean with the real display. See `PLAN.md`/`LOG.md`.

---

**Just closed:** M29 — Render Loop Dirty-Tracking (both phases): a centralized `Tree`-level dirty flag skipping real per-frame layout/GPU work on idle frames (Phase 1), and a genuine switch to `ControlFlow::Wait` when no window is animating, with explicit redraw-request wiring at every real input path plus the AccessKit action path (Phase 2) -- closing the one item M28 deliberately left open, with a real `max_frames`-hang bug found and fixed along the way and an empirical idle-CPU measurement confirming the real-world effect, not just the code review.

Real, separate finding made while scoping a proposed M30 around three specific gaps (overlay dismissal, `VirtualList` scroll wiring, a cross-window `Rc::ptr_eq` guard): direct verification found all three already real and working (overlay dismissal and the drop-zone highlight since M10, scroll dispatch already wired, both cross-window guards already present since M6/M10) -- this file's own Known Gaps section had drifted, corrected above rather than scoping a milestone for work that didn't need doing.
