## Milestone 30 — MD3 + Desktop Component Catalog (§5, §7, §8)

**Status: ⬜ Scoped, not started (2026-09-18).** User-directed: "Scope the component catalog and I want the full MD3 list and desktop only components identified in tre v1[/pyCopper]. Additionally, pair the Python type stubs for pyo3 API with each component... there are components that are mobile only. Do not include these components as this is a desktop only framework." PyPI publishing is explicitly *not* part of this milestone -- per the user's own direction it's deferred to be the very last thing done, once the Tesserae UI Framework itself is considered complete.

**Real research this scope is built on, not re-derived from scratch:** the mobile-vs-desktop component question was already answered with real, verified work in the sibling `pyCopper` project (`/home/phil/pyDev/projects/pyCopper`) -- a GPU-accelerated declarative desktop GUI framework for Python by the same author, with the identical explicit desktop-only design goal TRE v2 already commits to (§2's own Design Principles), which independently built a real MD3 widget catalog (58 `WidgetKind`s) and recorded its own findings in this session's memory graph. Reused directly rather than re-investigated: **bottom-anchored navigation (Navigation Bar, Bottom App Bar) and Bottom Sheet are explicitly mobile patterns in M3's own catalogue** -- Navigation Rail/Navigation Drawer and Side Sheet are the real desktop counterparts, kept in scope in their place. Desktop-adaptation rules that apply to every component below, not a phase of their own: pointer-precise hit targets, not MD3's 48dp minimum touch target; click-origin ripple, not touch-origin; no compact `<600dp` breakpoint; hover, focus rings and keyboard traversal, right-click context menus, cursor shape, visible scrollbars, and mouse text selection are first-class desktop affordances MD3 itself treats as secondary.

**Already real, not rebuilt by this milestone:** `Checkbox`, `Slider`, `TextField`, `Image`, `Icon`, `Canvas`, `VirtualList`, `Splitter` (all real `NodeKind`s already), and real 5-zone docking with live drag-and-drop (M4 Phase 9) -- already substantially more capable than pyCopper's own static-only `Dock`, nothing to add there. No dedicated `Button` exists yet -- today's examples hand-compose `Rect`+`Text`+ripple for anything button-shaped, a real gap Phase 1 closes.

**Real, important caveat found while researching pyCopper's own history, not assumed to transfer unchanged:** several of pyCopper's own "engine prerequisite" blockers (no diagonal-line primitive beyond a circular arc, no overlay/portal layer) were specific to *its own* custom SDF-shader renderer's real limitations. TRE v2's renderer (`vello_hybrid`/`kurbo`, real arbitrary bezier path and stroke support already) does not obviously share those constraints -- each phase below still needs its own real "does this need new engine-core/engine-render machinery" investigation when it's actually implemented, the same discipline every prior milestone in this file has already applied, not an assumption inherited from a different renderer's own history.

**Type stub convention, applies to every phase below:** each phase that adds real `Window.add_*`/`Node` methods or new state also updates `python/tre/__init__.pyi` with matching signatures in the *same* phase -- paired per phase per the user's own explicit instruction, not a separate end-of-milestone pass. Phase 0 below establishes the stub file and `py.typed` marker (confirmed via direct check: neither exists anywhere in `python/` today) against the *current* real API surface first, so every later phase extends an established file rather than inventing its own stub-authoring approach.

### Phase 0 — Type Stub Infrastructure ✅
- Step 1: `python/tre/py.typed` (PEP 561 marker) plus `python/tre/_core.pyi` covering every real, currently-shipped `Window`/`Node`/`App`/`View`/`CanvasContext` method and class — ✅ (stubs the compiled extension only, not `__init__.py` itself -- its own plain Python source (`Signal`/`ViewModel`) already gives full type inference, and a sibling `.pyi` for the same module would shadow it; `__init__.py`'s `from tre._core import ...` re-export line picks up the new stub's types automatically. **Two real bugs in the stub caught only by cross-checking against the actual Rust signatures, not assumed correct from memory:** `add_virtual_list`'s `materialize` callback returns a plain `(r, g, b, a)` color tuple, not a `Node` as first drafted -- confirmed via direct read of `set_virtual_list_window`'s own body, every materialized row is always a plain colored `Rect`, a real, stated limitation now documented in the stub itself, not silently wrong; `CanvasContext.stroke_path`'s real parameter order is `(points, color, width)`, not `(points, width, color, closed)` -- there is no `closed` parameter at all. **Real, pre-existing export gap found and fixed along the way:** `CanvasContext` was registered in the compiled `_core` module but never re-exported through the public `tre` package (`python/tre/__init__.py`) -- fixed, now importable as `from tre import CanvasContext` like every other real class, not just via the private `tre._core` path. Verified for real, not assumed: installed `mypy` into `.venv`, ran `mypy --strict` against a script exercising real correct usage of every stubbed method (clean) plus four deliberately wrong calls guarded by `# type: ignore` (all four confirmed genuinely needed -- `--warn-unused-ignores` stayed silent, and stripping the ignores made mypy report the exact 4 expected errors with accurate messages, proof the stubs carry real type information rather than silently accepting anything). Full pytest suite (187 passed, unchanged) and all 29 examples re-run clean after the `__init__.py` export change.)

### Phase 1 — Actions ✅
- Step 1: `Button`, MD3's five real variants (Elevated/Filled/Filled Tonal/Outlined/Text) — ✅ (`Window.add_button`, `crates/engine-py/src/window_factory.rs`: a `Rect` container, corner radius `height / 2.0` -- MD3's own "Full" shape family -- with one centered `Text` label child, colors resolved per variant through a new `ThemeState::role` general resolver, `window.rs`, widened beyond the old single-field `on_surface()`. **Two real, genuine engine gaps found and fixed along the way, not assumed already solved:** (1) `engine-render`'s text-shaping pipeline hardcoded `parley::Alignment::Start` unconditionally -- a real, verified blocker for any centered label -- fixed with a new universal `TextState.align: TextAlign` capability (Start/Center/End), proven with a real pixel-readback test (`engine-render/tests/text_align.rs`) showing ink genuinely moves off the left edge under `Center`, the same "universal capability, not component-specific" precedent `PaintProperties.border_color`/`border_width` set earlier this same phase for the Outlined variant's real stroked border (`engine-render/tests/border_paint.rs`). (2) A real, confirmed click-dispatch bug caught by `tests/test_button.py`'s own functional click test, not inferred: `Tree::hit_test_at` recurses into children first with no ancestor bubbling anywhere in `dispatch`, so Button's own centered `Text` child -- sized to fill the container's inner content width -- silently ate every click meant for its parent's registered handler. Fixed at the root: a bare `NodeKind::Text` label now never independently claims a hit, always deferring to whatever's behind it (`tree.rs`); confirmed via grep first that no existing example or test anywhere relies on a standalone `add_text` node being directly clickable, so this closes a real dead end rather than risking a new one -- a future standalone clickable label (`Link`, Phase 8) gets its own dedicated `NodeKind` when that phase investigates it, the same "each interactive component is its own real `NodeKind`" precedent `Checkbox`/`Slider`/`TextField` already establish. Un-themed default uses real Material 3 baseline hex tokens (`ButtonBaseline`), the same "real historical default, not black" contract `Checkbox`'s white mark/`Slider`'s gray track already establish. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (39 binaries, all green, `text_align.rs`/`border_paint.rs` included), `cargo clippy -D warnings` and `cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 197 passed/1 skipped (10 new in `test_button.py`, zero regressions), all 31 examples (including the new `examples/button.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/button.py` plus a deliberate-error probe confirming the new `.pyi` stub carries real type information.)
- Step 2: Icon Button — ✅ (`Window.add_icon_button`, real MD3 anatomy: a `Rect` container (corner radius `size / 2.0`) with one centered `Icon` child, `Button`'s own container-color machinery reused verbatim (`resolve_button_colors`) rather than a near-duplicate color table. Real MD3 naming fidelity kept even though the underlying colors are shared: Icon Button's four real variants are Filled/Filled Tonal/Outlined/**Standard**, not `Button`'s five -- there is no "Elevated Icon Button" in MD3's own vocabulary, and `"standard"` (not `"text"`) is translated to `resolve_button_colors`'s `"text"` colors at the call site, not aliased inside it, so `add_button`'s own error message never lists a name that isn't real for `Button` itself. Icon stays a fixed real MD3 token (24dp) regardless of the container's own `size`, centered on both axes via `justify_content`/`align_items` -- no `TextAlign`-equivalent needed, since an `Icon`'s own box is already exactly its glyph's bounds. **Proactively closed the identical hit-test gap `Button` (Step 1) found, before it could bite:** `NodeKind::Icon(_) => false` added to `Tree::hit_test_at` alongside the earlier `NodeKind::Text` fix, confirmed first via grep that `demo/showcase.py`'s only `add_icon` usage (its icon gallery) is purely decorative, never independently clicked -- `tests/test_icon_button.py`'s own click-dispatch test passed on the first run because of this, not found by a second failure. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (39 binaries green), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 207 passed/1 skipped (10 new in `test_icon_button.py`, zero regressions), all 32 examples (including the new `examples/icon_button.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/icon_button.py`.)
- Step 3: FAB and Extended FAB — ✅ (`Window.add_fab`/`Window.add_extended_fab`. Real MD3 color-variant system found to be genuinely distinct from `Button`'s own, verified against Material Web's own real component token source (`tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-fab-surface.scss`/`_md-comp-fab-primary.scss`) rather than assumed transferable -- `FAB` has its own four variants (Surface/Primary/Secondary/Tertiary), not `Button`'s five, with Surface (the real default) resolving `container` from `surface_container_high`/`icon` from `primary`, and Primary/Secondary/Tertiary each following the `<name>_container`/`on_<name>_container` pattern MD3 uses everywhere else in the spec. `FAB`'s three real sizes each pair their own independently-specified real shape token, not one proportional formula guessed from a single data point (Small 40dp/`corner-medium` 12dp, Default 56dp/`corner-large` 16dp, Large 96dp/`corner-extra-large` 28dp -- the three real ratios are close but not identical, so a formula would have been a fabricated approximation, not real fidelity). Every `FAB` variant carries a genuine rest-state elevation (level 3, `PaintProperties.elevation`'s own real level-index scale) -- `FAB` is inherently elevated in MD3, unlike `Icon Button`. `Extended FAB` reuses `FAB`'s own color/elevation system with a real icon-plus-label anatomy (icon optional, matching real MD3's own label-only variant -- the real, verified padding genuinely differs between the two cases: 16dp leading with an icon vs. 20dp without, both confirmed against Material Web's own real CSS, not invented), one real fixed size (56dp tall, `corner-large`). Real, small factoring finding along the way: `add_icon`'s own curated-icon-name-to-`BezPath` lookup, previously only duplicated once (by `add_icon_button`, Step 2), crossed this project's own established "2+ call sites" threshold for a shared helper once `add_fab`/`add_extended_fab` needed it a third and fourth time -- factored into `resolve_icon_path`, all four real call sites (`add_icon` included) now share it. `Md3Baseline` (renamed from `ButtonBaseline`, no longer pointing at one consumer) gained `primary_container`/`on_primary_container`/`tertiary_container`/`on_tertiary_container`/`surface_container_high` for `FAB`'s own real un-themed fallback. **Proactively applied the same hit-test-composite discipline a third time:** `Extended FAB`'s own `test_fab.py` click-dispatch test covers a composite `Icon`+`Text` node at once, the real combined case `Tree::hit_test_at`'s two earlier fixes (Steps 1-2) both needed to already be in place for. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (39 binaries green), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 227 passed/1 skipped (20 new in `test_fab.py`, zero regressions), all 33 examples (including the new `examples/fab.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/fab.py`.)
- Step 4: Segmented Button — ✅ (`Window.add_segmented_button`. **Real, honest finding: the scoped "docking tab anatomy" precedent didn't transfer** -- checked directly (`dock.rs`'s own `set_active_tab`) before assuming it would, and docking's own "active tab" is purely logical panel-visibility switching, no real shared-border/divider visual anatomy to reuse at all; genuine independent design was needed, exactly the outcome this milestone's own scope note already allowed for. **One real, new universal engine capability, not a Segmented-Button-specific hack:** `PaintProperties.corner_radii_override: Option<[f64; 4]>` -- MD3's own real anatomy rounds a group's first/last segments only on their outer edge, square on the edge touching the next segment, which the existing single-scalar `corner_radius` can't express; kurbo's own `RoundedRect::new` already accepts independent per-corner radii natively (confirmed via direct source read of the pinned `kurbo 0.13.1`, not assumed), so this exposes an existing real primitive rather than inventing new geometry. Proven with a real pixel-readback test (`engine-render/tests/corner_radii_paint.rs`): a 30px top-left-only radius genuinely clears a pixel 2px from that corner while the other three (radius 0) stay square right up to their own corners. Real anatomy: one shared outline frame (`corner-full`, 1px real outline), a real 1px divider `Rect` between each pair of adjacent segments, per-segment selected/unselected paint (selected: `secondary_container` background, `on_secondary_container` label, a real 18dp checkmark -- deliberately smaller than every other component's 24dp icon token, confirmed from Material Web's own real token source, `tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-outlined-segmented-button.scss`, not assumed consistent; unselected: transparent background, `on_surface` label, reusing `ThemeState::on_surface()` directly rather than a new baseline role). **Real, explicit design decision, not an oversight:** group-exclusivity is deliberately not built into this method -- `BUILD_TRACKER.md`'s own Phase 2 scope for the future `Radio Button` already states "group-exclusivity is application state... not engine-owned," reused here verbatim; the method paints only the real *initial* selected state from `selected` and returns every segment's own real `Node`, with `examples/segmented_button.py` demonstrating an app wiring up real live re-toggling itself using nothing but `set_on_click`/`Node.animate`. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (40 binaries green, `corner_radii_paint.rs` included), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 234 passed/1 skipped (7 new in `test_segmented_button.py`, zero regressions), all 34 examples (including the new `examples/segmented_button.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/segmented_button.py`.)
- Step 5: `.pyi` stubs for every method/class Steps 1-4 add — ✅ (satisfied incrementally, not as a separate deferred pass -- each of Steps 1-4 added its own real `.pyi` stub entry in the same step that added the method, per this milestone's own stated per-phase pairing convention; `add_button`/`add_icon_button`/`add_fab`/`add_extended_fab`/`add_segmented_button` are all present in `python/tre/_core.pyi`, each verified with `mypy --strict` against that step's own example.)

### Phase 2 — Selection ✅
- Step 1: Radio Button — ✅ (a real, new first-class `NodeKind::RadioButton` (`RadioButtonState { selected, select_progress: Animated<f64>, unselected_tint, selected_tint }`), `CheckboxState`'s own real shape mirrored exactly: `selected` is plain app-owned state, `select_progress` the engine-driven visual consequence, animated toward `1.0`/`0.0` -- `Tree::tick_all` needed its own explicit new ticking arm (an `if let`, not an exhaustive `match`, so this doesn't fail loudly on its own; found by deliberately checking `Checkbox`'s/`Slider`'s own real precedent rather than assuming the compiler would catch a missing tick arm). One real, necessary anatomy difference from `Checkbox`: MD3's real radio button is a stroked *ring* (not a filled box) whose own color genuinely transitions between `unselected_tint`/`selected_tint` as it toggles, not just a static box with an appearing/disappearing mark -- painted via `Interpolate for peniko::Color` (already real since §5's own animation core) using `select_progress` as the blend factor, so the ring's color rides the identical timeline the inner dot's scale-in already does. Real MD3 anatomy verified: a real accessibility mirror added too (`Tree`'s `access_node.set_toggled`, matching `Checkbox`'s own precedent exactly, checked first that `Checkbox` itself sets no explicit AccessKit `Role` either, so this stays honest parity, not new asymmetry). **Real, explicit, stated scope limit, not an oversight:** unlike `Checkbox`/`Slider`/`TextField`, a radio button created *before* `Window.set_theme` is not retroactively re-tinted by a later `set_theme` call -- `Tree::set_all_component_tints` deliberately reuses one shared `on_surface` tint across every component (its own already-documented scope choice), but a radio button genuinely needs two different real roles (`outline`/`primary`), which that single-color mechanism can't express without contradicting its own stated simplification; every radio button still starts correctly themed at construction time. `Node.set_selected`/`get_selected` and the `"select_progress"` arm of `animate`/`get` all mirror `Checkbox`'s own exact real API shape. Group-exclusivity deliberately left to the app (the real point this step's own scope text already named), demonstrated end to end in `examples/radio_button.py`. Verified: full workspace `cargo test --release` (41 binaries green, `engine-render/tests/radio_button_paint.rs` included -- proves the ring's real color interpolation and the dot's real scale-in, not just that the match arm compiles), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 243 passed/1 skipped (9 new in `test_radio_button.py`, zero regressions), all 35 examples (including the new `examples/radio_button.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/radio_button.py`.)

Real MD3 anatomy verified against Material Web's own token source (`tokens/versions/v0_192/_md-comp-switch.scss`) before any code was written: track 52dp × 32dp, `corner-full`; unselected track = `surface_container_highest` fill + a real, separate `outline`-role stroke (a genuinely different color from the fill, not the same role twice); selected track = `primary` fill, no stroke; handle grows from a real 16dp (unselected, `outline` tint) to 24dp (selected, `on_primary` tint) as it toggles -- not just a color change the way `RadioButton`'s dot is, a real size change too. The handle's own slide-and-grow formula (`cx = h*0.5 + t*(w-h)`, `handle_radius = h*(0.25+0.125*t)`) is derived directly from those real verified ratios, not fitted after the fact: unselected padding-from-edge (`(32-16)/2=8`) plus unselected handle radius (`8`) and selected padding (`(32-24)/2=4`) plus selected handle radius (`12`) both sum to the identical `16 = h*0.5`, which is what makes one clean symmetric travel formula exact for both ends, confirmed algebraically before committing to it rather than assumed to simplify. The track's own real separate outline stroke fades out via opacity (`1.0 - toggle_progress`) as the switch turns on, reusing the exact real "conditionally-drawn stroke, not the universal `PaintProperties` border" approach -- a switch's outline is genuinely a `Switch`-owned concept (a fourth tint no other component needed), not the shared `border_color`/`border_width` fields `Button`'s Outlined variant uses. `add_switch`'s own real, deliberate anatomy difference from `add_radio_button`: two real dimension parameters (`width`/`height`), not one shared `size`, since a real MD3 switch track is genuinely non-square (52×32), unlike a radio button's real circle. Same real accessibility mirror (`set_toggled`) and the same real, honest live-re-theming scope limit `RadioButton` already states (five distinct real roles here, further from `set_all_component_tints`'s single-shared-tint design than even `RadioButton`'s two were -- every switch still starts correctly themed at construction). `Node.set_on`/`get_on` and the `"toggle_progress"` arm of `animate`/`get` mirror `Checkbox`/`RadioButton`'s own exact real API shape a third time. ### Phase 4 — Overlay-Dependent: Dialog, Snackbar, Side Sheet ✅
- Step 1: Dialog, a real modal — ✅ (`Window.add_dialog`/`open_dialog`/`close_dialog`. **Real, confirmed engine-core gap, investigated directly before writing any code, not assumed solved:** `Tree::dismiss_overlays_outside` only ever blocked a background click when it *also* dismissed the overlay — there was no way to express "block interaction with everything behind this overlay, without dismissing it on an outside click," which a real modal dialog needs (a real dialog only closes via its own explicit action or Escape, never an accidental outside click). Fixed with a new, purely additive `OverlayMeta.modal: bool` field (`false` a true no-op, verified by a new regression test proving `modal: false` preserves the exact old dismiss-lets-clicks-through behavior) plus a new read-only `Tree::press_blocked_by_modal_overlay` helper and one new early-return branch in `dispatch`'s `PointerPressed` arm, placed right after the existing `dismiss_overlays_outside` check — two new `engine-core` unit tests both passed on the first attempt. All 9 pre-existing `OverlayMeta { ... }` construction sites across the codebase updated with `modal: false` to preserve their exact existing behavior. Real MD3 tokens verified against Material Web's own token source (`_md-comp-dialog.scss`) before writing any code: `surface_container_high` panel, real `corner-extra-large` shape (28dp), a real rest-state elevation (level 3), Headline Small (24sp/400 weight, `on_surface`) for the headline and Body Medium (14sp/400 weight, `on_surface_variant`) for the supporting text — a real, easy-to-miss distinction the first draft got wrong (see below); `Md3Baseline` gained `SCRIM`. **Real positioning technique, not a new primitive:** a real dialog is always centered, unlike every other overlay in this catalog (anchor-relative-below) — solved by reusing `open_overlay`'s existing anchor-relative math with a synthetic zero-size anchor pinned at the window origin (resolving to a `(0,0)` inset) combined with ordinary flex `justify_content`/`align_items: Center` on a full-window scrim, avoiding the need for a new/third positioning primitive; the synthetic anchor is tracked via `OverlayMeta.anchor` and explicitly `Tree::remove`d in `close_dialog`, closing a real per-open/close node-leak risk. **Two real bugs caught in my own first draft before it was ever compiled, not shipped and found later:** the draft had no theme resolution at all for `scrim`/`panel`/headline colors (hardcoded baseline constants, inconsistent with every other component this milestone), used `on_surface_variant` for the *headline* when real MD3 says headline uses plain `on_surface` (only the supporting text uses the variant role), and took optional `x`/`y` kwargs that would have silently defeated the deliberate flex-centering design if a caller ever passed them — all three fixed before compiling. Proven end to end through the real Python FFI, not just that the API doesn't raise: `tests/test_dialog.py`'s own click-dispatch test proves a real click on a background node is genuinely swallowed while a modal dialog is open and reaches its handler again once closed — the real, concrete proof the new `OverlayMeta.modal` fix actually works through the full stack. **Real, honest correction to this file's own Top Metrics table, found while updating it for this step:** M30's row had stayed at "0%, not started" through all of Phases 0-3 actually completing — a real drift from this project's own stated "update Top Metrics every time" convention, corrected now rather than left silently wrong. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, 2 new `tree.rs` unit tests included)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 308 passed/1 skipped (7 new in `test_dialog.py`, zero regressions), all 44 examples (including the new `examples/dialog.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/dialog.py`.)
- Step 2: Snackbar — ✅ (`Window.add_snackbar`/`open_snackbar`/`close_snackbar`. Real MD3 anatomy verified against Material Web's own token source (`_md-comp-snackbar.scss`) before writing any code: `inverse_surface` fill, real `corner-extra-small` shape (4dp), a real rest-state elevation (level 3), 48dp single-line height. **Real, confirmed token-chain finding, not guessed:** the action label's own real color role, `inverse-primary`, has no direct hex in the snackbar's own token file — traced one level deeper through `_md-sys-color.scss` (`inverse-primary` = the light-theme palette's `primary80` tone) and `_md-ref-palette.scss` (`primary80` = `#D0BCFF` for the real M3 baseline seed, `primary40` = `#6750A4`, matching this file's own already-real `Md3Baseline::PRIMARY`) — `Md3Baseline` gains `INVERSE_PRIMARY` from this real chain. Supporting text and the action label both reuse already-declared shared-role constants (`DIALOG_BODY_FONT_SIZE`/`_WEIGHT` for Body Medium, `BUTTON_LABEL_FONT_SIZE`/`_WEIGHT`/`_LINE_HEIGHT` for Label Large) rather than new per-component constants for roles this catalog already has real values for. **Real, deliberate anatomy departure from every prior component in this catalog: `add_snackbar` returns up to three independent real `Node`s, not one.** `Segmented Button`'s own real precedent (Phase 1 Step 4, `Vec<Node>`, one per independently-selectable segment) already established that a genuinely multi-interactive-region composite returns multiple real `Node`s — `Chip`'s own `removable` trailing icon (re-checked directly before choosing this shape) is real precedent for the *other* case, a purely decorative sub-icon never independently clickable; a real MD3 snackbar's action button must be independently clickable from the rest of the snackbar (which isn't itself a button), so the decorative-icon shape doesn't fit here. Returns `(container, action, close)`, the last two `None` unless requested — proven for real, not just that the API doesn't raise: `tests/test_snackbar.py`'s own click-dispatch tests prove the action and close nodes each reach their own independently-registered handler, and that clicking close doesn't also fire the action's handler. Real positioning reuse, not a new primitive: `open_snackbar` reuses `open_dialog`'s own synthetic-zero-size-anchor technique a second time, placed at the real desktop bottom-left corner (this milestone's own already-established desktop-adaptation rule for MD3's mobile-only full-width-at-bottom anatomy) instead of the origin. `modal: false`/`dismiss_on_outside_click: false`/`dismiss_on_escape: false` — a real snackbar is a passive notification, not an interaction-blocking overlay, and isn't dismissed by an accidental outside click or Escape. **Real, stated, deliberate scope limit, not a silently missing feature:** this engine has no timer/scheduler primitive anywhere (confirmed by grep across `engine-core`/`engine-py` before scoping this step — zero hits), so a real auto-dismiss-after-duration is the app's own responsibility, matching Design Principle 6 ("engine gives primitives, app composes behavior") — `examples/snackbar.py` closes it explicitly rather than relying on any engine-side timeout. **Real, honest caveat:** horizontal padding/gap/action-width/desktop-anchor-margins have no discrete token in the snackbar's own token file (confirmed by the same fetch that found the real color/shape/elevation/type tokens) — reasonable, MD3-consistent values, the identical caveat `Dialog`'s own padding constants already carry. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, unchanged — a pure composition plus existing overlay primitives needed no new `engine-render` capability or pixel test), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 318 passed/1 skipped (10 new in `test_snackbar.py`, zero regressions), all 45 examples (including the new `examples/snackbar.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/snackbar.py`.)
- Step 3: Side Sheet (the real desktop counterpart to Bottom Sheet, which stays excluded as a mobile pattern per this milestone's own scope) — ✅ (`Window.add_side_sheet`/`open_side_sheet`/`close_side_sheet`. **Real, confirmed finding before writing any code:** Material Web has no dedicated side-sheet token file at all (a direct fetch 404s, the identical real finding `Menu`'s own token investigation already hit) — reuses `Navigation Drawer`'s own real tokens instead (`_md-comp-navigation-drawer.scss`), the structurally closest real MD3 component (an edge-docked elevated panel): real `Standard` container color `surface`/elevation `level0` (flat, embedded in the layout, never floating) vs. real `Modal` container color `surface_container_low`/elevation `level1` (a real shadow, since it floats over content); `container-shape` `corner-large-end` (16dp, matching `FAB`'s own already-confirmed real `corner-large` value, rounded only on the two corners *away* from the docked edge — reuses `PaintProperties.corner_radii_override`, `Segmented Button`'s own already-real universal capability, not a new per-corner primitive); real `container-width` (360px) and `container-height` (100%, `self.height` when not overridden). **Real, deliberate behavioral fork by variant, not one uniform shape forced onto two genuinely different real MD3 lifecycles:** `Standard` (`modal=False`, the default) is a real layout participant, not an overlay at all — attached immediately to `self.root`, the identical real precedent `add_card` already establishes (a plain positioned `Rect`, optional `x`/`y`, the app re-parents it into its own layout via the already-generic `Node.add_child`/`Tree::try_add_child`, or docks it via the existing real 5-zone `Dock` from M4 Phase 9, confirmed still the most capable real docking mechanism this codebase has) — no scrim, no blocking, no open/close lifecycle needed; `open_side_sheet`/`close_side_sheet` are real, explicit, documented no-ops on a standard sheet, proven by a dedicated test. `Modal` (`modal=True`) genuinely floats over content and must block interaction behind it — reuses `Dialog`'s own real full-window scrim (`DIALOG_SCRIM_OPACITY`) and its real `OverlayMeta.modal` capability from Step 1 verbatim, anchored to the real right edge (flex `justify_content: FLEX_END`, full height) instead of `Dialog`'s own centered placement; `open_side_sheet` reuses `open_dialog`'s own synthetic-zero-size-anchor technique a third time this milestone. Proven end to end through the real Python FFI, not just that the API doesn't raise: `tests/test_side_sheet.py`'s own click-dispatch test proves a real background click is genuinely swallowed while a modal side sheet is open and reaches its handler again once closed (reusing Step 1's own real proof technique against a second, independently-built modal overlay), and a separate test proves a standard sheet re-parents cleanly into an app's own shell via `Node.add_child` with no duplicate-child panic. **Real Rust finding along the way, not shipped broken:** `Size<taffy::Dimension>` doesn't implement `Default` in the pinned taffy version (`Dimension` itself has no `Default` impl) — first hit during Step 2, this step's own `..Default::default()` usages were written avoiding it from the start. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, unchanged — a pure composition plus existing overlay/corner-radius primitives needed no new `engine-render` capability or pixel test), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 327 passed/1 skipped (9 new in `test_side_sheet.py`, zero regressions), all 46 examples (including the new `examples/side_sheet.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/side_sheet.py`.)

### Phase 6 — Lists & Disclosure ✅
- Step 1: List / ListItem — ✅ (`Window.add_list_item`/`add_list`, a plain, non-virtualized list for small real collections -- `VirtualList` (already real since M4/M8) stays the real choice for large ones, not two unrelated mechanisms, this step's own real scope note now confirmed rather than just asserted. Real per-item anatomy reuses List Item's own token file, already investigated once (Phase 2 Step 4, `Menu`'s own real finding that Material Web has no dedicated menu-item token file and reuses List Item's directly) — `MENU_ITEM_*` constants reused verbatim, plus a real, confirmed new finding this step made: the *two-line* variant (72dp, vs. the already-known one-line 56dp) for a headline + real supporting-text second line, Body Medium (`DIALOG_BODY_FONT_SIZE`/`_WEIGHT` reused directly), `on_surface_variant` — the same real role trailing supporting text (metadata) also uses. No divider token exists in the list's own file (confirmed by the same fetch) — an app composes one itself via the already-real `add_divider`, the same "engine gives primitives, app composes" contract every other bare-container component in this catalog already has. **Real, deliberate design applying `Navigation Rail`/`Tabs`'s own hit-test lesson proactively this time, not found the hard way a third time:** the two-line variant's headline+supporting-text block is a real, necessary `NodeKind::Container` wrapper (two stacked `Text` children need *some* grouping node) — `Tree::set_hit_testable(text_block, false)` applied immediately on insertion, verified again by this step's own click-dispatch test rather than assumed safe from precedent alone; both the one-line and two-line independent-click tests passed on the first run. **Real, deliberate reuse, not a new re-parenting mechanism:** `add_list` moves each item (`Tree::detach` then `add_child`) into the returned frame, the identical real mechanism `build_menu` already established. **Real bug self-caught before compiling, not shipped broken:** a first draft of `add_list` summed each item's own `Tree::layout(...).size.height` to compute the frame's own height — a real mistake, since an item's `Layout` is only meaningful after a real `compute_layout` pass has run at least once, which this method has no guarantee of; fixed to use `auto()` for the frame's own height instead, the identical real "definite cross-axis, auto main-axis sizes from children" pattern `AppShell`'s own `content` region (`flex_grow: 1.0`, no explicit height) already uses. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, unchanged — a pure composition reusing `Menu Item`'s own real anatomy plus the existing `hit_testable` primitive needed no new `engine-render` capability), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 385 passed/1 skipped (11 new in `test_list.py`, zero regressions), all 52 examples (including the new `examples/list.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/list.py` (after fixing a real inline-lambda type-inference finding by switching to the established factory-function pattern).)
- Step 2: Accordion — ✅ (`Window.add_accordion_header`. No official M3 component page (confirmed by pyCopper's own real research against the full M3 reference library, not re-checked here since the finding transfers directly; grounded instead in the Lists guideline's own "expand and collapse in a folder-like manner" text). **Real, deliberate scope: only the header is this step's own real new anatomy** — the collapsible content region has no distinctive MD3 styling of its own, so no dedicated `add_accordion_content` method was invented; the app composes it from any existing container (`add_rect`/`add_card`), the identical "engine gives primitives, app composes" contract `Card` itself already has. Header reuses List Item's own real anatomy exactly (`MENU_ITEM_*`, the same real "no dedicated token file, reuse List Item's" pattern `Menu`/`List` already established) — 56dp height, `on_surface` headline (Label Large), a trailing 24dp chevron (`on_surface_variant`). **Real, confirmed engine limitation found and honestly worked around, not silently assumed solved:** `Node.animate("transform", ...)` only ever composes translate+scale (`extract_translate_scale`'s own real, stated shape since M6 Phase 2) — there is no rotation capability exposed to Python at all, so a literal spinning chevron isn't buildable through the existing API. The real, working substitute: `Affine::scale(-1.0)` (a uniform negative scale, flipping both axes) is mathematically identical to a 180° rotation for any point-symmetric glyph, and the curated chevron is exactly that — `Node.animate("transform", (0.0, 0.0, -1.0))` on the real, independently-returned `chevron` genuinely flips it into a convincing "expanded" orientation, reusing scale (already real) rather than needing a new rotation primitive; `Node.get` has no way to read a transform's own resulting value back (not among its real scalar-only properties, the same "no pixel/value readback from Python" limitation this codebase already has elsewhere), so this is proven by the call succeeding end to end, not by reading the matrix back. **Real, confirmed asset addition, not shipped without one:** MD3's real `expand_more` chevron icon didn't exist in this project's curated set (only 8 icons existed: home/search/menu/close/check/arrow_back/add/settings) — fetched its real, verbatim SVG path data directly from the identical Google CDN `engine-md3`'s own icon module already cites, the ninth curated icon, growing the set exactly the way its own doc comment says it will ("additive... when a real need asks for more"); its own real parse-as-BezPath test covers it automatically, no new test needed. **Real API gap found while writing the example, not shipped silently wrong:** `Node.remove()` is full, irreversible destruction (`Tree::remove`), not a detach — this codebase exposes no Python-level "hide without destroying" for an arbitrary node the way `Tree::close_overlay` already has internally for real overlays; `examples/accordion.py` toggles the content region's own real `opacity` instead (already-general, avoids the destroy pitfall entirely). Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (`engine-core`/`engine-md3` unaffected in count, `engine-md3`'s own icon-parsing test automatically covers the new glyph)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 390 passed/1 skipped (5 new in `test_accordion.py`, zero regressions), all 53 examples (including the new `examples/accordion.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/accordion.py`.)
- Step 3: Tree View — ✅ (`Window.add_tree_node`, the identical real grounding `Accordion` already established (no official M3 page, the Lists guideline's own "expand and collapse" text), applied recursively. Real, deliberate design: a tree row is `Accordion`'s own header anatomy again (`MENU_ITEM_*`/`ACCORDION_CHEVRON_ICON` reused verbatim, not re-declared), with one real, new addition a flat accordion header never needed — a per-node `depth: usize` real left-indent (24dp per level, not a discrete MD3 token, the identical honest caveat `Dialog`'s own padding constants carry), the one real, load-bearing difference "recursively" actually means here. Real, honest design choice: `leaf: bool` omits the chevron entirely for a childless node — a leaf has nothing to expand, matching real desktop file-browser convention, not left for the app to fake with an invisible one. Returns `(header, chevron)`, `chevron` `None` for a leaf; toggling reuses the identical real contract `Accordion`'s own header already has (`Node.animate("transform", (0.0, 0.0, -1.0))`, the same real `Affine::scale(-1.0)`-as-180°-flip substitute, this engine still having no rotation primitive). **Real, deliberate regression check, not assumed safe from `Accordion`'s own precedent alone:** a dedicated test proves a deeply-nested (`depth=3`) row's own extra left padding doesn't reintroduce a hit-test problem — passed on the first run, confirming the added indentation composes cleanly with the already-working header anatomy. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, unchanged — a pure composition reusing `Accordion`'s own real anatomy needed no new `engine-render` capability), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 399 passed/1 skipped (9 new in `test_tree_view.py`, zero regressions), all 54 examples (including the new `examples/tree_view.py`, a small file-browser-shaped tree) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/tree_view.py`.)
- Step 4: `.pyi` stubs for every method/class Steps 1-3 add — ✅ (satisfied incrementally, not as a separate deferred pass — each of Steps 1-3 added its own real `.pyi` stub entry in the same step that added the method; `add_list_item`/`add_list`/`add_accordion_header`/`add_tree_node` are all present in `python/tre/_core.pyi`, each verified with `mypy --strict` against that step's own example. **Phase 6 is now fully complete, all 4 steps.**)

### Phase 7 — Date & Time ✅
- Step 1: Date Picker — ✅ (`Window.add_date_picker_day`, the real *docked* variant's own day-cell anatomy — the real desktop-adaptation choice over the mobile-oriented *modal* full dialog variant, the identical real "docked over full-screen" precedent `Search View` already made. Real anatomy verified against Material Web's own token source (`_md-comp-date-picker-docked.scss`): 48×48dp, `corner-full` (a real circle). Selected: `primary` fill, `on_primary` label. Today (not selected): a real 1dp `primary` outline (`PaintProperties.border_color`/`border_width`, already-real since Phase 1 Step 1), `primary` label, no fill. Neither: no fill/border, `on_surface` label — `on_surface_variant` instead for a day belonging to an adjacent month (a real, confirmed distinct role, not assumed identical to the plain unselected case). Label reuses Body Large's own already-declared constants (`SEARCH_INPUT_FONT_SIZE`/`_WEIGHT`, the identical real MD3 type role `Search Bar`'s own input text already uses). **Real, deliberate scope, not a partial build:** only the day *cell* is this step's own real new anatomy — a real calendar grid needs real date arithmetic (month lengths, weekday-of-month, leap years), genuinely application logic with zero real MD3-specific content, already trivially available via Python's own `datetime`/`calendar` modules; no engine-owned calendar primitive was invented for something that isn't actually a rendering/interaction concern, the identical real "engine gives primitives, app composes" contract `Card`/`Accordion`'s own content region already has. Proven for real, not just asserted: `test_a_real_month_grid_of_day_cells_can_be_built_from_python_date_math` builds a genuine September 2026 calendar (30 real days, via `calendar.monthcalendar`) using nothing but this one primitive, and `examples/date_picker.py` positions the resulting cells into a real 7-column grid on screen. Real, deliberate state precedence: `selected` wins over `today` when both are `True` (a selected today still shows the filled circle, not the outline) — both are independent booleans the app computes from its own real date model, not mutually exclusive at the type level. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, unchanged — a pure composition reusing the already-real border/corner-radius primitives needed no new `engine-render` capability), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 407 passed/1 skipped (8 new in `test_date_picker.py`, zero regressions), all 55 examples (including the new `examples/date_picker.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/date_picker.py`.)
- Step 2: Time Picker — ✅ (`Window.add_time_input_field`/`add_period_selector`, the real MD3 *Time Input* variant (digital hour:minute entry). **Real, deliberate scope:** MD3's other real Time variant, the analog clock-face dial (`_md-comp-time-picker.scss`, confirmed to exist via the same real directory listing `Date Picker`'s own investigation already found), needs a genuinely new engine-core capability this catalog doesn't have anywhere — drag-to-angle gesture handling and converting a circular hit point into a time value — a real, separate, much larger undertaking than any per-component investigation this milestone has done so far; Time Input is the real, desktop-realistic scope this step actually built. Real field anatomy verified against Material Web's own token source (`_md-comp-time-input.scss`): 96×72dp, `surface_container_highest` fill, real `corner-small` shape — the identical real 8dp value `Chip`'s own `CHIP_CORNER_RADIUS` already confirmed for the same real token, reused directly. Label is Display Medium (2.8125rem = 45px, `weight-regular` = 400, traced through `_md-sys-typescale.scss`/`_md-ref-typeface.scss`), `on_surface` — a real, genuinely large numeral display, distinct from every other type role this catalog has used so far. **Real, deliberate reuse of `TextField`'s own existing real `NodeKind`, the identical real design `Search Bar` already established:** the hour/minute field is a genuine `NodeKind::TextField`, not a bare styled box — proven end to end by `tests/test_time_picker.py`'s own real `press_key`/`type_text`/`get_text` round-trip, not just that the node exists. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, unchanged — reusing `TextField`/`Chip`'s own real corner-radius and the already-real `tertiary_container` role needed no new `engine-render` capability), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 415 passed/1 skipped (8 new in `test_time_picker.py`, zero regressions), all 56 examples (including the new `examples/time_picker.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/time_picker.py`.)

### Phase 8 — Desktop Specialty (no official M3 page, real pyCopper precedent) ✅
- Step 1: Popover — ✅ (`Window.add_popover`, grounded in MD3's own real Rich Tooltip anatomy — pyCopper's own real prior research (quoted directly from its own component library, reused rather than re-researched), with the exact real token values still directly re-verified against Material Web's own token source (`_md-comp-rich-tooltip.scss`), not assumed to still match from a different codebase. Supporting text is Body Medium (`on_surface_variant`, `DIALOG_BODY_FONT_SIZE`/`_WEIGHT` reused directly). **Real, deliberate reuse of `Window.open_menu`/`close_menu` directly, the identical real design `Tooltip`/`Search View` already established, not new dedicated methods:** a real Popover is genuinely *persistent* — unlike the already-real Plain `Tooltip` (Phase 3 Step 5), which dismisses automatically on hover-exit, a Rich Tooltip stays open until the user interacts elsewhere or explicitly dismisses it, exactly the real behavior `open_menu`'s own `dismiss_on_outside_click: true` already gives for free. - Step 2: Link — ✅ (`Window.add_link`. MD3 has no official Link component page (confirmed by the same real per-directory-listing technique this whole milestone already uses — no `_md-comp-link*` file exists). Real, honest choice, not independently token-verified: `primary` is the well-established, near-universal real MD3 link-color convention, reused directly; label reuses Body Large's own already-declared constants (`SEARCH_INPUT_FONT_SIZE`/`_WEIGHT`, the identical real MD3 type role `Search Bar`'s own input text already uses) — ordinary running-text-sized content, not a control-button label. **Real, new engine-core capability this step fulfills, not invented fresh:** a genuine `NodeKind::Link`, closing a real, explicit commitment this codebase already made to itself back in Phase 1's own `Tree::hit_test_at` fix — a bare `Text` node deliberately never independently claims a hit (always deferring to its real interactive container), so a real standalone clickable label needs its own dedicated `NodeKind`, the same "each interactive component is its own real `NodeKind`" precedent `Checkbox`/`Slider`/`TextField` already established. `NodeKind::Link` reuses `TextState` verbatim as its own payload (identical real content/font shape to `Text`, only the variant tag differs) and needs zero new hit-test logic at all — by simply not matching `NodeKind::Text(_) => false`, it falls through to `hit_test_at`'s own existing `_ => rect_contains(...)` catch-all, independent hit-testing "for free." Proven directly at the Rust level with a new, dedicated `engine-core` unit test (`link_independently_claims_a_hit_where_text_would_defer`): the identical real geometry built twice, once with a bare `Text` child (defers, the parent claims the hit) and once with a `Link` child (claims it directly) — a real, concrete contrast, not assumed from the enum shape alone — then reproduced through the real Python FFI by `test_link.py`'s own equivalent test. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (`engine-core` 150 up from 149, the new direct `Link` hit-test unit test included)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 424 passed/1 skipped (4 new in `test_link.py`, zero regressions), all 58 examples (including the new `examples/link.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/link.py`.)
- Step 3: SpinBox, a numeric increment control — ✅ (`Window.add_spin_box`. Deliberately not named "Stepper" -- pyCopper's own real naming-risk finding: M3's own vocabulary already uses "Stepper" for a multi-step flow indicator, a completely different control; naming this correctly from the start avoids the same collision pyCopper caught and had to rename around mid-build. MD3 has no official page for either real name (confirmed via the same directory-listing technique this milestone already uses). Real, honest anatomy, not independently token-verified: the numeric field reuses the identical real `surface_container_highest`/`corner-small` convention `Time Input` already established for a small boxed numeric display, Body Large text. Increment/decrement reuse `Icon Button`'s own exact real anatomy (Phase 1 Step 2), `SEARCH_ICON_BUTTON_SIZE` reused directly. **Real, deliberate reuse of `TextField`'s own existing real `NodeKind` for the numeric display, the identical real design `Search Bar`/`Time Input` already established** -- proven end to end by `tests/test_spin_box.py`'s own real `press_key`/`type_text`/`get_text` round-trip. **Real, confirmed asset addition:** the tenth curated icon, `remove` (a real minus glyph, fetched verbatim from the same real Google CDN, pairing with the already-curated `add`), needed to pair with `add`'s own "+" for a genuine decrement affordance -- its own real parse-as-BezPath test covers it automatically. Returns `(field, decrement, increment)`, the app owns the value's own real numeric semantics entirely (Design Principle 6 -- the engine has no notion of bounds or step size). Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, unchanged -- reusing `TextField`/`Icon Button`'s own real anatomy needed no new `engine-render` capability), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 429 passed/1 skipped (5 new in `test_spin_box.py`, zero regressions), all 59 examples (including the new `examples/spin_box.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/spin_box.py`.)
- Step 4: Pagination — ✅ (`Window.add_pagination`. MD3 has no official Pagination page (confirmed via the same real per-directory-listing technique this whole milestone already uses). Real, honest anatomy, not independently token-verified: each page indicator reuses the identical real 40dp circular footprint `Search Bar`/`Top App Bar`/`SpinBox` already settled on (`SEARCH_ICON_BUTTON_SIZE`, `corner_radius = size / 2.0`). Selected: `primary` fill, `on_primary` label — the identical real selected-state pair `Date Picker`'s own day cell already uses. Unselected: transparent fill, `on_surface_variant` label — the same real convention `Segmented Button`/`Chip`/`Navigation Rail` already established. Prev/next reuse `Icon Button`'s own exact real anatomy a fourth time this catalog already has, with the newly curated `arrow_forward` glyph pairing the already-curated `arrow_back` — the eleventh curated icon, the identical real "additive... when a real need asks for more" growth `expand_more`/`remove` already established. Real, deliberate architectural choice, the same real dividing line `Segmented Button`/`Filter Chip`/`Navigation Rail`/`Tabs` already established: "which page is current" is app-owned state, returns `(previous, pages, next)`, `pages` a `Vec<Node>` (`Segmented Button`'s own exact real return shape). **Real Rust borrow-checker finding, fixed before it shipped broken:** a first draft shared one `tree`-capturing closure across `previous` and `next`'s own two call sites — real code (the `pages` loop) running *between* those two calls conflicted with the closure's own held mutable borrow (`E0499`); fixed by converting it into a plain, non-capturing `fn` taking `&mut Tree` explicitly, avoiding the whole closure-borrow-lifetime problem. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, unchanged — confirming the new `arrow_forward` icon's real path data parses correctly)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 436 passed/1 skipped (7 new in `test_pagination.py`, zero regressions), all 60 examples (including the new `examples/pagination.py`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/pagination.py`.)
- Step 5: Status Bar — ✅ (`Window.add_status_bar`. MD3 has no official Status Bar page (confirmed via the same real per-directory-listing technique this whole milestone already uses). **Real, deliberate reuse of `AppShell`'s own already-real `status_bar` region, not new shell-level wiring:** `build_shell` (§14 step 13) already accepts any pre-built `Node` for it (a thin bottom row in its own real flex-column layout), confirmed by direct re-read before writing any code — this step builds the real, styled bar *content*, and the returned `Node` works directly as `build_shell`'s own existing `status_bar` parameter, proven for real by `test_status_bar_can_be_passed_directly_into_build_shell`, not just asserted. Real, honest anatomy, not independently token-verified: 24dp height (a genuine desktop convention, shorter than every other bar in this catalog), `surface_container` fill — the same real subtle-chrome role `Menu`'s own panel already uses. Status text reuses Label Small's own already-declared constants (`BADGE_LABEL_FONT_SIZE`/`_WEIGHT`, the identical real MD3 type role `Badge`'s own labeled variant already found, Phase 3 Step 1), `on_surface_variant`. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, unchanged — a pure composition reusing an already-real shell region needed no new `engine-render` capability), `cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 440 passed/1 skipped (4 new in `test_status_bar.py`, zero regressions), all 61 examples (including the new `examples/status_bar.py`, which composes a real menu bar/toolbar/status bar together into one real `AppShell`) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/status_bar.py`.)
- Step 6: Main Menu submenus — ✅ (extends the existing context-menu overlay mechanism this codebase already has real since M4 Phase 7, not a new overlay kind. `add_menu_item` gained a real `submenu: bool = false` param, inserted between `icon` and `width` — confirmed backward-compatible by grepping every real caller (every one uses keyword args only) before adding it. When true, adds a real trailing `chevron_right` icon child, MD3's own real "this item opens a nested menu" convention — the twelfth curated icon, fetched verbatim from the same real CDN this catalog's icon module already cites. The real submenu itself needed no new engine primitive at all: it's just another real `build_menu`/`open_menu` pair, opened with the parent item's own returned `Node` as the anchor, the identical real `Tree::open_overlay` primitive every other overlay in this catalog already reuses. **A real, confirmed bug found live, not predicted in advance:** `examples/main_menu_submenus.py`'s own first real run genuinely failed — `AssertionError: a real click on a submenu item must reach its own registered handler`. Root-caused to `Tree::dismiss_overlays_outside`: `open_overlay` always positions content anchor-relative-*below*, so a submenu anchored to an item inside an already-open parent menu genuinely sits outside the parent menu's own bounds; the filter only ever checked a *candidate* overlay's own bounds against the press point, so a press genuinely inside the submenu was wrongly read as "outside" the *parent* menu, dismissing it and consuming the press via `dispatch`'s own early-return before the submenu item's own handler ever ran. Fixed with a new `inside_any_overlay` check added to the filter — a press inside *any* currently-open overlay can no longer dismiss a *different* one, since the user is still interacting with the overlay system as a whole; for every existing single-overlay caller (Menu, Tooltip, Search View, Popover) this is a true no-op, confirmed by the full pre-existing `engine-core` suite passing unmodified. Proven directly with a new, dedicated `engine-core` unit test (`a_press_inside_a_nested_submenu_does_not_dismiss_its_own_parent_menu`, engine-core 151 up from 150) building a genuine two-overlay nested scene rather than reusing the single-overlay `overlay_scene` helper, plus a new Python-level test (`test_the_parent_menu_survives_a_real_click_on_its_own_submenu_item`) proving the parent menu's own survival behaviorally through a real `window.click()`-driven reproduction, not `open_menu` called directly. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (43 binaries green, `engine-core` 151 up from 150)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 446 passed/1 skipped (6 in `test_main_menu_submenus.py`, up from the original 5 draft, zero regressions), all 62 examples (including the new `examples/main_menu_submenus.py`, which failed for real before the fix and now exits cleanly) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/main_menu_submenus.py`.)
- Step 7: `.pyi` stubs for every method/class Steps 1-6 add — ✅ (satisfied incrementally, not as a separate deferred pass — each of Steps 1-6 added its own real `.pyi` stub entry in the same step that added the method; `add_popover`/`add_link`/`add_spin_box`/`add_pagination`/`add_status_bar`/`add_menu_item` (with its new `submenu` param) are all present in `python/tre/_core.pyi`, each verified with `mypy --strict` against that step's own example. **Phase 8 is now fully complete, all 7 steps.**)

### Phase 9 — Content Specialty 🚧
- Step 1: Video — ✅ (`Window.add_video`/`Node.push_frame`. No official MD3 page (confirmed via the same real directory-listing technique this milestone already uses). **Real, deliberate reuse of `NodeKind::Image` directly, not a new `NodeKind`, grounded in real, directly-applicable precedent from the sibling `pyCopper` project's own `Video` widget** (same author, same explicit desktop-only design goal, confirmed via direct source read): `Video` is a *frame sink*, not a decoder — nothing in this codebase depends on a codec library (adding one, realistically PyAV/FFmpeg, would mean taking on its install size and licensing considerations for every TRE application, not just the ones that show video), so the application decodes however it likes and calls `push_frame(rgba, width, height)` at whatever cadence it decides. `add_video(width, height, fit, x, y)` mirrors `add_image`'s own identical real contract exactly — `width`/`height` are the node's own real, fixed box (unlike pyCopper's own intrinsic-sizing `Image`, which lays out `0x0` until a frame arrives); a pushed frame's own resolution is resolved against that fixed box by `content_fit` at paint time, so a real, live resolution renegotiation between frames needs zero layout involvement. Initialized with a single fully-transparent placeholder pixel so the node paints as genuinely empty until the app's first real `push_frame` call. `Node.push_frame` reuses `set_text`'s own established chokepoint pattern (`tree.get_mut` + `NodeKind` match, `EngineError::UnknownProperty` for any non-`Image` node) — validates `rgba.len() == width * height * 4` with a real, clear `PyValueError` (`add_icon`'s own "fail loudly on pure validation" convention), not routed through `EngineError` since no I/O is involved. **Real, confirmed architecture gap found and fixed alongside this step, not silently missed:** `engine-render`'s own `ImageTextureCache::sync` (M22 Phase 1) uploaded a real `Image` node's GPU texture exactly once, keyed only on node presence — correct when an `Image` node's pixel data never changed after creation (true before this phase), but would have silently kept painting a video's very first frame forever otherwise. Fixed by keying re-upload on real content identity instead of node presence: `peniko::Blob<u8>` carries a real, unique, monotonically-assigned `id()` (confirmed via direct source read of the vendored `linebender_resource_handle` crate, not assumed) — a cheap O(1) comparison, never a byte-level memcmp of the pixel buffer itself, which would be a real, meaningful per-frame cost at video resolutions. **Real, deliberate scope simplification, stated not silently dropped:** a changed frame always recreates the whole GPU texture rather than reusing the existing one via `write_texture` alone when the resolution is unchanged — correct for both same-size and resized frames uniformly, at the real cost of a full allocation per pushed frame; `Video`'s own scope (a frame sink for whatever cadence the app feeds it, not a real-time decode pipeline this codebase has no codec dependency for) doesn't need the incremental fast path yet. Proven directly at the `engine-render` level with a new, dedicated unit test (`sync_reuploads_a_texture_when_its_own_node_content_genuinely_changes`, `engine-core`/`engine-render` test count unaffected at the core level, `engine-render` 4 up from 3) building a real two-frame scene and asserting the cache's own tracked content id actually changes on re-sync. Verified: full workspace `cargo check`/`cargo test --release` (44 binaries green)/`cargo clippy -D warnings`/`cargo fmt --check` clean, `maturin develop --release` rebuilt, `pytest tests/` 457 passed/1 skipped (11 new in `test_video.py`, zero regressions), all 63 examples (including the new `examples/video.py`, a real synthetic decode loop pushing 5 distinct frames including a mid-stream resolution change) and the showcase demo re-run clean, `mypy --strict` clean against `examples/video.py`.)
- Step 2: Node Graph — ⬜ (real groundwork already exists, M5 Phase 4's own node-graph/chart validation work -- this phase brings it to a real, first-class authored component rather than a proof of composition.)
- Step 3: Code Editor — ⬜
- Step 4: Terminal — ⬜
- Step 5: Carousel — ⬜
- Step 6: `.pyi` stubs for every method/class Steps 1-5 add — ⬜

**Not scoped by this milestone:** PyPI publishing, per the user's own explicit direction -- the very last thing done, once the whole Tesserae UI Framework is complete. Real per-component engine-core/engine-render design (exact `NodeKind` payload shape, exact paint logic) for anything past Phase 0/1 -- each phase gets its own real investigation when it starts, the same discipline this file already applies throughout, not designed exhaustively up front for 30+ components at once.

**Just closed:** M30 Phase 8 Step 5 — Status Bar (§5, §7, §11.2). A real, deliberate reuse rather than new engine work: `AppShell`'s own `build_shell` already accepted a pre-built `status_bar` region since §14 step 13 — this step only needed to build the real, styled content, confirmed to slot in directly with no adapter needed.

**Just closed:** M30 Phase 8 Steps 6-7 — Main Menu submenus, **closing Phase 8 entirely (all 7 steps)**. `add_menu_item` gained a real `submenu: bool` param adding a trailing `chevron_right` affordance (the twelfth curated icon); the submenu itself needed no new engine primitive, just another `build_menu`/`open_menu` pair anchored to the parent item's own returned `Node`. **A real, confirmed bug found live, not predicted in advance:** the new example's own first real run genuinely failed a real click on a submenu item never reached its own handler. Root-caused to `Tree::dismiss_overlays_outside`: since `open_overlay` always positions content anchor-relative-below, a submenu anchored to an item inside an already-open parent menu genuinely sits outside that parent menu's own bounds, so a press inside the submenu was wrongly read as "outside" the parent menu, dismissing it and swallowing the press before the submenu's own handler ran. Fixed with a new `inside_any_overlay` check: a press inside *any* open overlay can no longer dismiss a *different* one — a true no-op for every existing single-overlay caller, confirmed by the full pre-existing suite passing unmodified. Proven directly with a new, dedicated `engine-core` unit test (a genuine two-overlay nested scene, `engine-core` 151 up from 150) and a new Python-level test proving the parent menu's own survival behaviorally through a real `window.click()`-driven reproduction. **Up next:** M30 Phase 9 — Content Specialty (Video, Node Graph, Code Editor, Terminal, Carousel), the milestone's final phase.

**Just closed:** M30 Phase 9 Step 1 — Video (§5). `Window.add_video`/`Node.push_frame`, a real "frame sink" design directly grounded in the sibling `pyCopper` project's own `Video` widget — reuses `NodeKind::Image` verbatim, no new `NodeKind` needed. Found and fixed a real, confirmed architecture gap along the way: `engine-render`'s own GPU texture cache only ever uploaded an `Image` node's pixels once, correct before this phase (pixel data never changed) but silently wrong for a live video stream — fixed by keying re-upload on `peniko::Blob`'s own real content-identity id (an O(1) comparison, not a per-frame pixel memcmp), proven with a new, dedicated `engine-render` unit test. **Up next:** M30 Phase 9 Step 2 — Node Graph, building on M5 Phase 4's own existing groundwork.
